# OBG — Bookmark-Derived Pearls

*Distilled from bookmarked INI-CET / NEET-PG MCQs. Only high-yield, revisable learning points were kept; points already covered by the existing pearl collection were dropped.*

---

## 1. The Partograph

**PEARL:** Plotting begins in **ACTIVE LABOUR, and the first plot is placed ON or BEFORE the ALERT LINE.** The **ALERT LINE** runs at **1 cm/hour** of cervical dilatation from the start of the active phase; the **ACTION LINE lies 4 HOURS to the RIGHT of it**. Crossing the alert line means **transfer/reassess**; crossing the ACTION line means **INTERVENE**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The partograph is a **graphical early-warning system**, not a diagnostic test. The 4-hour gap between the lines exists because **most labours that lag briefly will still progress spontaneously** — so the alert line buys time for referral in low-resource settings while the action line marks the point beyond which delay itself becomes dangerous (obstructed labour, rupture, fistula, sepsis).

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The MODIFIED WHO partograph OMITS THE LATENT PHASE altogether** — plotting starts only in active labour, because latent-phase duration is too variable to be a useful warning signal.
- **Active phase was traditionally defined from 3–4 cm; current WHO guidance places it at 5 cm, and the "1 cm/hour" rule is now recognised as too rigid** — the modern WHO Labour Care Guide uses wider, dilatation-specific limits.
- **The partograph records THREE groups of observations: FETAL (heart rate, membranes/liquor colour, moulding), PROGRESS OF LABOUR (cervical dilatation, descent of the head in fifths palpable, contractions per 10 minutes and their duration), and MATERNAL (pulse, blood pressure, temperature, urine output, drugs and fluids).**
- **Descent is recorded in FIFTHS PALPABLE ABDOMINALLY, not by vaginal station, because abdominal assessment is unaffected by caput and moulding.**
- **MOULDING grading: 0 — bones separated; 1+ — bones touching; 2+ — overlapping but REDUCIBLE; 3+ — overlapping and NOT reducible (severe, suggesting cephalopelvic disproportion).**
- **Liquor: I = intact, C = clear, M = meconium, B = blood, A = absent.**
- **Its proven benefit is in reducing prolonged labour, augmentation and emergency caesarean in low-resource settings** — it is a tool of vigilance rather than of prediction.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Where do you start plotting" → **before/at the alert line**.

**REMEMBER:** *Alert line means think and refer; action line four hours later means act.*

![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/d570ef2f11e74e289248156d276cabbax1280x1092.JPEG)

## 2. The Levator Ani and Pelvic Organ Prolapse

**PEARL:** The **PUBOCOCCYGEUS is the deepest and most important component of the levator ani** — injury to it causes **CYSTOCELE, ENTEROCELE and URETHRAL DESCENT**. The levator ani has three parts: **PUBOCOCCYGEUS (anterior — with pubovaginalis, puboperinealis and puboanalis), PUBORECTALIS (intermediate) and ILIOCOCCYGEUS (posterior).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The levator ani is **tonically contracted at rest**, holding the urogenital hiatus closed and the pelvic organs on a horizontal shelf so that **intra-abdominal pressure presses them AGAINST the muscle rather than THROUGH it**. Once childbirth stretches or avulses the pubococcygeus from the pubic bone, the hiatus widens and **every rise in intra-abdominal pressure now drives the organs downwards** — which is why prolapse is progressive and why it presents years after the delivery that caused it.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Degrees of UTERINE prolapse: FIRST — external os stays above the introitus. SECOND — os descends to/through the introitus but the uterine body remains inside. THIRD (complete procidentia in its full form) — cervix, body and fundus lie OUTSIDE the introitus. PROCIDENTIA — the whole uterus with complete vaginal eversion.** The modern system is **POP-Q**, staged 0–IV against the hymen.
- **Management: pelvic floor muscle training and PESSARY for mild disease or when surgery is unsuitable (elderly, unfit, wishing further children). For THIRD-DEGREE prolapse in a postmenopausal woman, VAGINAL HYSTERECTOMY WITH PELVIC FLOOR REPAIR (with McCall culdoplasty/vault suspension) is the standard.**
- **FOTHERGILL'S (MANCHESTER) OPERATION — amputation of the cervix with plication of the Mackenrodt (cardinal) ligaments and anterior colporrhaphy — PRESERVES the uterus and is chosen in a young woman with prolapse and cervical elongation who wants to retain her uterus; it causes cervical stenosis, infertility and mid-trimester loss. SLING operations are for VAULT prolapse (sacrocolpopexy) or for stress incontinence.** The **SHIRODKAR procedure is a cervical CERCLAGE — nothing to do with prolapse.**
- **The main supports of the uterus are the MACKENRODT'S (CARDINAL/transverse cervical) LIGAMENTS and the UTEROSACRAL ligaments — the round and broad ligaments provide little support.**
- **Risk factors: vaginal delivery (especially instrumental and prolonged second stage), multiparity, ageing and menopause (oestrogen loss), obesity, chronic cough and constipation, and connective tissue disorders.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Deepest part causing cystocele and enterocele" → **pubococcygeus**.

**REMEMBER:** *The levator ani is a shelf, not a sling — tear the pubococcygeus and everything slides through the gap.*

## 3. Proximal Tubal Block on HSG

**PEARL:** A **BILATERAL CORNUAL (proximal) BLOCK on hysterosalpingography must be CONFIRMED — it is frequently a FALSE POSITIVE due to CORNUAL SPASM.** The next step is **LAPAROSCOPY WITH CHROMOPERTUBATION combined with HYSTEROSCOPY**, which is both diagnostic and therapeutic.

**UNDERSTAND:** The uterotubal junction is a **thick, muscular sphincter-like segment that contracts in response to the contrast injection and to anxiety** — so dye fails to pass despite an anatomically patent tube. **Up to a third of "proximal blocks" on HSG open up on laparoscopy.** Committing a woman to IVF or tubal surgery on an unconfirmed HSG finding is therefore the classic error the question tests.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **HSG is done in the PROLIFERATIVE PHASE (day 6–11), after menses have stopped and before ovulation, to avoid disturbing a pregnancy and to avoid a thick endometrium mimicking filling defects.** **Contraindications: pregnancy, active pelvic infection, and active bleeding.**
- **HSG also has a THERAPEUTIC effect — pregnancy rates rise after the procedure (especially with oil-soluble contrast, the H2Oil trial), by flushing debris and by an effect on the endometrium.**
- **Laparoscopy with chromopertubation (METHYLENE BLUE) is the GOLD STANDARD for tubal patency** and additionally reveals **endometriosis, adhesions and peritubal disease that HSG cannot see.**
- **HYSTEROSCOPY is the gold standard for the UTERINE CAVITY — used diagnostically for cavity assessment and therapeutically for SEPTUM resection, ASHERMAN'S adhesiolysis, polypectomy, submucous fibroid resection, retained IUCD and abnormal uterine bleeding.**
- **Basic infertility workup: SEMEN ANALYSIS FIRST (simplest and non-invasive — male factor accounts for ~30–40%), documentation of OVULATION (mid-luteal day-21 progesterone), and TUBAL PATENCY.**
- **Distal (fimbrial) block with a HYDROSALPINX is different — it responds poorly to surgery, and a hydrosalpinx should be REMOVED OR CLIPPED BEFORE IVF because it halves implantation rates.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Bilateral cornual block on HSG → **laparoscopy/hysteroscopy**, not IVF.

**REMEMBER:** *A cornual block on HSG is a spasm until laparoscopy says otherwise.*

## 4. Evaluating Amenorrhoea by Gonadotrophins

**PEARL:** **HIGH FSH and LH = HYPERGONADOTROPHIC HYPOGONADISM = OVARIAN FAILURE** — the next step is **SERUM ESTRADIOL** (and karyotype). Elevated gonadotrophins also **EXCLUDE PREGNANCY**, because pregnancy suppresses FSH and LH. **LOW FSH/LH = HYPOGONADOTROPHIC hypogonadism (hypothalamic-pituitary). NORMAL FSH/LH = EUGONADOTROPHIC (outflow tract or PCOS).**

**UNDERSTAND:** FSH and LH are the **read-out of the negative feedback loop**. If the ovary fails, oestradiol falls, feedback is lost and gonadotrophins rise — so **high FSH localises the lesion to the GONAD**. If the hypothalamus or pituitary fails, there is nothing to drive the ovary, so **both gonadotrophins and oestradiol are low**. One hormone pair therefore localises the level of the defect before any imaging.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **PRIMARY AMENORRHOEA — no menses by 15 years with secondary sexual characteristics, or by 13 years WITHOUT them. SECONDARY — cessation for 3 months (regular cycles) or 6 months (irregular).**
- **The classification by breast and uterus is the fastest bedside tool: BREASTS PRESENT + UTERUS ABSENT → ANDROGEN INSENSITIVITY (46,XY, testes present, testosterone in the male range) or MÜLLERIAN AGENESIS (MAYER–ROKITANSKY–KÜSTER–HAUSER, 46,XX, normal testosterone, associated RENAL anomalies). BREASTS ABSENT + UTERUS PRESENT → gonadal failure (high FSH — TURNER or pure gonadal dysgenesis) or hypothalamic failure (low FSH — KALLMANN with ANOSMIA, constitutional delay). BOTH PRESENT → outflow obstruction (imperforate hymen, transverse septum) or PCOS.**
- **PURE GONADAL DYSGENESIS (SWYER when 46,XY): NORMAL stature, streak gonads, absent thelarche, HIGH FSH/LH, and a NORMAL karyotype (46,XX or 46,XY) — the absence of Turner's short stature and webbed neck is the discriminator.** **A Y CHROMOSOME MANDATES GONADECTOMY** because of the risk of gonadoblastoma/dysgerminoma.
- **TURNER SYNDROME (45,X): SHORT stature, webbed neck, shield chest, cubitus valgus, streak ovaries, COARCTATION OF THE AORTA and bicuspid valve, horseshoe kidney — the uterus is present but infantile.**
- **PREMATURE OVARIAN INSUFFICIENCY (<40 years) — causes include FMR1 PREMUTATION (fragile X), autoimmune (check thyroid and adrenal antibodies), chemotherapy/radiotherapy, galactosaemia, and Turner mosaicism. Treat with HORMONE REPLACEMENT until the average age of menopause** to protect bone and cardiovascular health.
- **The PROGESTERONE CHALLENGE TEST assesses oestrogenisation: withdrawal bleeding means adequate oestrogen and a patent outflow tract (anovulation, e.g. PCOS); no bleed means hypo-oestrogenism or an outflow problem.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Amenorrhoea with high FSH/LH → **measure oestradiol** (not a pregnancy test, which is already excluded).

**REMEMBER:** *High gonadotrophins point at the ovary; low ones point at the brain.*

## 5. Abnormal Cervical Cytology

**PEARL:** **ANY significantly abnormal Pap smear — including CARCINOMA IN SITU (CIN III) — is followed by COLPOSCOPY WITH DIRECTED BIOPSY**, not by immediate conisation or hysterectomy. **Cytology SCREENS; histology DIAGNOSES; treatment follows the histological diagnosis.** In **POST-COITAL BLEEDING, examine with a speculum first — if a growth is visible, take a PUNCH BIOPSY directly** (do not rely on a Pap smear, which is falsely negative in frank cancer).

**UNDERSTAND:** The sequence exists because **cytology cannot distinguish intraepithelial disease from invasion** — and that single distinction changes management completely (local excision versus radical treatment). Colposcopy identifies the **transformation zone and the worst-looking area** to biopsy, using **ACETIC ACID (aceto-white change from increased nuclear protein) and LUGOL'S IODINE (SCHILLER'S TEST — normal glycogen-rich squamous epithelium stains brown; abnormal epithelium does NOT take up iodine).**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Screening: begin at 21 years. 21–29 — cytology every 3 YEARS. 30–65 — CO-TESTING (cytology + HPV) every 5 YEARS, or primary HPV testing every 5 years. STOP at 65 with adequate prior negative screening.** In India, **VIA (visual inspection with acetic acid)** is the programme tool because it is cheap and gives immediate results, allowing screen-and-treat.
- **COLPOSCOPIC FEATURES OF INVASION: ATYPICAL VESSELS, a coarse mosaic and punctation, and a raised irregular surface. If the lesion extends into the canal or the transformation zone is not fully visible (an "UNSATISFACTORY colposcopy"), a CONE BIOPSY/LEEP is required.**
- **Treatment of CIN: ABLATION (cryotherapy, thermal ablation) for satisfactory colposcopy with a fully visible small lesion; EXCISION (LEEP/LLETZ or cold-knife cone) when invasion must be excluded, for CIN II–III, for glandular lesions and for unsatisfactory colposcopy — excision has the advantage of providing histology.** **Cone biopsy increases the risk of PRETERM BIRTH and cervical stenosis.**
- **A pregnant woman with an abnormal smear may have colposcopy, but ENDOCERVICAL CURETTAGE IS CONTRAINDICATED and treatment is DEFERRED until after delivery unless invasion is suspected.**
- **CERVICAL CANCER STAGING IS CLINICAL (FIGO) — based on examination, with imaging now permitted; the commonest histology is SQUAMOUS CELL CARCINOMA, and HPV 16 and 18 cause ~70%.**
- **Treatment of early cervical cancer is surgery (radical hysterectomy = WERTHEIM'S) or chemoradiation; from stage IIB onwards, CONCURRENT CHEMORADIATION with cisplatin is standard.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Abnormal Pap (any grade) → **colposcopy and directed biopsy**.

**REMEMBER:** *Never treat on cytology alone — colposcope, biopsy, then act.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/c8d766a6c9db4794abe17633684b9833x1280x1033.JPEG)

## 6. Position for Caesarean Section — Aortocaval Compression

**PEARL:** The woman is placed **SUPINE WITH A WEDGE UNDER THE RIGHT HIP, producing a 15° LEFT LATERAL TILT** — this displaces the uterus off the inferior vena cava and aorta, preserving venous return and preventing **SUPINE HYPOTENSION SYNDROME.**

**UNDERSTAND:** From about 20 weeks, the **gravid uterus compresses the IVC when the woman lies flat**, cutting venous return by up to 30% and dropping cardiac output — producing hypotension, nausea, pallor and fetal compromise. The **uterus is normally DEXTRO-ROTATED**, so tilting to the LEFT rolls it off the great vessels. **Manual left uterine displacement is at least as effective as a wedge** and is what is used during resuscitation.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **In MATERNAL CARDIAC ARREST after 20 weeks: continuous MANUAL LEFT UTERINE DISPLACEMENT (not tilt, which compromises chest compressions), and PERIMORTEM CAESAREAN DELIVERY started within 4 MINUTES and delivered by 5 MINUTES — performed to save the MOTHER by relieving aortocaval compression, whatever the fetal outcome.**
- **Spinal anaesthesia is the usual choice for caesarean section; its hypotension is managed with left lateral tilt, a fluid co-load and PHENYLEPHRINE (the vasopressor of choice — better fetal acid–base status than ephedrine).**
- **Other standard caesarean measures: PROPHYLACTIC ANTIBIOTICS BEFORE SKIN INCISION (not after cord clamping), a urinary catheter, and thromboprophylaxis.**
- **Thromboprophylaxis: PNEUMATIC COMPRESSION DEVICES for every woman NOT on pharmacological prophylaxis undergoing caesarean, applied before surgery and DISCONTINUED ONCE SHE IS AMBULANT. Women with additional risk factors (thrombophilia, obesity, heart failure, prolonged immobility, previous VTE) get BOTH mechanical devices AND LMWH/unfractionated heparin.**
- **Pregnancy is a hypercoagulable state (rising factors VII, VIII, X and fibrinogen, falling protein S, venous stasis) — VTE remains a leading direct cause of maternal death.**
- **DOYEN'S RETRACTOR is the instrument used to retract the abdominal wall and bladder during caesarean section; EPISIOTOMY SCISSORS are angulated to keep the incision away from the anal sphincter.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Position on the OT table" → **supine with a wedge under the RIGHT hip**.

**REMEMBER:** *Wedge the right hip to tilt her left — the uterus must come off the vena cava.*

## 7. Chorionicity in Twin Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **Chorionicity must be determined in the FIRST TRIMESTER — ideally 11+0 to 13+6 WEEKS (CRL 45–84 mm)**, when the membranes have not yet fused. **DICHORIONIC → the LAMBDA (twin-peak) SIGN and a thick (>2 mm) dividing membrane with FOUR layers. MONOCHORIONIC DIAMNIOTIC → the "T" SIGN with a thin two-layer membrane. MONOCHORIONIC MONOAMNIOTIC → ONE placenta, TWO cords and NO dividing membrane at all.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Chorionicity — not zygosity — **determines risk and surveillance**, because a **shared placenta means shared circulation**. It becomes progressively harder to determine as pregnancy advances because **the membranes thin and fuse**, which is why the first-trimester scan is irreplaceable. The **timing of the split determines the type: <4 days → dichorionic diamniotic; 4–8 days → monochorionic diamniotic; 8–13 days → monochorionic monoamniotic; >13 days → CONJOINED twins.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Monochorionic pregnancies need scans EVERY 2 WEEKS from 16 weeks (dichorionic every 4 weeks) to detect TWIN–TWIN TRANSFUSION SYNDROME — a donor twin who is anaemic, growth-restricted and OLIGOHYDRAMNIOTIC ("STUCK TWIN") and a recipient who is plethoric, POLYHYDRAMNIOTIC and in high-output cardiac failure. Staged by QUINTERO; treated with FETOSCOPIC LASER ABLATION of the placental anastomoses.**
- **Monoamniotic twins carry a very high risk of CORD ENTANGLEMENT and are delivered by CAESAREAN at 32–34 weeks with inpatient monitoring.**
- **Other monochorionic complications: TWIN ANAEMIA–POLYCYTHAEMIA SEQUENCE, selective fetal growth restriction, TWIN REVERSED ARTERIAL PERFUSION (acardiac twin), and — after single fetal death — acute exsanguination of the survivor into the dead twin, causing neurological injury.**
- **Timing of delivery: dichorionic ~37–38 weeks, monochorionic diamniotic ~36 weeks, monoamniotic 32–34 weeks.**
- **DIFFERENT SEXES or TWO SEPARATE PLACENTAS prove DICHORIONICITY (and different sexes prove dizygosity); the same sex proves nothing on its own.**
- **All twins carry higher risks of preterm labour, pre-eclampsia, anaemia, growth restriction, malpresentation and POSTPARTUM HAEMORRHAGE.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Gross specimen with two cords, one placenta and NO dividing membrane → **monochorionic monoamniotic**; "best time to determine type" → **11–14 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *Lambda is thick and safe; T is thin and shares a placenta; no membrane at all is the most dangerous of all.*

## 8. Gestational Trophoblastic Neoplasia

**PEARL:** Indicators of malignant transformation after a molar pregnancy are a **PLATEAU of hCG across 4 measurements over ≥3 weeks, a RISE of ≥10% across 3 values over 2 weeks, hCG persisting beyond 6 MONTHS, a PERSISTENTLY ENLARGED uterus, and SUBURETHRAL/vaginal and myometrial NODULES with hypervascularity on ultrasound.** **The mere PRESENCE of theca lutein cysts is NOT an indicator — it is their INCREASING SIZE or PERSISTENCE that matters.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Theca lutein cysts are simply a **physiological ovarian response to very high hCG** — they occur in normal molar pregnancy and regress as hCG falls. Their persistence is meaningful only because it **mirrors persistently high hCG**. This is why hCG surveillance, not imaging, is the backbone of follow-up: **hCG is an almost perfect tumour marker, produced by every trophoblastic cell.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Risk factors for malignant change: COMPLETE mole (15–20% risk versus 1–5% for PARTIAL), age <20 or ≥40, hCG >100,000 IU/mL, uterine size greater than dates (>20 weeks), THECA LUTEIN CYSTS >6 cm, and previous molar pregnancy.**
- **COMPLETE MOLE: 46,XX (or XY) entirely PATERNAL (an empty ovum fertilised by one sperm that duplicates, or two sperm), NO fetal parts, diffuse trophoblastic proliferation, "SNOWSTORM"/bunch-of-grapes appearance, markedly raised hCG, and the classic associations — hyperemesis, EARLY PRE-ECLAMPSIA (before 20 weeks), HYPERTHYROIDISM (hCG cross-reacts with the TSH receptor) and theca lutein cysts. PARTIAL MOLE: TRIPLOID 69,XXY, fetal parts present, focal proliferation, lower hCG, and much lower malignant potential.**
- **Treatment is SUCTION EVACUATION (never sharp curettage or medical evacuation as first choice), with anti-D if Rh-negative, followed by SERIAL hCG monitoring — weekly until three normal values, then monthly.** **CONTRACEPTION IS MANDATORY throughout follow-up (combined pills are acceptable once hCG normalises) because a new pregnancy makes hCG uninterpretable.**
- **CHORIOCARCINOMA: a rapidly growing, HIGHLY VASCULAR tumour of sheets of syncytio- and cytotrophoblast with NO CHORIONIC VILLI; it metastasises early and haematogenously to the LUNG (commonest — "CANNONBALL" lesions), vagina, brain and liver. It follows a molar pregnancy in ~50%, a normal pregnancy in 25%, and an abortion/ectopic in the rest.**
- **It is EXQUISITELY CHEMOSENSITIVE and CURABLE even when metastatic: LOW-RISK (WHO score ≤6) disease is treated with SINGLE-AGENT METHOTREXATE (with folinic acid rescue) or actinomycin-D; HIGH-RISK disease with EMA-CO.** **Hysterectomy is not required.**
- **PLACENTAL SITE TROPHOBLASTIC TUMOUR is the exception — it secretes LITTLE hCG (raised human placental lactogen instead) and is CHEMORESISTANT, so it needs HYSTERECTOMY.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "All are indicators EXCEPT" → **an increased NUMBER of theca lutein cysts**.

**REMEMBER:** *Follow the hCG curve — a plateau or a rise is cancer until proved otherwise.*

`Q9116`

## 9. Diabetes in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **OVERT (pregestational) diabetes is diagnosed by FASTING GLUCOSE ≥126 mg/dL, RANDOM ≥200 mg/dL, or HbA1c ≥6.5% — and it, NOT gestational diabetes, causes CONGENITAL MALFORMATIONS.** GDM appears in the **second trimester, after organogenesis is complete**, so it causes **macrosomia, polyhydramnios, neonatal hypoglycaemia and stillbirth — but NOT malformations.** **Delivery timing in GDM: diet-controlled 39–40+6 weeks; insulin/oral-agent-controlled 39–39+6 weeks; poor control 37–38+6 weeks.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The distinction is entirely about **TIMING relative to organogenesis (weeks 3–8)**. Hyperglycaemia during that window is teratogenic; hyperglycaemia after it simply **drives fetal hyperinsulinaemia**, and insulin is the fetus's main growth hormone — producing **macrosomia, organomegaly and, at birth, sudden hypoglycaemia when the maternal glucose supply is cut but fetal insulin remains high.** Everything about GDM follows from that single mechanism.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **CAUDAL REGRESSION (sacral agenesis) is the most SPECIFIC anomaly of pregestational diabetes; the COMMONEST are CARDIAC (transposition, VSD, ASD, hypoplastic left heart) and NEURAL TUBE DEFECTS. Small left colon syndrome is another.** **Preconception HbA1c correlates directly with malformation risk — which is why PRECONCEPTION counselling and high-dose (5 mg) FOLIC ACID matter so much.**
- **Neonatal problems: HYPOGLYCAEMIA (feed early, monitor), hypocalcaemia, hypomagnesaemia, POLYCYTHAEMIA and hyperbilirubinaemia, RESPIRATORY DISTRESS SYNDROME (insulin ANTAGONISES cortisol-driven surfactant production, so lung maturity is DELAYED), and birth injury from SHOULDER DYSTOCIA.**
- **DIAGNOSIS: India (DIPSI) uses a NON-FASTING 75 g oral glucose load with a 2-hour value ≥140 mg/dL. The IADPSG/WHO 75 g criteria are fasting ≥92, 1 h ≥180, 2 h ≥153 mg/dL — ONE abnormal value suffices.**
- **Management: diet and exercise first; INSULIN is the drug of choice if targets are not met (fasting <95, 1 h <140, 2 h <120 mg/dL); metformin and glyburide are alternatives that cross the placenta.**
- **Consider CAESAREAN for estimated fetal weight ≥4.5 kg in a diabetic (≥5 kg in a non-diabetic) because of shoulder dystocia risk.**
- **GDM confers a ~50% lifetime risk of type 2 diabetes — arrange a 75 g OGTT AT 6–12 WEEKS POSTPARTUM and lifelong screening.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Seen in GDM EXCEPT" → **malformations**; "overt diabetes cut-off" → **fasting ≥126**.

**REMEMBER:** *GDM starts too late to malform — it only makes the baby big and then hypoglycaemic.*

`Q9118, Q9124, Q9139`

---

## 10. Anticoagulation in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **WARFARIN IS TERATOGENIC IN THE FIRST TRIMESTER (weeks 6–12) — causing WARFARIN EMBRYOPATHY: nasal hypoplasia, STIPPLED EPIPHYSES (chondrodysplasia punctata), limb hypoplasia, plus later CNS abnormalities and fetal haemorrhage.** With a **history of warfarin embryopathy, or a dose >5 mg/day, REPLACE warfarin with HEPARIN/LMWH in the first trimester.** If the dose is **≤5 mg/day, warfarin may be continued.** **Switch to LMWH at ~36 weeks, then to UNFRACTIONATED heparin near delivery, and stop on the day of delivery.**

**UNDERSTAND:** **Warfarin CROSSES the placenta; HEPARIN (both unfractionated and low-molecular-weight) DOES NOT** — it is a large, polar molecule. That single pharmacological fact governs the whole protocol. Warfarin is nonetheless preferred in mid-pregnancy for **MECHANICAL HEART VALVES**, because heparin is less effective at preventing valve thrombosis — a genuine trade-off between maternal and fetal risk that the woman must be counselled about.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **UNFRACTIONATED heparin is used near term because its SHORTER HALF-LIFE and reversibility with PROTAMINE reduce the risk of bleeding and allow regional anaesthesia; LMWH must be stopped 24 hours before neuraxial block (12 hours for prophylactic doses) to avoid SPINAL HAEMATOMA.**
- **Restart anticoagulation 6 hours after vaginal delivery and 6–12 hours after caesarean; overlap heparin with warfarin until INR is therapeutic. WARFARIN AND HEPARIN ARE BOTH SAFE IN BREASTFEEDING.**
- **Heparin's own risks in pregnancy: OSTEOPOROSIS and HEPARIN-INDUCED THROMBOCYTOPENIA (both far lower with LMWH).**
- **ANTIPHOSPHOLIPID SYNDROME in pregnancy is treated with LOW-DOSE ASPIRIN PLUS HEPARIN — aspirin alone is inadequate, and STEROIDS ADD NO BENEFIT and increase harm.** **Obstetric criteria: ≥3 unexplained consecutive losses before 10 weeks; ≥1 unexplained fetal death at or after 10 weeks; or ≥1 preterm birth before 34 weeks due to eclampsia, severe pre-eclampsia or placental insufficiency — PLUS a persistently positive antibody (lupus anticoagulant, anticardiolipin or anti-β2-glycoprotein I) on TWO occasions 12 WEEKS APART.**
- **APS also causes arterial and venous thrombosis, thrombocytopenia and livedo reticularis, with a PARADOXICALLY PROLONGED aPTT that fails to correct on mixing.**
- **DOACs are CONTRAINDICATED in pregnancy and breastfeeding.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Warfarin embryopathy in the first trimester → **replace warfarin with heparin**.

**REMEMBER:** *Heparin cannot cross the placenta; warfarin can — so the first trimester belongs to heparin.*

`Q9119, Q9141`

---

## 11. Anti-D Prophylaxis

**PEARL:** **Anti-D is given ONLY to Rh-NEGATIVE, NON-SENSITISED (INDIRECT COOMBS-NEGATIVE) women. It is USELESS — and therefore not given — once ALLOIMMUNISATION HAS ALREADY OCCURRED**, as in a pregnancy requiring **INTRAUTERINE TRANSFUSION**. Indications include **abortion (spontaneous or induced, at any gestation), ectopic pregnancy, molar pregnancy, fetal death, amniocentesis/CVS/cordocentesis, external cephalic version, antepartum haemorrhage, abdominal trauma, MANUAL REMOVAL OF PLACENTA, and delivery of an Rh-positive baby.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Anti-D works by **PREVENTION, not treatment** — the injected antibody coats fetal red cells and clears them before the maternal immune system can mount its own primary response. Once the mother has **memory B cells** (i.e. she is already sensitised, which is what an intrauterine transfusion implies), **no amount of passive antibody can undo that** — and the immune response is now anamnestic and self-sustaining.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Routine antenatal prophylaxis: 300 µg (1500 IU) at 28 WEEKS (± 34 weeks), and 300 µg WITHIN 72 HOURS OF DELIVERY of an Rh-positive infant. If missed, it is still worth giving up to 10–13 days later.**
- **Dose by gestation: 50 µg (250 IU) suffices before 12 weeks (small fetal blood volume); 300 µg thereafter. One 300 µg dose covers ~30 mL of fetal WHOLE blood (15 mL of red cells).**
- **The KLEIHAUER–BETKE TEST (or flow cytometry) quantifies FETOMATERNAL HAEMORRHAGE and determines whether ADDITIONAL doses are needed — essential after abruption, trauma or manual removal of placenta.** It exploits the **resistance of fetal haemoglobin to acid elution**.
- **Monitoring the ALREADY-sensitised pregnancy: maternal antibody TITRE (critical titre 1:16 or 1:32), then MIDDLE CEREBRAL ARTERY PEAK SYSTOLIC VELOCITY — a value >1.5 MoM predicts fetal anaemia and is the trigger for cordocentesis and INTRAUTERINE TRANSFUSION.** MCA Doppler has replaced serial amniocentesis with the Liley/Queenan curves.
- **Rh disease WORSENS WITH EACH SUCCESSIVE PREGNANCY**, and the fetal consequence is haemolysis → anaemia → high-output failure → HYDROPS FETALIS, with neonatal jaundice and KERNICTERUS.
- **ABO incompatibility (mother O, baby A or B) partially PROTECTS against Rh sensitisation** — maternal anti-A/anti-B clears fetal cells before Rh sensitisation occurs.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Anti-D NOT given in" → **intrauterine transfusion** (already alloimmunised).

**REMEMBER:** *Anti-D prevents sensitisation; it cannot reverse it.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/4413ce522e1a41d2b88fec6e0c6b0cf9x1280x3281.JPEG)

`Q9120`

---

## 12. Fetal Congenital Heart Block and Anti-Ro

**PEARL:** **CONGENITAL COMPLETE HEART BLOCK in a fetus should prompt evaluation of the mother for SLE and Sjögren's — specifically for ANTI-Ro/SSA and ANTI-La/SSB ANTIBODIES**, which cross the placenta and damage the conducting tissue between the AV node and the bundle of His. The block is **PERMANENT and usually requires a PACEMAKER.**

**UNDERSTAND:** These are **IgG antibodies, and only IgG crosses the placenta (actively, from 16 weeks, peaking in the third trimester)** — which is why fetal cardiac monitoring is performed **between 18 and 26 weeks**, the window of maximal antibody transfer and vulnerability. The antibodies **bind fetal cardiac antigens exposed during apoptosis and provoke inflammation then FIBROSIS** — and fibrosis, once established, cannot be reversed, which is exactly why the block is permanent while the rash of neonatal lupus resolves.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **NEONATAL LUPUS: an annular/photosensitive rash (which RESOLVES as maternal antibody is cleared over ~6 months), cytopenias, transaminitis — and the ONE PERMANENT feature, CONGENITAL HEART BLOCK. Mortality is ~20% in affected fetuses.**
- **MATERNAL HYDROXYCHLOROQUINE reduces the risk of heart block, especially in a woman who has had a previously affected child** (recurrence risk otherwise ~15–20%) — a key preventive fact.
- **The mother may be entirely ASYMPTOMATIC** — anti-Ro positivity, not clinical lupus, is what matters.
- **SLE in pregnancy: pregnancy is best undertaken after 6 MONTHS OF REMISSION; flares are commonest postpartum. Continue HYDROXYCHLOROQUINE throughout. AZATHIOPRINE, tacrolimus and steroids are permitted; METHOTREXATE, MYCOPHENOLATE and CYCLOPHOSPHAMIDE ARE CONTRAINDICATED.** **Add LOW-DOSE ASPIRIN for pre-eclampsia prophylaxis.**
- **Distinguishing a LUPUS NEPHRITIS FLARE from PRE-ECLAMPSIA is a classic dilemma: flare favours ACTIVE URINARY SEDIMENT (red cell casts), RISING anti-dsDNA and FALLING COMPLEMENT and involvement of other organs; pre-eclampsia favours rising uric acid, raised transaminases, a normal sediment and NORMAL complement.**
- **Coexisting APS greatly increases the risk of fetal loss, growth restriction and pre-eclampsia.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Fetal heart block → **evaluate the mother for SLE** (anti-Ro/SSA).

**REMEMBER:** *Maternal anti-Ro crosses the placenta and scars the conducting system — the rash fades, the block does not.*

`Q9122`

---

## 13. Severe Pre-eclampsia

**PEARL:** A woman with **headache, visual disturbance, EPIGASTRIC/right upper quadrant PAIN and heavy proteinuria has SEVERE PRE-ECLAMPSIA with IMPENDING ECLAMPSIA — the immediate next step is IV MAGNESIUM SULPHATE for seizure prophylaxis**, before steroids and before any decision about delivery.

**UNDERSTAND:** Magnesium is given first because **eclampsia can occur within minutes and is the event that kills**, whereas steroids act over 24–48 hours and delivery can be organised in parallel. Magnesium acts as a **cerebral vasodilator relieving vasospasm, an NMDA receptor antagonist and a membrane stabiliser** — it is an ANTICONVULSANT here, not an antihypertensive, and is **superior to phenytoin and diazepam (the MAGPIE and Collaborative Eclampsia trials).**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Features defining SEVERE disease: BP ≥160/110, PLATELETS <100,000, serum CREATININE >1.1 mg/dL (or doubled), transaminases twice normal, PULMONARY OEDEMA, and new cerebral or visual symptoms.** **PROTEINURIA IS NO LONGER REQUIRED for the diagnosis of pre-eclampsia** if there is hypertension with any of these features — and the AMOUNT of proteinuria is NOT a severity criterion.
- **Magnesium regimens: PRITCHARD (IM — 4 g IV + 10 g IM loading, then 5 g IM 4-hourly) and ZUSPAN (IV — 4 g loading, then 1 g/hour). Monitor DEEP TENDON REFLEXES (lost first, at ~10 mEq/L), RESPIRATORY RATE ≥12–16/min and URINE OUTPUT ≥30 mL/hour (magnesium is renally excreted, so oliguria causes toxicity). The ANTIDOTE IS 10 mL of 10% CALCIUM GLUCONATE IV.** Continue for **24 hours after delivery or the last seizure.**
- **Antihypertensives are given for BP ≥160/110: LABETALOL, NIFEDIPINE or HYDRALAZINE. ACE inhibitors, ARBs and atenolol are CONTRAINDICATED.** The target is a **controlled reduction — a precipitous fall compromises placental perfusion.**
- **DELIVERY IS THE ONLY CURE. Deliver immediately, regardless of gestation, for ECLAMPSIA, HELLP, pulmonary oedema, DIC, abruption, uncontrollable hypertension, non-reassuring fetal status or a gestation ≥34 weeks. Below 34 weeks with stable disease, give ANTENATAL CORTICOSTEROIDS and manage expectantly under close surveillance.**
- **HELLP SYNDROME — Haemolysis, Elevated Liver enzymes, Low Platelets — presents with epigastric pain and may occur WITHOUT hypertension; complications include SUBCAPSULAR LIVER HAEMATOMA and rupture.**
- **PROPHYLAXIS: LOW-DOSE ASPIRIN (75–150 mg at night) from 12–16 weeks in high-risk women, plus CALCIUM supplementation where dietary intake is low.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Severe features at 33 weeks → **IV magnesium sulphate first**.

**REMEMBER:** *Magnesium stops the fit, labetalol lowers the pressure, delivery cures the disease.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/20a6c3215a3540eaad8d2a7485603fa9x557x600.JPEG)

`Q9123`

## 14. Varicella in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** The risk of **CONGENITAL (FETAL) VARICELLA SYNDROME is HIGHEST when maternal infection occurs between 13 and 20 WEEKS (~2%)**, lower before 13 weeks (~0.4%) and rare after 20 weeks. Features: **CICATRICIAL SKIN SCARRING in a dermatomal/zigzag distribution, LIMB HYPOPLASIA, microcephaly and cortical atrophy, chorioretinitis, microphthalmia and cataract, and neurogenic bladder/hydronephrosis.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The syndrome is essentially **intrauterine SHINGLES** — the virus establishes latency in fetal dorsal root ganglia and reactivates in utero, which is why the **scarring follows a DERMATOMAL pattern and the underlying limb is hypoplastic (denervation of the developing limb bud).** The 13–20 week peak reflects the window when **limb and nervous system development is still active but the fetus cannot yet mount an immune response.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The other, more lethal risk is NEONATAL VARICELLA — when the mother develops the rash from 5 DAYS BEFORE to 2 DAYS AFTER DELIVERY. The virus crosses transplacentally but maternal IgG has not yet been made and transferred, so the newborn is infected without protection: mortality up to 30%.** **Give VZIG to the neonate and treat with IV ACYCLOVIR.**
- **MATERNAL VARICELLA PNEUMONIA is the major maternal danger (10–20% of adult cases, more severe in pregnancy and in smokers) — treat with IV ACYCLOVIR.** Oral acyclovir is given to any pregnant woman presenting within 24 hours of the rash after 20 weeks.
- **Post-exposure: check immunity (most adults are immune); a non-immune pregnant woman exposed to chickenpox should receive VZIG within 10 days of exposure.**
- **The VARICELLA VACCINE IS LIVE and therefore CONTRAINDICATED IN PREGNANCY; avoid conception for 1 month after vaccination — ideally vaccinate non-immune women BEFORE pregnancy.**
- **Compare the other congenital infections: RUBELLA — worst in the FIRST trimester, causing cataract, sensorineural deafness and PDA/pulmonary artery stenosis. CMV — periventricular calcification and deafness. TOXOPLASMOSIS — transmission risk RISES with gestation but SEVERITY FALLS, giving diffuse intracranial calcification, hydrocephalus and chorioretinitis. SYPHILIS — after 16 weeks; snuffles, Hutchinson's teeth, saddle nose, saber shins.**
- **HERPES ZOSTER (shingles) in pregnancy does NOT cause fetal varicella syndrome** — reactivation in an immune mother is not a fetal risk.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Maximum risk of fetal varicella syndrome" → **13–20 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *Thirteen to twenty weeks scars the skin and stunts the limb; five days either side of delivery kills the newborn.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/a9ca7f26f1f745e6b367632ca66f2ddfx383x576.JPEG)

`Q9125`

## 15. Mechanisms of Hormonal Contraception

**PEARL:** Combined oral contraceptives act by **INHIBITING GnRH (and thus LH/FSH) release — they do NOT increase GnRH** — plus **thickening cervical mucus** and rendering the **endometrium unfavourable for implantation.** **PROGESTERONE-ONLY PILLS work MAINLY by THICKENING CERVICAL MUCUS** (inhibiting sperm penetration), plus inhibiting tubal motility and suppressing ovulation in some cycles — **they do NOT kill sperm** (that is a spermicide).

**UNDERSTAND:** The primary mechanism differs because of what is doing the suppressing. **Oestrogen provides consistent, reliable suppression of the mid-cycle LH surge — so combined pills reliably block OVULATION.** Progestogen alone suppresses ovulation only inconsistently (except **desogestrel/Cerazette, which does inhibit ovulation in ~97% of cycles**), so its dependable contraceptive action is the **local, immediate effect on cervical mucus** — which also explains the reduced rates of PID and the strict timing requirements.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **POPs are the contraceptive of choice for BREASTFEEDING women (no effect on milk, and usable from 6 weeks — or immediately postpartum), and are SAFE where oestrogen is contraindicated: hypertension, smokers over 35, migraine WITH AURA, obesity, diabetes with vascular disease, SLE, and a history of VTE. They do NOT affect clotting, lipid or carbohydrate metabolism.** **The commonest side effect and reason for discontinuation is IRREGULAR BLEEDING.**
- **POPs must be taken at the SAME TIME EVERY DAY — the mucus effect wanes after ~27 hours (a 3-hour window; 12 hours for desogestrel).**
- **POPs reduce the ABSOLUTE risk of all pregnancy including ectopic, but the RELATIVE proportion of pregnancies that are ectopic rises** (tubal motility is inhibited) — a previous ectopic is NOT a contraindication.
- **Non-contraceptive benefits of COCs: reduced OVARIAN and ENDOMETRIAL CANCER risk (persisting for decades), less dysmenorrhoea and menorrhagia, improved acne, fewer functional ovarian cysts and less benign breast disease, and control of PCOS symptoms.** **Risks: VTE, and a small increase in cervical and breast cancer risk.**
- **Absolute contraindications to COCs: <6 weeks postpartum if breastfeeding, smoking ≥15 cigarettes/day over age 35, hypertension ≥160/100, VTE or known thrombophilia, ischaemic heart disease or stroke, MIGRAINE WITH AURA at any age, current breast cancer, severe cirrhosis/liver tumour, and major surgery with prolonged immobilisation.**
- **EMERGENCY contraception: LEVONORGESTREL 1.5 mg within 72 hours (mainly delays/inhibits ovulation), ULIPRISTAL within 120 hours (more effective, works closer to ovulation), and the COPPER IUCD within 5 days — the MOST EFFECTIVE method, which also provides ongoing contraception. MIFEPRISTONE acts primarily by preventing implantation.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "OCPs act by all EXCEPT" → **increase GnRH release**; "not a mechanism of POPs" → **kills sperm**.

**REMEMBER:** *Combined pills stop the egg; mini-pills stop the sperm at the cervix.*

`Q9126, Q9135`

---

## 16. The Biophysical Profile

**PEARL:** The BPP has **FIVE components: NON-STRESS TEST, FETAL BREATHING MOVEMENTS, GROSS BODY MOVEMENTS, FETAL TONE and AMNIOTIC FLUID VOLUME** — each scored 0 or 2, over 30 minutes, for a maximum of 10. **The CONTRACTION STRESS TEST is NOT a component.** A score of **8–10 is reassuring; 6 is equivocal; ≤4 indicates fetal ACIDAEMIA and usually mandates delivery.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The components are arranged in the **reverse order of their development in utero, and they are lost in that same reverse order under hypoxia** — the "gradual hypoxia" concept. **Fetal TONE develops first (7–8 weeks) and is lost LAST; movement (9 weeks), then breathing (20–21 weeks), then heart-rate reactivity (last to develop, ~28 weeks) is lost FIRST.** So **loss of NST reactivity is an early sign and loss of tone is a very late, ominous one.** **Amniotic fluid volume is different — it reflects CHRONIC hypoxia**, since sustained redistribution of blood away from the kidneys reduces fetal urine output.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Normal criteria: NST — ≥2 accelerations of 15 bpm for 15 seconds in 20–40 minutes. BREATHING — ≥1 episode of rhythmic breathing lasting ≥30 seconds. MOVEMENT — ≥3 discrete body/limb movements. TONE — ≥1 episode of extension with return to flexion. AMNIOTIC FLUID — a single deepest pocket ≥2 cm in two perpendicular planes.**
- **OLIGOHYDRAMNIOS makes ANY score abnormal — a "score of 8 with reduced fluid" is managed as abnormal and warrants delivery at term, because reduced fluid indicates chronic uteroplacental insufficiency.**
- **A NON-REACTIVE NST is most often due to a FETAL SLEEP CYCLE — use vibroacoustic stimulation or extend the test to 40 minutes before calling it abnormal. Prematurity, maternal sedation, smoking and magnesium also reduce reactivity.**
- **The MODIFIED BPP = NST + AMNIOTIC FLUID INDEX — a quicker screen combining an acute marker with a chronic one.**
- **The CONTRACTION STRESS TEST (oxytocin challenge) is a separate test: LATE decelerations with ≥50% of contractions is POSITIVE (abnormal). It is CONTRAINDICATED when labour is contraindicated — previous classical caesarean, placenta praevia, and risk of preterm labour.**
- **UMBILICAL ARTERY DOPPLER is the surveillance test that reduces perinatal mortality in GROWTH-RESTRICTED fetuses: increasing resistance → ABSENT end-diastolic flow → REVERSED end-diastolic flow (ominous). A DUCTUS VENOSUS "a" wave that is absent or reversed indicates cardiac decompensation and is the trigger for delivery in early-onset FGR.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not a component of the BPP" → **contraction stress test**.

**REMEMBER:** *Five components, thirty minutes, ten points — and the fluid is the chronic marker.*

`Q9127`

---

## 17. Cord Prolapse

**PEARL:** On finding a **PROLAPSED CORD, the IMMEDIATE step is to RELIEVE CORD COMPRESSION — put the woman in a STEEP TRENDELENBURG, exaggerated SIMS' or KNEE–CHEST position and ELEVATE THE PRESENTING PART manually — while arranging IMMEDIATE CAESAREAN SECTION. DO NOT ATTEMPT TO REPLACE THE CORD.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The fetus dies from **cord COMPRESSION between the presenting part and the pelvic brim, and from arterial VASOSPASM caused by cooling and handling** — not from the cord being outside. So every immediate measure aims at **getting the presenting part off the cord and keeping the cord warm and untouched.** Replacement attempts waste time, cause spasm, and fail.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Additional manoeuvres: FILLING THE BLADDER with 500–700 mL of saline (the VAGO method) elevates the presenting part and is useful when transfer will take time; TOCOLYSIS (terbutaline) reduces contractions; keep the cord warm and moist and handle it minimally.**
- **DELIVERY IS BY IMMEDIATE CAESAREAN unless the cervix is FULLY DILATED and instrumental vaginal delivery can be accomplished faster and safely** — the decision-to-delivery interval determines the outcome.
- **Risk factors: MALPRESENTATION (transverse lie, breech — especially FOOTLING), POLYHYDRAMNIOS, PREMATURITY and low birth weight, MULTIPLE PREGNANCY, a high or unengaged head, long cord, and — importantly — IATROGENIC causes: ARTIFICIAL RUPTURE OF MEMBRANES with a high presenting part, and internal podalic version.** **Never rupture membranes when the head is unengaged.**
- **Distinguish CORD PRESENTATION (cord below the presenting part with INTACT membranes — an obstetric warning) from CORD PROLAPSE (membranes RUPTURED) and from OCCULT prolapse (cord alongside, not below, the presenting part — presenting as variable decelerations).**
- **VARIABLE DECELERATIONS on the CTG are the hallmark of cord compression; a sudden prolonged deceleration or bradycardia immediately after membrane rupture should prompt an immediate vaginal examination.**
- **VASA PRAEVIA is the different, equally lethal entity — FETAL vessels crossing the membranes over the os, associated with a velamentous cord insertion, succenturiate lobe and IVF. It presents with painless bleeding and rapid FETAL exsanguination at membrane rupture; when diagnosed antenatally, deliver by ELECTIVE CAESAREAN at 34–36 weeks.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Midwife finds a prolapsed cord → **Trendelenburg position** (relieve compression first).

**REMEMBER:** *Lift the presenting part, never push the cord back, and go straight to theatre.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/0dc37cf41518466b91e256c25f641497x1280x1870.JPEG)

`Q9128`

## 18. Episiotomy

**PEARL:** In a **MEDIOLATERAL EPISIOTOMY the structures cut are: posterior vaginal wall, SUPERFICIAL and DEEP TRANSVERSE PERINEAL muscles, BULBOSPONGIOSUS, part of the LEVATOR ANI, the fascia over them, and the TRANSVERSE PERINEAL BRANCHES OF THE PUDENDAL VESSELS AND NERVES.** **The OBTURATOR muscle is NOT cut** — it lies on the lateral pelvic wall, far from the perineum.

**UNDERSTAND:** The mediolateral incision runs **downwards and outwards from the midpoint of the fourchette, at about 60° from the midline**, precisely to **direct the incision AWAY from the anal sphincter and rectum**. That single geometric decision is the whole reason for choosing it over a midline incision, and it explains its trade-offs: **less sphincter injury, but more bleeding, more pain and poorer healing.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **MIDLINE versus MEDIOLATERAL: MIDLINE — easier repair, less blood loss, less pain, better healing and less dyspareunia, BUT a much HIGHER risk of extension into the ANAL SPHINCTER AND RECTUM (third/fourth degree tear). MEDIOLATERAL — the reverse on every count, and the type used in most of the world including India.**
- **EPISIOTOMY IS NO LONGER PERFORMED ROUTINELY — restrictive use is evidence-based. Indications: a rigid perineum, instrumental delivery (especially forceps), shoulder dystocia, breech and other manipulative deliveries, an anticipated large baby, and fetal distress requiring expedited delivery.** **It is done at CROWNING, under local infiltration or pudendal block, when 3–4 cm of head is visible.**
- **PERINEAL TEAR degrees: FIRST — skin/vaginal mucosa only. SECOND — perineal muscles, sphincter intact (equivalent to an episiotomy). THIRD — involves the ANAL SPHINCTER (3a <50% of external sphincter, 3b >50%, 3c internal sphincter involved). FOURTH — through the ANAL EPITHELIUM.** **Third and fourth degree tears ("OASIS") need repair in theatre by an experienced operator, antibiotics, laxatives and follow-up — the risk is FAECAL INCONTINENCE and fistula.**
- **Complications of episiotomy: extension into the rectum, VULVAL HAEMATOMA, wound dehiscence and infection, blood loss, and later DYSPAREUNIA and rarely SCAR ENDOMETRIOSIS.**
- **The pudendal nerve (S2, 3, 4) supplies the perineum — a pudendal block is given transvaginally at the ISCHIAL SPINE.**
- **Episiotomy scissors are ANGULATED for exactly this purpose — to keep the blade directed away from the anal margin.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "All are cut EXCEPT" → **obturator muscle**.

**REMEMBER:** *Cut down and out at sixty degrees — everything in the perineum, nothing on the pelvic wall.*

## 19. Acute Fatty Liver of Pregnancy and Peripartum Cardiomyopathy

**PEARL:** **ACUTE FATTY LIVER OF PREGNANCY occurs in the THIRD TRIMESTER or early postpartum period and is the commonest cause of ACUTE LIVER FAILURE in pregnancy** — presenting with persistent nausea and vomiting, progressive JAUNDICE, epigastric pain, HYPOGLYCAEMIA, coagulopathy and encephalopathy. **PERIPARTUM CARDIOMYOPATHY is a DILATED cardiomyopathy with LEFT VENTRICULAR SYSTOLIC — not diastolic — DYSFUNCTION**, developing in the **last month of pregnancy or within 5 MONTHS of delivery**, with **NO identifiable cause and no pre-existing heart disease** (Pearson's criteria).

**UNDERSTAND:** AFLP is a **MITOCHONDRIAL fatty-acid oxidation disorder**: a fetus homozygous for **LCHAD (long-chain 3-hydroxyacyl-CoA dehydrogenase) deficiency** floods the heterozygous mother with unmetabolised fatty acid intermediates that overwhelm her liver — which is why **DELIVERY IS THE ONLY TREATMENT** and why the **newborn must be screened for a fatty-acid oxidation defect.** The microvesicular steatosis it produces is the same lesion as Reye's syndrome.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **AFLP vs HELLP: HYPOGLYCAEMIA, marked COAGULOPATHY with a LOW FIBRINOGEN, raised ammonia, encephalopathy and RENAL failure favour AFLP; HELLP has more prominent haemolysis, higher transaminases and hypertension. Both may coexist — 20–40% of AFLP is associated with pre-eclampsia. SWANSEA CRITERIA are used for diagnosis.** **Management: PROMPT DELIVERY, supportive intensive care (glucose, blood products, N-acetylcysteine), and transplantation in the few who do not recover.**
- **Other pregnancy liver disease: INTRAHEPATIC CHOLESTASIS OF PREGNANCY — third-trimester PRURITUS (palms and soles, worse at night) WITHOUT a rash, raised BILE ACIDS, with a risk of sudden STILLBIRTH; treat with URSODEOXYCHOLIC ACID and deliver at 37 weeks. HYPEREMESIS GRAVIDARUM causes transaminitis in the first trimester.**
- **PPCM: predisposed by MULTIPARITY, ADVANCED AGE, MULTIFETAL gestation, PRE-ECLAMPSIA, African ancestry and smoking; the mechanism involves a cleaved 16 kDa fragment of PROLACTIN and the placental antiangiogenic factor sFlt-1 — which is why BROMOCRIPTINE (a prolactin inhibitor) is used as adjunctive therapy alongside standard heart failure treatment.**
- **Heart failure treatment in pregnancy: beta-blockers, diuretics and hydralazine/nitrates are acceptable; ACE INHIBITORS AND ARBs ARE CONTRAINDICATED ANTENATALLY but may be used postpartum (enalapril and captopril are compatible with breastfeeding). Anticoagulate if the ejection fraction is very low.**
- **PROGNOSIS AND COUNSELLING: about half recover LV function within 6 months. A SUBSEQUENT PREGNANCY carries a high risk of relapse and is STRONGLY DISCOURAGED IF LV FUNCTION HAS NOT NORMALISED.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Trimester of AFLP" → **third**; "true about PPCM EXCEPT" → **diastolic dysfunction** (it is systolic).

**REMEMBER:** *AFLP is the fetus's enzyme defect poisoning the mother's liver — deliver her; PPCM is a dilated, systolic failure of the last month.*

`Q9130, Q9134`

---

## 20. Uterine Leiomyoma

**PEARL:** A hysterectomy specimen showing **MULTIPLE WELL-CIRCUMSCRIBED, FIRM, GREY-WHITE NODULES with a WHORLED CUT SURFACE = LEIOMYOMA (fibroid)** — the commonest benign tumour and the commonest pelvic tumour in women. **INTRAMURAL is the commonest type; SUBMUCOUS is the most SYMPTOMATIC (menorrhagia, infertility, recurrent miscarriage).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The **whorled appearance is the histology made visible** — interlacing bundles of smooth muscle and fibrous tissue. The **sharp circumscription reflects a compressed PSEUDOCAPSULE of surrounding myometrium** (not a true capsule), which is exactly what permits **enucleation at myomectomy**. Fibroids are **OESTROGEN- and PROGESTERONE-DEPENDENT**, hence their growth in the reproductive years and in pregnancy, and their **regression after menopause** — the single fact that governs both medical treatment and expectant management.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The commonest symptom is MENORRHAGIA (from increased endometrial surface area, impaired uterine contraction and venous congestion); others are pressure symptoms, subfertility, pain and, rarely, polycythaemia (erythropoietin secretion).** **Most fibroids are ASYMPTOMATIC and need no treatment.**
- **Degenerations: HYALINE (the commonest), CYSTIC, calcific ("WOMB STONES"), FATTY, and RED (CARNEOUS) DEGENERATION — which classically occurs IN PREGNANCY, presenting with acute pain, low-grade fever and localised tenderness, and is managed CONSERVATIVELY with analgesia.** **SARCOMATOUS change is rare (<0.5%)** — suspect it with rapid growth, especially POSTMENOPAUSAL growth.
- **In pregnancy fibroids may cause miscarriage, malpresentation, obstructed labour, placental abruption and PPH; MYOMECTOMY DURING PREGNANCY OR CAESAREAN IS AVOIDED because of torrential bleeding.**
- **Management: medical — tranexamic acid and NSAIDs for bleeding, the LNG-IUS (for a normal cavity), GnRH AGONISTS (shrink fibroids but cause hypo-oestrogenism and are used only for ~3–6 months preoperatively to reduce size and correct anaemia; the fibroids regrow on stopping), ulipristal and GnRH antagonists. Surgical — MYOMECTOMY (fertility-preserving; hysteroscopic for submucous), HYSTERECTOMY (definitive), and UTERINE ARTERY EMBOLISATION (avoid if fertility is desired).**
- **Distinguish ADENOMYOSIS: a diffusely enlarged, boggy, TENDER uterus with DYSMENORRHOEA and menorrhagia in a parous woman in her forties, with endometrial glands within the myometrium — no discrete whorled nodules, and MRI shows a thickened JUNCTIONAL ZONE.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Gross specimen with whorled white nodules → **leiomyoma**.

**REMEMBER:** *Firm, white, whorled and shelling out cleanly — a fibroid, and it shrinks after menopause.*

## 21. Uterotonics for Postpartum Haemorrhage

**PEARL:** **CARBOPROST (15-methyl PGF2α, Hemabate) is given as 0.25 mg IM, repeatable every 15–90 MINUTES to a MAXIMUM OF 8 DOSES = 2 mg.** It is **CONTRAINDICATED IN ASTHMA** (bronchoconstriction) and in suspected amniotic fluid embolism.

**UNDERSTAND:** Prostaglandins are **paracrine hormones acting where they are made — PGF2α comes from the DECIDUA and directly stimulates myometrial contractility**, which is why it works when oxytocin (a receptor-mediated agent, subject to receptor desaturation after prolonged labour or oxytocin augmentation) has failed. Its adverse effects — **bronchospasm, diarrhoea, vomiting, fever, hypertension** — are all predictable smooth-muscle and thermoregulatory effects of a prostaglandin given systemically.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The stepwise medical management of ATONIC PPH: OXYTOCIN (10 IU IM or 5 IU slow IV, then an infusion — first-line, and used routinely in ACTIVE MANAGEMENT OF THE THIRD STAGE) → ERGOMETRINE/methylergometrine 0.2 mg IM (CONTRAINDICATED IN HYPERTENSION, pre-eclampsia and heart disease) → CARBOPROST 0.25 mg IM (contraindicated in ASTHMA) → MISOPROSTOL 800–1000 µg per rectum (heat-stable, ideal where refrigeration is unavailable).** **TRANEXAMIC ACID within 3 hours reduces death from bleeding (the WOMAN trial) and should be given early.**
- **Mechanical and surgical steps in order: uterine massage and bimanual compression → BALLOON TAMPONADE (Bakri) → compression sutures (B-LYNCH) → stepwise devascularisation (uterine and internal iliac artery ligation) → HYSTERECTOMY as the last resort. Aortic compression and a non-pneumatic anti-shock garment buy time during transfer.**
- **The causes of PPH are the "4 Ts": TONE (atony — 70–80%, the commonest), TRAUMA (tears, rupture, inversion), TISSUE (retained placenta), THROMBIN (coagulopathy).**
- **Definition: blood loss ≥500 mL after vaginal delivery or ≥1000 mL after caesarean, or any loss causing haemodynamic instability. PRIMARY = within 24 hours; SECONDARY = 24 hours to 12 weeks (usually retained products or endometritis).**
- **ACTIVE MANAGEMENT OF THE THIRD STAGE — prophylactic uterotonic, controlled cord traction and uterine massage — is the single most effective preventive measure.**
- **Carboprost is also used as a second-trimester ABORTIFACIENT.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Maximum dose of PGF2α in PPH" → **2 mg** (8 × 0.25 mg).

**REMEMBER:** *Oxytocin, then ergometrine unless hypertensive, then carboprost unless asthmatic — eight doses, two milligrams, no more.*

`Q9137`

---

## 22. First-Trimester Aneuploidy Screening

**PEARL:** **NUCHAL TRANSLUCENCY is measured between 11 and 14 WEEKS (CRL 45–84 mm)** — the maximum thickness of the subcutaneous translucency behind the fetal neck. **Combined first-trimester screening = NT + free β-hCG + PAPP-A.** In **DOWN SYNDROME, β-hCG is HIGH and PAPP-A is LOW; in TRISOMY 18 and 13, BOTH are LOW.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The window is fixed by biology: **before 11 weeks the fetus is too small to measure reliably, and after 14 weeks the fetal lymphatic system matures and drains the nuchal fluid**, so the marker disappears. Increased NT reflects **impaired lymphatic drainage, cardiac dysfunction or altered extracellular matrix** — which is why a raised NT with a NORMAL karyotype still demands a **detailed FETAL ECHOCARDIOGRAM**, since it also flags major congenital heart disease.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Other first-trimester ultrasound markers: ABSENT NASAL BONE, reversed "a" wave in the DUCTUS VENOSUS, and tricuspid regurgitation.**
- **SECOND-TRIMESTER screening (15–20 weeks) is the QUADRUPLE TEST — AFP, unconjugated oestriol, hCG and INHIBIN A. In DOWN SYNDROME: hCG and INHIBIN A HIGH, AFP and OESTRIOL LOW. In trisomy 18, all except inhibin are low. A HIGH AFP suggests NEURAL TUBE DEFECT, abdominal wall defect, multiple pregnancy or wrong dates.**
- **The NUCHAL FOLD is a different, SECOND-TRIMESTER measurement (≥6 mm is abnormal) — do not confuse it with nuchal translucency.**
- **CELL-FREE FETAL DNA (NIPT) from maternal plasma, done from 10 weeks, has the highest detection rate (>99% for trisomy 21) with a very low false-positive rate — but it remains a SCREENING test; a positive result must be confirmed by CVS or amniocentesis.**
- **DIAGNOSTIC tests: CHORIONIC VILLUS SAMPLING at 10–13 weeks (earlier result, ~1% loss rate, risk of limb reduction if performed before 10 weeks, and confined placental mosaicism); AMNIOCENTESIS from 15 weeks (~0.5% or lower loss rate).**
- **A cystic hygroma (a grossly increased NT with septations) carries a high risk of TURNER SYNDROME, aneuploidy, cardiac defects and fetal demise.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Ideal time for nuchal translucency" → **11–14 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *Eleven to fourteen weeks — after that the lymphatics drain the evidence away.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/80eecda627d649f88eabb4729ca2c85ax796x599.JPEG)

`Q9140`

## 23. The Appearance of Amniotic Fluid at Term

**PEARL:** At **TERM the amniotic fluid is CLOUDY WITH WHITE FLECKS (VERNIX CASEOSA)**. The progression is: **clear and flake-free at ~32 weeks → a few flakes by 36 weeks → increasingly cloudy with abundant flakes at term.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The flecks are **shed VERNIX, lanugo and desquamated fetal squames** — so their quantity is a direct index of **skin maturity**. This is the same principle behind the old **"amniotic fluid macroscore"** and behind cytological tests of maturity (Nile blue staining of fetal fat cells). Fluid appearance is therefore a crude marker of gestational age, seen at amniotomy or amnioscopy.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Abnormal colours and their meaning: GREENISH/yellow — MECONIUM staining (fetal distress, or simply post-dates); GOLDEN/yellow — Rh isoimmunisation (bilirubin); GREENISH-YELLOW — postmaturity; DARK "tobacco juice"/brown — old intrauterine fetal death; and a foul odour with turbidity — CHORIOAMNIONITIS.**
- **AMNIOTIC FLUID VOLUME peaks at ~34 weeks (~800–1000 mL) and DECLINES thereafter — being about 400 mL at 42 weeks. After 16–20 weeks, fluid volume is determined mainly by FETAL URINE production and swallowing.**
- **OLIGOHYDRAMNIOS (AFI <5 cm or single deepest pocket <2 cm): renal agenesis (POTTER SEQUENCE — pulmonary hypoplasia, limb and facial deformities), obstructive uropathy, ruptured membranes, placental insufficiency/FGR, post-dates, and NSAIDs/ACE inhibitors.**
- **POLYHYDRAMNIOS (AFI >24 cm or pocket >8 cm): maternal DIABETES, oesophageal/duodenal ATRESIA and other swallowing defects, ANENCEPHALY, twin–twin transfusion, fetal anaemia/hydrops, and chest masses. Management for symptoms: amnioreduction and INDOMETHACIN (which reduces fetal urine output — avoid after 32 weeks because of ductal constriction).**
- **MECONIUM-STAINED liquor requires attention to fetal status; routine intrapartum suctioning is no longer recommended, and vigorous babies need no intervention.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Normal amniotic fluid at term" → **cloudy with white specks**.

**REMEMBER:** *The flecks are the baby's shed vernix — the more there are, the more mature the skin.*

`Q9113`

---

## 24. Contraindications to Induction of Labour

**PEARL:** **HEART DISEASE is NOT a contraindication to induction — vaginal delivery is actually PREFERRED in most cardiac patients.** Contraindications are essentially **the contraindications to labour itself**: **placenta praevia and vasa praevia, transverse lie, cord prolapse, PELVIC TUMOUR obstructing the birth canal, a previous CLASSICAL caesarean or myomectomy entering the cavity, ACTIVE GENITAL HERPES, invasive cervical cancer, and severe cephalopelvic disproportion.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Cardiac patients tolerate **labour better than surgery**: caesarean section brings greater blood loss, larger fluid shifts, anaesthetic risk and a higher thromboembolic burden. The dangerous moment in labour is the **IMMEDIATE POSTPARTUM AUTOTRANSFUSION** of ~500 mL from the contracting uterus into the circulation — so the strategy is a **short, painless second stage with an assisted vaginal delivery, good analgesia (epidural) and careful fluid balance**, not a caesarean.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Cardiac disease is the leading INDIRECT cause of maternal death. Caesarean is reserved for OBSTETRIC indications, plus a few cardiac ones: severe aortic stenosis, aortic root dilatation/MARFAN, EISENMENGER SYNDROME, pulmonary hypertension, and a woman anticoagulated with WARFARIN at the time of labour (risk of fetal intracranial bleed).**
- **A previous LOWER SEGMENT transverse caesarean is NOT a contraindication to induction — VBAC is permissible, though PROSTAGLANDINS increase the risk of scar rupture, so mechanical methods (Foley catheter) or careful oxytocin are preferred.** **A previous CLASSICAL (vertical) incision IS a contraindication.**
- **BISHOP SCORE (dilatation, effacement, station, consistency, position) predicts inducibility: ≥6–8 is favourable; ≤5 warrants CERVICAL RIPENING with prostaglandin E2 (dinoprostone), misoprostol (PGE1) or a Foley catheter.**
- **Methods: membrane sweeping, amniotomy, oxytocin infusion, prostaglandins, and mechanical dilators.** **Complications: uterine HYPERSTIMULATION with fetal distress, scar rupture, failed induction leading to caesarean, cord prolapse at amniotomy, and water intoxication with high-dose oxytocin (its ADH-like effect).**
- **Common indications: post-dates (≥41 weeks), pre-eclampsia, prelabour rupture of membranes, diabetes, growth restriction, oligohydramnios, chorioamnionitis and fetal death.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not a contraindication" → **heart disease**.

**REMEMBER:** *Cardiac patients should labour, not be operated on — the danger is the autotransfusion, not the contractions.*

`Q9142`

---

## 25. Normal Cardiac Changes in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** Normal in pregnancy: a **15° LEFT AXIS DEVIATION, mild ST changes in the INFERIOR leads, atrial and ventricular PREMATURE CONTRACTIONS, a loud widely split S1, an S3, a SYSTOLIC EJECTION (flow) murmur, a venous hum and a MAMMARY SOUFFLÉ.** **A PANSYSTOLIC (or any DIASTOLIC) MURMUR IS NEVER PHYSIOLOGICAL** and requires investigation.

**UNDERSTAND:** The physiological changes all follow from **a 40–50% rise in plasma volume and cardiac output with a fall in systemic vascular resistance**, plus **elevation of the diaphragm** by the gravid uterus. High flow across normal valves produces a **soft systolic ejection murmur; the raised diaphragm rotates the heart, giving left axis deviation and inferior ST-T changes.** But **no amount of flow creates a pansystolic or diastolic murmur** — those require a structurally abnormal valve or a shunt, which is why the rule holds.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Haemodynamics: cardiac output rises 30–50%, peaking at ~24–28 weeks, and rises FURTHER during labour and immediately postpartum (the autotransfusion). Plasma volume rises MORE than red cell mass, producing PHYSIOLOGICAL ANAEMIA OF PREGNANCY (haemodilution). Systemic vascular resistance and blood pressure FALL, reaching a nadir in the SECOND trimester and returning to baseline by term.**
- **Because blood pressure physiologically falls in mid-pregnancy, a woman with undiagnosed CHRONIC HYPERTENSION may appear normotensive at booking and "develop hypertension" in the third trimester** — a classic diagnostic trap.
- **Respiratory changes: TIDAL VOLUME and minute ventilation INCREASE (progesterone-driven), producing a COMPENSATED RESPIRATORY ALKALOSIS (pCO2 ~30 mmHg with a low bicarbonate); functional residual capacity FALLS; vital capacity is UNCHANGED. Dyspnoea is common and normal.**
- **Haematological: a hypercoagulable state (raised fibrinogen and factors VII, VIII, X; falling protein S), leucocytosis, and a raised ESR and D-dimer — so D-DIMER IS USELESS for excluding VTE in pregnancy.**
- **NYHA class and pulmonary hypertension determine cardiac risk; the WHO classification guides counselling. Pulmonary hypertension and Eisenmenger syndrome carry very high maternal mortality and are indications to AVOID PREGNANCY.**
- **PERIPARTUM CARDIOMYOPATHY and mitral stenosis (which decompensates as tachycardia shortens diastolic filling) are the classic decompensations of pregnancy.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Normal physiological change EXCEPT" → **pansystolic murmur**.

**REMEMBER:** *Flow murmurs are systolic and soft — anything pansystolic or diastolic belongs to disease.*

`Q9143`

---

## 26. Lactational Mastitis

**PEARL:** ***STAPHYLOCOCCUS AUREUS* is the commonest organism, and DICLOXACILLIN (or flucloxacillin/cephalexin) 500 mg four times daily for 10–14 days is first-line;** **ERYTHROMYCIN/clindamycin if penicillin-allergic, and VANCOMYCIN or cotrimoxazole/clindamycin if MRSA is suspected.** **BREASTFEEDING MUST CONTINUE from the affected breast** — it is both safe for the baby and essential to treatment.

**UNDERSTAND:** Mastitis begins as **MILK STASIS** — an inadequately drained segment — which then becomes infected via a cracked nipple. That is why **continued and effective drainage is the single most important intervention**: stopping feeding worsens engorgement, raises intraductal pressure and drives progression to abscess. The organism comes from the **infant's nasopharynx and the mother's skin**, entering through nipple fissures — which is why correcting the **latch** prevents recurrence.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **It typically occurs in the FIRST WEEKS of lactation (inexperience, poor latch, engorgement) or at WEANING. Presentation: a wedge-shaped area of redness, pain and swelling with FEVER, rigors and flu-like malaise — the systemic upset distinguishes it from simple engorgement.**
- **Suspect an ABSCESS if there is FLUCTUATION or failure to improve within 48–72 hours of antibiotics — confirm by ULTRASOUND and treat by NEEDLE ASPIRATION UNDER ULTRASOUND GUIDANCE (now preferred) or incision and drainage; continue breastfeeding or express.**
- **RECURRENT or non-resolving "mastitis", especially with peau d'orange and nipple changes, must raise INFLAMMATORY BREAST CARCINOMA — biopsy the skin. This is the diagnosis that must not be missed.**
- **Supportive care: frequent effective emptying, analgesia (ibuprofen/paracetamol — both safe in lactation), warm compresses before and cold after feeds, breast support, and fluids.**
- **NON-LACTATIONAL periductal mastitis is different: it occurs in SMOKERS, is often anaerobic/mixed, is associated with DUCT ECTASIA, nipple retraction and MAMMARY DUCT FISTULA, and needs anaerobic cover (amoxicillin-clavulanate or metronidazole).**
- **BREAST ENGORGEMENT (bilateral, day 3–5, no fever or low-grade only) is managed with frequent feeding and expression, not antibiotics.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "First-line treatment of lactational mastitis" → **dicloxacillin**.

**REMEMBER:** *Keep the breast empty and give an anti-staphylococcal penicillin — stopping feeds makes an abscess.*

`Q9144`

---

## 27. Magnesium Sulphate — Monitoring and Toxicity

**PEARL:** **Toxicity in order of appearance: LOSS OF THE PATELLAR (deep tendon) REFLEX at ~10 mEq/L — the EARLIEST sign; RESPIRATORY DEPRESSION (<12/min) at ~12 mEq/L; and CARDIAC ARREST at ~25–30 mEq/L.** Therapeutic level is **4–7 mEq/L (4.8–8.4 mg/dL).** **REDUCED URINE OUTPUT IS NOT A FEATURE OF TOXICITY — it is a CAUSE of it**, since magnesium is renally excreted. **Antidote: 1 g (10 mL of 10%) CALCIUM GLUCONATE IV.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Magnesium is a **physiological calcium antagonist** — it blocks calcium entry at the presynaptic nerve terminal and reduces acetylcholine release, so **neuromuscular transmission fails progressively from the most sensitive structures (reflex arcs) to the least (cardiac conduction).** That produces the predictable ladder of signs, and it is why **calcium — which competes directly at the same site — reverses it instantly.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Before EVERY repeat dose check the three parameters: PATELLAR REFLEX PRESENT, RESPIRATORY RATE ≥12–16/min, and URINE OUTPUT ≥30 mL/hour (100 mL over 4 hours). If any fails, withhold the dose.** Serum levels are not routinely needed if these are monitored.
- **Regimens: PRITCHARD (IM — 4 g IV + 10 g IM loading, then 5 g IM alternate buttocks 4-hourly), ZUSPAN (IV — 4 g load then 1 g/hour), plus the DHAKA and SIBAI (single/low-dose) regimens used where monitoring is limited.** **"MAGPIE" IS A TRIAL, NOT A REGIMEN** — it established that magnesium halves the risk of eclampsia.
- **Continue for 24 HOURS after delivery or after the last convulsion**, whichever is later.
- **Magnesium is also used for FETAL NEUROPROTECTION before anticipated preterm birth under 32 weeks (reducing cerebral palsy) and as a TOCOLYTIC (less favoured).**
- **CAUTION with concurrent CALCIUM CHANNEL BLOCKERS (profound hypotension and neuromuscular blockade) and with MYASTHENIA GRAVIS (magnesium is contraindicated — it precipitates crisis). It also potentiates non-depolarising neuromuscular blockers.**
- **Other toxicity features: flushing, warmth, nausea, double vision, slurred speech, muscle weakness, pulmonary oedema and heart block.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Features of magnesium toxicity" → **loss of deep tendon reflexes and reduced respiratory rate** only; "patellar reflex disappears at" → **10 mEq/L**.

**REMEMBER:** *Reflex, respiration, urine output before every dose — and calcium gluconate on the trolley.*

`Q9602, Q9613, Q9624`

---

## 28. Screening and Delivery Timing in Diabetic Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **In EARLY pregnancy (first visit) a woman with RISK FACTORS is screened with HbA1c (or fasting glucose) to detect UNRECOGNISED OVERT/pre-gestational diabetes.** **The GLUCOSE CHALLENGE TEST is a NON-FASTING 50 g screen performed at 24–28 WEEKS; a positive result is followed by a formal GLUCOSE TOLERANCE TEST.** **OVERT (pre-gestational) diabetes on insulin is delivered at ~38 WEEKS; well-controlled GDM at 39–40 weeks.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The two time points test two different things. **At 8 weeks, hyperglycaemia means the woman was ALREADY diabetic before conception — which matters because it implies teratogenic exposure during organogenesis, so HbA1c (an integrated measure of pre-conception control) is the right test.** **At 24–28 weeks, insulin resistance from placental hormones (human placental lactogen, progesterone, cortisol, prolactin) peaks — so that is when true GESTATIONAL diabetes declares itself.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The GCT is done in the NON-FASTING state (its convenience is the point); a 1-hour value ≥140 mg/dL is positive. LOWERING the threshold RAISES SENSITIVITY and lowers specificity.** In India, **DIPSI** uses a single non-fasting 75 g load with a 2-hour value ≥140 mg/dL as diagnostic in one step.
- **Overt diabetes is delivered EARLIER than GDM because of the higher stillbirth risk, vasculopathy and macrosomia; poor control shifts delivery earlier still (37–38+6 weeks), while awaiting 39 weeks is safe only with excellent control.**
- **Fetal surveillance in pre-gestational diabetes: detailed ANOMALY SCAN plus FETAL ECHOCARDIOGRAPHY at 18–22 weeks, serial growth scans, and antenatal testing from 32 weeks.**
- **Insulin requirements FALL in the first trimester (fetal glucose draw and nausea), RISE steadily from the second trimester, and DROP ABRUPTLY after delivery of the placenta** — insulin doses must be reduced immediately postpartum to avoid hypoglycaemia.
- **During labour, maintain glucose 70–110 mg/dL with an insulin–dextrose infusion to prevent NEONATAL HYPOGLYCAEMIA (fetal hyperinsulinaemia responds to intrapartum maternal hyperglycaemia).**
- **Screen all GDM women with a 75 g OGTT at 6–12 weeks postpartum and lifelong thereafter.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Screening at 8 weeks with a family history → **HbA1c**; GCT → **non-fasting, 24–28 weeks**; overt diabetes on insulin → **deliver at 38 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *HbA1c early to catch the pre-existing diabetic; a non-fasting challenge at 24–28 weeks to catch the gestational one.*

`Q9603, Q9604, Q9616`

---

## 29. Glucose and Insulin Physiology in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **MATERNAL INSULIN DOES NOT CROSS THE PLACENTA — GLUCOSE DOES (by facilitated diffusion).** Maternal **insulin SECRETION INCREASES** to overcome the **INSULIN RESISTANCE caused by placental hormones (human placental lactogen, progesterone, cortisol, prolactin, TNF-α)**, and the **FETUS BEGINS SECRETING ITS OWN INSULIN AT ~12 WEEKS.**

**UNDERSTAND:** This single asymmetry — **glucose crosses, insulin does not** — explains the entire pathology of diabetic pregnancy (the **PEDERSEN HYPOTHESIS**). Maternal hyperglycaemia becomes fetal hyperglycaemia; the fetal pancreas responds with **hyperinsulinaemia**; and **insulin is the fetus's principal anabolic growth factor**, producing macrosomia, organomegaly and increased oxygen consumption. At delivery the glucose supply is cut but the hyperinsulinaemia persists — hence **neonatal hypoglycaemia within hours.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Pregnancy is deliberately DIABETOGENIC: insulin resistance in the second half diverts glucose to the fetus. Fasting glucose is LOWER than in the non-pregnant state (continuous fetal draw, "accelerated starvation" with a tendency to KETOSIS), while POSTPRANDIAL levels are HIGHER and more prolonged.**
- ***SIRENOMELIA (MERMAID SYNDROME) and CAUDAL REGRESSION are the anomalies most CHARACTERISTIC of pre-gestational diabetes; CARDIAC defects (VSD commonest) are the most FREQUENT, followed by neural tube defects.***
- **Fetal hyperinsulinaemia also causes: increased oxygen demand → relative hypoxia → ERYTHROPOIETIN release → POLYCYTHAEMIA and subsequent hyperbilirubinaemia; DELAYED SURFACTANT production (insulin antagonises cortisol) → RESPIRATORY DISTRESS SYNDROME even at term; and organomegaly with SEPTAL HYPERTROPHY of the heart.**
- **Macrosomia in diabetes is TRUNCAL and shoulder-predominant (insulin-sensitive adipose tissue) with a relatively normal head — which is precisely why SHOULDER DYSTOCIA is so much commoner than in constitutionally large babies.**
- **Because insulin does not cross the placenta, INSULIN IS SAFE IN PREGNANCY and is the treatment of choice; metformin and glyburide DO cross.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "True about pregnancy physiology EXCEPT" → **maternal insulin crosses the placenta**.

**REMEMBER:** *Glucose crosses and insulin does not — so the fetus makes its own and grows fat on it.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/15/picture14_kY8nI7M.jpg)

`Q9605, Q9606, Q9615`
