## 30. Renal Changes and Acute Kidney Injury in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** In pregnancy the **kidneys ENLARGE by ~1–1.5 cm; GFR and renal plasma flow RISE by ~50%, so SERUM CREATININE AND UREA FALL (a creatinine of 1.0 mg/dL is ABNORMAL in pregnancy); GLYCOSURIA occurs in up to 50% of normal women; and there is PHYSIOLOGICAL HYDRONEPHROSIS — which is MORE MARKED ON THE RIGHT, not the left.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Right-sided predominance has two anatomical causes: the **DEXTROROTATION of the gravid uterus**, and the **right ureter crossing the iliac and ovarian vessels at a sharper angle**, while the **left ureter is cushioned by the sigmoid colon.** Progesterone-induced smooth muscle relaxation adds to it. The stasis explains why **asymptomatic bacteriuria progresses to PYELONEPHRITIS in up to 30% of untreated pregnant women — and why it is one of the few situations where asymptomatic bacteriuria MUST be treated.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Glycosuria occurs because the rise in GFR exceeds the tubular reabsorptive capacity for glucose — so it does NOT reliably indicate diabetes and is useless as a screening test for GDM.**
- **OBSTETRIC causes of acute kidney injury: PRE-ECLAMPSIA/ECLAMPSIA and HELLP, ABRUPTIO PLACENTAE, POSTPARTUM HAEMORRHAGE and hypovolaemic shock, SEPTIC ABORTION and puerperal sepsis, ACUTE FATTY LIVER OF PREGNANCY, amniotic fluid embolism, and the pregnancy-specific microangiopathies.** **ANTIPHOSPHOLIPID SYNDROME causes renal injury but is a MEDICAL/thrombotic, not an obstetric, cause.**
- **Bilateral RENAL CORTICAL NECROSIS is classically associated with abruption and septic abortion in pregnancy — a cause of irreversible renal failure.**
- **Postpartum, consider ATYPICAL HUS/pregnancy-associated thrombotic microangiopathy when renal failure and haemolysis persist or worsen after delivery — treated with ECULIZUMAB.**
- **Anatomical/physiological consequences to remember: uric acid FALLS in early pregnancy and RISES in pre-eclampsia; there is a mild respiratory alkalosis with compensatory bicarbonate loss; and bladder capacity and urinary frequency change with uterine size.**
- **Renal biopsy and imaging: ULTRASOUND is safe; MRI without gadolinium is acceptable; GADOLINIUM IS AVOIDED (it crosses the placenta).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not seen in renal changes of pregnancy" → **pelvicalyceal dilatation more on the LEFT** (it is more on the right).

**REMEMBER:** *Everything gets bigger and faster, creatinine falls, and the RIGHT ureter is the one that kinks.*

`Q9607, Q9609`

---

## 31. Liver Disease in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **VIRAL HEPATITIS — not obstetric cholestasis — is the COMMONEST CAUSE OF JAUNDICE IN PREGNANCY** (in India, hepatitis E is particularly dangerous, with up to 20% mortality in the third trimester). **INTRAHEPATIC CHOLESTASIS OF PREGNANCY is the commonest PREGNANCY-SPECIFIC liver disorder**: third-trimester **PRURITUS of the PALMS AND SOLES, worse at night, WITHOUT a primary rash**, with raised **BILE ACIDS**, a **recurrence rate of 45–70%**, and a risk of **meconium staining, preterm birth and sudden STILLBIRTH.** **GESTATIONAL DIABETES does NOT cause liver dysfunction; hyperemesis, cholestasis, pre-eclampsia/HELLP and AFLP all do.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The itch of cholestasis is caused by **retained BILE ACIDS**, which is why the diagnosis rests on **serum bile acids rather than transaminases or bilirubin** (jaundice occurs in only ~10%). It is also why **fetal risk correlates with the BILE ACID LEVEL** — concentrations >100 µmol/L carry the highest stillbirth risk, presumably through a direct arrhythmogenic effect of bile acids on the fetal heart. **Delivery removes the placental hormonal drive and the disease resolves within days.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Cholestasis management: URSODEOXYCHOLIC ACID (improves itch and biochemistry), vitamin K if the prothrombin time is prolonged (fat-soluble vitamin malabsorption), and DELIVERY at 37–38 weeks (earlier if bile acids are very high). It recurs in subsequent pregnancies and with combined oral contraceptives.**
- **Map the liver disorders by trimester: FIRST — hyperemesis gravidarum (mild transaminitis, ketonuria, weight loss; associated with molar and multiple pregnancy; treat with fluids, THIAMINE to prevent Wernicke's, and antiemetics). SECOND/THIRD — cholestasis. THIRD — pre-eclampsia/HELLP and AFLP.**
- **HELLP: haemolysis (schistocytes, raised LDH), transaminases >2× normal, platelets <100,000 — may occur WITHOUT hypertension; watch for SUBCAPSULAR HAEMATOMA of the liver.**
- **AFLP: hypoglycaemia, coagulopathy with LOW FIBRINOGEN, encephalopathy, renal failure; associated with fetal LCHAD deficiency; treatment is DELIVERY.**
- **HEPATITIS E in pregnancy causes fulminant hepatic failure disproportionately in the third trimester and is a major cause of maternal death in India — it is faeco-orally transmitted, so prevention is water sanitation.**
- **Distinguish PRURITUS WITH a rash (PUPPP/polymorphic eruption of pregnancy, pemphigoid gestationis, atopic eruption) from pruritus WITHOUT a rash (cholestasis) — the presence of a primary lesion is the discriminator.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Incorrect about obstetric cholestasis" → **it is the commonest cause of jaundice in pregnancy** (viral hepatitis is).

**REMEMBER:** *Itchy palms and soles at night with no rash — check bile acids and deliver at 37 weeks.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/04/30/screenshot-2023-04-30-144703.jpg)

`Q9610, Q9611`

## 32. Classifying Hypertension in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **GESTATIONAL HYPERTENSION = BP ≥140/90 arising AFTER 20 WEEKS WITHOUT proteinuria or other severe features, in a previously normotensive woman** — it is a **RETROSPECTIVE, provisional diagnosis that requires BP to RETURN TO NORMAL BY 12 WEEKS POSTPARTUM** (if it persists, it was CHRONIC hypertension all along). **About 10–15% of eclamptic seizures occur in women with "gestational hypertension" and no proteinuria** — which is why the absence of proteinuria is never reassuring.

**UNDERSTAND:** The 20-week dividing line exists because **hypertension caused by pregnancy requires an established placenta with abnormal spiral artery invasion** — so hypertension appearing BEFORE 20 weeks is almost always pre-existing (or a hydatidiform mole, the classic exception). The postpartum requirement follows the same logic: **placental disease resolves once the placenta is gone**, so persistent hypertension means the diagnosis was wrong.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The four categories: (1) GESTATIONAL hypertension; (2) PRE-ECLAMPSIA–ECLAMPSIA; (3) CHRONIC hypertension (before 20 weeks or persisting >12 weeks postpartum); (4) CHRONIC hypertension with SUPERIMPOSED PRE-ECLAMPSIA.**
- **Superimposed pre-eclampsia is diagnosed by NEW-ONSET PROTEINURIA (or a sudden increase in existing proteinuria), a sudden rise in previously controlled BP, THROMBOCYTOPENIA <100,000, rising transaminases, new renal insufficiency, pulmonary oedema, new cerebral/visual symptoms, or FRESH RETINAL CHANGES.** **A RISE OF 30 mmHg SYSTOLIC / 15 mmHg DIASTOLIC IS NO LONGER A CRITERION** — it was abandoned because it lacks predictive value; this is the most examined negative.
- **PRE-ECLAMPSIA can be diagnosed WITHOUT PROTEINURIA if hypertension after 20 weeks is accompanied by PLATELETS <100,000, CREATININE >1.1 mg/dL (or doubled), transaminases twice normal, pulmonary oedema, or new headache/visual symptoms.** Proteinuria is defined as **≥300 mg in 24 hours, a protein:creatinine ratio ≥0.3, or dipstick 2+**.
- **IMMINENT (impending) ECLAMPSIA: severe frontal/occipital HEADACHE, VISUAL disturbance (scotomata, blurring — from retinal vasospasm, occipital vasogenic oedema or retinal detachment), EPIGASTRIC/right upper quadrant PAIN (stretching of Glisson's capsule from hepatic ischaemia and oedema), sudden generalised OEDEMA, oliguria, and brisk reflexes with clonus.**
- **About one third of eclamptic seizures occur POSTPARTUM (up to 6 weeks) — so vigilance does not end at delivery.**
- **Postpartum antihypertensives compatible with breastfeeding: LABETALOL, NIFEDIPINE, METOPROLOL, ENALAPRIL, CAPTOPRIL, atenolol ("ECLAM-N"). AMLODIPINE lacks safety data and is avoided; diuretics may reduce milk supply.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** BP 150/100 after 20 weeks, no proteinuria, no symptoms → **gestational hypertension**; "not a criterion for superimposed pre-eclampsia" → **the 30/15 mmHg rise**.

**REMEMBER:** *After twenty weeks and gone by twelve weeks postpartum — otherwise it was never gestational.*

## 33. Predicting and Preventing Pre-eclampsia

**PEARL:** **sFlt-1 (soluble fms-like tyrosine kinase-1) and s-ENG (soluble endoglin) are ANTI-ANGIOGENIC factors that are INCREASED — not decreased — in women destined to develop pre-eclampsia**, while the pro-angiogenic **PlGF and PAPP-A FALL.** **LOW-DOSE ASPIRIN 150 mg at night, started BEFORE 16 WEEKS, is the intervention that reduces risk** (the ASPRE trial); **calcium supplementation helps only where dietary intake is low, and salt restriction and antioxidants do NOT work.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Pre-eclampsia begins with **failed trophoblastic invasion of the spiral arteries in the first half of pregnancy**, leaving them narrow and high-resistance; the ischaemic placenta then releases **anti-angiogenic factors that bind and neutralise VEGF and PlGF, producing systemic ENDOTHELIAL DYSFUNCTION** — the vasospasm, proteinuria, oedema and organ injury. **Aspirin must be started before 16 weeks because that is while placentation is still occurring** — given later, there is nothing left to modify.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **BIOPHYSICAL prediction: raised UTERINE ARTERY PULSATILITY INDEX at 11–14 weeks, and a persistent DIASTOLIC NOTCH in the uterine artery waveform beyond 24 weeks — the notch reflects the unremodelled, high-resistance spiral arteries.**
- **BIOCHEMICAL: low PAPP-A and PlGF, high sFlt-1 and s-Eng, and a raised sFlt-1:PlGF RATIO (now used clinically to RULE OUT pre-eclampsia in the short term). CELL-FREE FETAL DNA is INCREASED because of accelerated cytotrophoblast apoptosis.**
- **Obsolete tests still asked: the ROLL-OVER TEST at 28–32 weeks (a ≥20 mmHg rise in diastolic BP on turning supine) and the ANGIOTENSIN II SENSITIVITY TEST — both reflect the loss of the normal pregnancy REFRACTORINESS TO ANGIOTENSIN II.**
- **HIGH-RISK criteria warranting aspirin: previous pre-eclampsia, chronic hypertension, chronic kidney disease, pre-gestational diabetes, autoimmune disease (SLE/APS), and multifetal gestation. MODERATE risk factors (two or more): nulliparity, age ≥35, BMI >30, family history, IVF conception, and an interpregnancy interval >10 years.**
- **Pathophysiology in one line: INADEQUATE SPIRAL ARTERY REMODELLING → placental ischaemia → anti-angiogenic factor release → generalised ENDOTHELIAL DYSFUNCTION with VASOSPASM, capillary leak and activation of coagulation.**
- **The classic renal lesion is GLOMERULAR ENDOTHELIOSIS.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "All true about predictive tests EXCEPT" → **sFlt-1 and s-Eng are decreased** (they are increased); prevention → **aspirin before 16 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *The placenta is starved and floods the blood with anti-VEGF — and aspirin only works if it is started while placentation is still happening.*

## 34. Genital Herpes and Mode of Delivery

**PEARL:** **Caesarean section is indicated ONLY if there are ACTIVE GENITAL LESIONS OR PRODROMAL SYMPTOMS AT THE TIME OF LABOUR** — a history of herpes alone is not an indication. **With no active lesions, VAGINAL DELIVERY is appropriate.** **SUPPRESSIVE ACYCLOVIR from 36 WEEKS** reduces recurrences and the need for caesarean.

**UNDERSTAND:** Neonatal herpes is acquired **during passage through an infected birth canal (85% of cases)** — so the risk is a function of **viral shedding at the moment of delivery**, not of past infection. The risk is far higher with **PRIMARY infection near term (30–50%) than with a RECURRENCE (<1–3%)**, because in primary infection the mother has **no protective transplacental IgG** to pass to the baby, and viral shedding is heavier and longer.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Caesarean should ideally be performed BEFORE or WITHIN 4–6 HOURS OF MEMBRANE RUPTURE — after prolonged rupture, ascending infection has already occurred and the benefit falls.**
- **A PRIMARY episode in the THIRD TRIMESTER (especially within 6 weeks of delivery) is the highest-risk situation and is an indication for CAESAREAN REGARDLESS of visible lesions, plus treatment of both mother and neonate.**
- **NEONATAL HERPES has three forms: SKIN-EYE-MOUTH (best prognosis), CNS (encephalitis, often presenting at 2–3 weeks, high morbidity), and DISSEMINATED (multi-organ, presenting in the first week, highest mortality). Treat with high-dose IV ACYCLOVIR.**
- **Acyclovir is SAFE in pregnancy and lactation.**
- **Herpes lesions on the buttocks or thighs (non-genital) can be covered and vaginal delivery permitted; AVOID FETAL SCALP ELECTRODES and invasive monitoring in any woman with herpes.**
- **Preconception care generally: check RUBELLA immunity and VACCINATE BEFORE CONCEPTION if non-immune (rubella vaccine is LIVE — contraindicated during pregnancy, with conception avoided for 1 month after); start FOLIC ACID 400 µg (5 mg with a previous neural tube defect, diabetes, obesity or antiepileptic use); optimise diabetes, epilepsy and thyroid control; and screen for HIV, syphilis and hepatitis B.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Primary genital herpes → **vaginal delivery if no active lesions at labour**.

**REMEMBER:** *It is the lesion at the time of labour that decides the route, not the history.*

`Q9619, Q9622`

## 35. Types of Abortion and Their Management

**PEARL:** **Bleeding, cramping, an OPEN CERVIX with PRODUCTS VISIBLE IN THE OS = INEVITABLE/INCOMPLETE ABORTION → SURGICAL EVACUATION.** The cervix is the discriminator: **THREATENED — closed os, viable pregnancy (expectant management). INEVITABLE — open os, products still inside. INCOMPLETE — open os, partial expulsion. COMPLETE — closed os, empty uterus. MISSED — closed os, non-viable pregnancy retained (medical management with MISOPROSTOL is appropriate here). SEPTIC — fever, offensive discharge, tenderness (antibiotics plus evacuation).**

**UNDERSTAND:** Once the **cervix is open and products are in the canal**, the pregnancy cannot be saved, and leaving tissue in situ risks **ongoing haemorrhage and ascending infection** — so the aim shifts entirely from preservation to **prompt, complete emptying of the uterus.** Misoprostol has no advantage here because the cervix is already open and expulsion has already begun; it is reserved for the **closed cervix of a missed abortion**, where ripening is the obstacle.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Evacuation is by SUCTION (MANUAL VACUUM ASPIRATION or electric), not sharp curettage — lower risk of perforation and of ASHERMAN'S SYNDROME.**
- **GIVE ANTI-D to every Rh-negative non-sensitised woman after ANY abortion, ectopic or evacuation.**
- **Complications of septic abortion: pelvic sepsis, ENDOTOXIC SHOCK, ACUTE KIDNEY INJURY and renal cortical necrosis, DIC, and *CLOSTRIDIUM PERFRINGENS* infection.**
- **RECURRENT PREGNANCY LOSS (≥2–3 consecutive losses) workup: ANTIPHOSPHOLIPID ANTIBODIES (the only proven treatable cause — aspirin plus heparin), parental KARYOTYPE, uterine anatomy (septum, submucous fibroid, Asherman's), thyroid function and diabetes, and cervical insufficiency.**
- **CERVICAL INSUFFICIENCY presents as PAINLESS mid-trimester dilatation and loss; treatment is CERCLAGE (McDonald or Shirodkar) at 12–14 weeks, or progesterone.**
- **Under the MTP Act (amended 2021), termination is permitted up to 20 weeks on ONE registered practitioner's opinion and up to 24 weeks (for specified categories, including survivors of rape, minors and those with a change of marital status) on TWO opinions; there is NO upper limit for substantial fetal abnormality diagnosed by a Medical Board.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Open os with visible products → **surgical evacuation** (misoprostol is for the closed os of a missed abortion).

**REMEMBER:** *Feel the cervix — open means empty it, closed means decide whether it is alive.*

`Q9642`

## 36. Obstetric Formula — GPLA

**PEARL:** **GRAVIDA counts ALL pregnancies INCLUDING THE CURRENT ONE (and including abortions, ectopics and molar pregnancies). PARA counts deliveries BEYOND THE AGE OF VIABILITY (~20–28 weeks depending on the convention) — a TWIN DELIVERY COUNTS AS ONE PARA. LIVING = children alive now. ABORTIONS = losses before viability.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The commonest errors follow from two rules: **the current pregnancy is included in gravidity but NOT in parity**, and **parity counts DELIVERY EVENTS, not babies** — so a woman with twins delivered once is **P1, L2**. Getting the notation right matters clinically because **parity, not gravidity, predicts the behaviour of labour** (a grand multipara's rapid labour, atony and rupture risk).

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The 4-digit TPAL system is the alternative: TERM deliveries, PRETERM deliveries, ABORTIONS, LIVING children.**
- **PRIMIGRAVIDA — pregnant for the first time. NULLIPARA — never delivered beyond viability. PRIMIPARA — delivered once. MULTIPARA — delivered more than once. GRAND MULTIPARA — five or more deliveries. ELDERLY PRIMIGRAVIDA — first pregnancy at ≥30–35 years.**
- **Each carries distinct risks: PRIMIGRAVIDA — pre-eclampsia, prolonged labour, cephalopelvic disproportion. GRAND MULTIPARA — malpresentation, anaemia, ATONIC PPH, uterine rupture, placenta praevia. ELDERLY PRIMIGRAVIDA — aneuploidy, hypertension, diabetes, higher caesarean rate.**
- **Dating: NAEGELE'S RULE — EDD = LMP + 9 months + 7 days (for a 28-day cycle); adjust for longer or shorter cycles. FIRST-TRIMESTER CROWN–RUMP LENGTH is the most accurate method of dating and should override an uncertain LMP.**
- **Fundal height landmarks: 12 weeks — just above the symphysis; 20 weeks — umbilicus; 36 weeks — xiphisternum; then it falls slightly with engagement ("lightening").**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Read the obstetric history carefully — **include the present pregnancy in G, exclude it from P**.

**REMEMBER:** *Gravida counts pregnancies including this one; para counts deliveries, not babies.*

## 37. Endometrial Histology Through the Cycle

**PEARL:** **SECRETORY (luteal) endometrium — TORTUOUS "SAW-TOOTH"/CORKSCREW GLANDS with subnuclear glycogen vacuoles, stromal oedema and predecidual change — is produced by PROGESTERONE FROM THE CORPUS LUTEUM.** **PROLIFERATIVE (follicular) endometrium — straight tubular glands with pseudostratified, mitotically active epithelium — is produced by OESTROGEN from the GRANULOSA cells.** Progesterone in the follicular phase is **<1 ng/mL**, rising to **>5 ng/mL (peaking ~10–20)** in the luteal phase.

**UNDERSTAND:** The **TWO-CELL, TWO-GONADOTROPHIN model** underlies all of this: **LH drives THECA INTERNA cells to make ANDROGENS; FSH drives GRANULOSA cells to AROMATISE those androgens into OESTROGEN.** After ovulation, LH luteinises the granulosa cells into the **corpus luteum, which secretes PROGESTERONE** — converting a proliferative endometrium into a secretory one ready for implantation. Histology therefore dates the cycle precisely, and **a mid-luteal (day 21) progesterone >5 ng/mL is the standard proof that ovulation occurred.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The corpus luteum survives ~14 days unless rescued by hCG from the implanting embryo — which is why the LUTEAL PHASE IS CONSTANT (14 days) while the FOLLICULAR PHASE VARIES, and why ovulation is dated backwards from the next period, not forwards from the last.**
- **The LUTEAL–PLACENTAL SHIFT occurs at 7–9 weeks — before that, progesterone comes from the corpus luteum (so ovariectomy before 7 weeks causes miscarriage); after that, from the placenta.**
- **Hormone landmarks: oestradiol peaks ~24–36 hours BEFORE the LH surge and triggers it by POSITIVE feedback; OVULATION occurs ~34–36 hours after the LH surge begins and ~10–12 hours after the LH PEAK.**
- **Endometrial layers: the functionalis (supplied by SPIRAL arteries, shed each month) and the basalis (supplied by BASAL/straight arteries, retained and regenerative). Menstruation follows progesterone withdrawal → spiral artery vasoconstriction → ischaemic necrosis and shedding.**
- **Clinically: an ENDOMETRIAL BIOPSY showing a secretory pattern confirms ovulation; a persistently PROLIFERATIVE endometrium in a woman with amenorrhoea/AUB indicates ANOVULATION (unopposed oestrogen — the pathway to hyperplasia and endometrial carcinoma in PCOS and perimenopause).**
- **Progesterone also raises basal body temperature by ~0.3–0.5 °C after ovulation and thickens cervical mucus (losing the oestrogenic FERNING and SPINNBARKEIT).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Corkscrew glands → **progesterone from the corpus luteum**.

**REMEMBER:** *Straight glands are oestrogen; corkscrew glands are the corpus luteum.*

## 38. Endometriosis on Examination

**PEARL:** **NODULARITY and TENDERNESS in the POUCH OF DOUGLAS AND UTEROSACRAL LIGAMENTS on rectovaginal/bimanual examination is most characteristic of ENDOMETRIOSIS**, often with a **FIXED, RETROVERTED uterus.** The classic history is **cyclical, progressively worsening DYSMENORRHOEA, DEEP DYSPAREUNIA, chronic pelvic pain and INFERTILITY.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The distribution follows **SAMPSON'S RETROGRADE MENSTRUATION** hypothesis — refluxed endometrial cells settle where gravity takes them in the supine woman: the **POUCH OF DOUGLAS (commonest site of implants), uterosacral ligaments and OVARIES (the commonest site of endometriomas).** Repeated cyclical bleeding into these implants produces **inflammation, fibrosis and adhesions** — hence the palpable nodules and the fixed, tender, retroverted uterus.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Examination is best performed DURING MENSTRUATION, when nodules are largest and most tender. LAPAROSCOPY IS THE GOLD STANDARD for diagnosis (with biopsy); typical findings are "POWDER-BURN"/gunshot lesions, chocolate cysts and adhesions. CA-125 may be raised but is NON-SPECIFIC and not diagnostic.**
- **There is NO correlation between the extent of disease and the severity of pain** — a small implant on a nerve can be agonising while a large endometrioma is silent.
- **ENDOMETRIOMA ("CHOCOLATE CYST") of the ovary contains altered, tarry blood; on ultrasound it shows homogeneous LOW-LEVEL "GROUND-GLASS" ECHOES. Rupture causes acute peritonitis.**
- **Medical treatment SUPPRESSES but does not cure: COCs (continuous), progestogens, the LNG-IUS, GnRH AGONISTS with add-back therapy, danazol (androgenic side effects) and aromatase inhibitors. NONE improve fertility — for infertility, the treatment is SURGERY (laparoscopic excision/ablation) or ART.**
- **Distinguish ADENOMYOSIS — a diffusely enlarged, boggy, tender uterus with menorrhagia and dysmenorrhoea in a parous woman in her forties, with a thickened JUNCTIONAL ZONE on MRI.**
- **Endometriosis carries a small increased risk of CLEAR CELL and ENDOMETRIOID ovarian carcinoma.**
- **The other causes of a nodular pouch of Douglas — TUBERCULOSIS and disseminated MALIGNANCY — must be considered, especially in India.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Nodularity in the pouch of Douglas → **endometriosis**.

**REMEMBER:** *Retrograde menstruation lands in the pouch of Douglas — feel for the nodules while she is bleeding.*

![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/02/29/screenshot-2024-02-29-164935.jpg)

## 39. Amniotic Fluid Embolism

**PEARL:** **Sudden cardiorespiratory collapse DURING LABOUR or WITHIN 30 MINUTES OF DELIVERY, followed by profuse bleeding with OVERT DIC, and NO FEVER = AMNIOTIC FLUID EMBOLISM.** The triad is **HYPOTENSION/cardiac arrest + HYPOXIA + DIC**. It is a **clinical diagnosis of exclusion** — there is no confirmatory test.

**UNDERSTAND:** It is not really an "embolism" but an **ANAPHYLACTOID SYNDROME OF PREGNANCY** — fetal antigens entering the maternal circulation trigger a massive inflammatory and complement-mediated response. The course is BIPHASIC: an initial phase of **pulmonary vasospasm with acute right heart failure and hypoxia**, then, in survivors, **LEFT ventricular failure and a devastating consumptive coagulopathy** driven by the potent thromboplastin activity of amniotic fluid — which is why DIC is so characteristic and so early.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Risk factors: RAPID/TUMULTUOUS labour, MECONIUM-stained liquor, uterine or cervical tears, operative delivery, placental abruption and praevia, polyhydramnios, advanced maternal age, multiparity and eclampsia.**
- **Management is entirely SUPPORTIVE and simultaneous: high-flow oxygen and early intubation, aggressive circulatory support with vasopressors and inotropes, MASSIVE TRANSFUSION with a 1:1:1 ratio plus cryoprecipitate and fibrinogen for the DIC, and PERIMORTEM CAESAREAN within 4–5 minutes if the mother arrests.** Mortality remains very high, with neurological sequelae common in survivors.
- **The differential is the reason for the diagnostic criteria: PULMONARY EMBOLISM (hypoxia and collapse but NO early DIC), MYOCARDIAL INFARCTION, anaphylaxis, septic shock (FEVER present — hence the criterion of no fever ≥38 °C), eclampsia, high spinal block, and local anaesthetic toxicity.**
- **Compare the other causes of postpartum collapse with bleeding: UTERINE RUPTURE (severe pain, loss of station, fetal bradycardia, scar tenderness), ATONIC PPH (a soft boggy uterus, no early DIC), UTERINE INVERSION (a mass at the introitus, profound vagal shock out of proportion to blood loss), and ACUTE PERIPARTUM CARDIOMYOPATHY (a subacute course, not sudden collapse).**
- **Fetal squames in maternal pulmonary vasculature at autopsy support the diagnosis but are also found in normal pregnancy** — they are not diagnostic.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Collapse plus DIC in the immediate peripartum period → **amniotic fluid embolism**.

**REMEMBER:** *Collapse, hypoxia, and blood that will not clot — within thirty minutes of delivery.*

`Q10106`

---

## 40. Cervical Cerclage

**PEARL:** **RUPTURED MEMBRANES is a contraindication to cerclage.** The full list: **ruptured membranes, INTRAUTERINE INFECTION, active uterine CONTRACTIONS/labour, VAGINAL BLEEDING, CERVICAL DILATATION >4 cm, and fetal death or a lethal fetal anomaly.** **PROLAPSING (bulging) MEMBRANES are NOT an absolute contraindication** — an emergency ("rescue") cerclage may still be attempted.

**UNDERSTAND:** Each contraindication marks a situation in which the stitch **either cannot help or actively harms**. With ruptured membranes or intrauterine infection, a suture **traps infection inside the uterus and converts a preterm delivery into maternal sepsis**. With established contractions or bleeding, the process is already irreversible and the suture will simply **tear through the cervix**. The stitch treats a **mechanical failure of a quiet cervix**, nothing else.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **CERVICAL INSUFFICIENCY: recurrent PAINLESS mid-trimester cervical dilatation with painless expulsion — the absence of pain and contractions is the diagnostic hallmark, distinguishing it from preterm labour.** Risk factors: previous mid-trimester loss, cervical surgery (LEEP, cone biopsy), repeated dilatation, DES exposure and congenital uterine anomalies.
- **Types: ELECTIVE/history-indicated cerclage at 12–14 weeks; ULTRASOUND-INDICATED cerclage for a cervical length <25 mm before 24 weeks with a relevant history; RESCUE/emergency cerclage for dilatation with bulging membranes. McDONALD (a simple purse-string, easily removed) and SHIRODKAR (submucosal, with bladder dissection) are the vaginal techniques; TRANSABDOMINAL cerclage (which mandates caesarean delivery) is reserved for failed vaginal cerclage or an absent/very short cervix.**
- **REMOVE THE STITCH AT 36–37 WEEKS, or immediately with labour, rupture of membranes or infection** — leaving it in during labour causes cervical laceration or uterine rupture.
- **VAGINAL PROGESTERONE is the alternative/adjunct for a short cervix, especially without a history of loss.**
- **Complications: rupture of membranes, infection, cervical laceration, bleeding and preterm labour.**
- **A short cervix on transvaginal ultrasound (<25 mm) is the best predictor of preterm birth; FETAL FIBRONECTIN in cervicovaginal secretions between 22 and 34 weeks has a high NEGATIVE predictive value — a negative test reliably excludes delivery within 2 weeks.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Contraindication for cerclage" → **ruptured membranes**.

**REMEMBER:** *Only stitch a quiet, closed, uninfected cervix with intact membranes.*

`Q10132`

---

## 41. Precocious Puberty

**PEARL:** **PRECOCIOUS PUBERTY in a girl = any secondary sexual characteristic before 8 YEARS; PRECOCIOUS MENSTRUATION = menarche before 10 YEARS.** **McCUNE–ALBRIGHT SYNDROME is the classic peripheral cause — the triad of POLYOSTOTIC FIBROUS DYSPLASIA (with recurrent fractures), "COAST OF MAINE" CAFÉ-AU-LAIT macules, and PRECOCIOUS PUBERTY**, caused by an activating **GNAS mutation.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The GNAS mutation is **post-zygotic and MOSAIC — it constitutively activates the Gs alpha subunit**, so affected tissues behave as if permanently stimulated by their trophic hormone without any hormone being present. That single mechanism explains all three features and predicts the associated endocrinopathies (**hyperthyroidism, acromegaly, Cushing syndrome**). Crucially, it is **GONADOTROPHIN-INDEPENDENT** — LH and FSH are SUPPRESSED — which is what separates peripheral from central precocity.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **CENTRAL (true, GnRH-dependent) precocious puberty: LH and FSH are HIGH/pubertal with a pubertal response to a GnRH stimulation test; puberty is ISOSEXUAL and follows the NORMAL SEQUENCE (thelarche → pubarche → growth spurt → menarche). Most cases in girls are IDIOPATHIC, but MRI BRAIN is mandatory to exclude a hypothalamic HAMARTOMA, glioma, hydrocephalus or previous CNS insult. Treatment is a GnRH AGONIST (continuous, which desensitises the pituitary).**
- **PERIPHERAL (pseudo, GnRH-INDEPENDENT) precocity: LH and FSH SUPPRESSED. Causes — McCune–Albright, ovarian follicular cysts and GRANULOSA CELL TUMOURS, adrenal tumours, congenital adrenal hyperplasia, exogenous oestrogen, and severe primary HYPOTHYROIDISM (the VAN WYK–GRUMBACH syndrome, where very high TSH cross-reacts with the FSH receptor — the one form of precocity with DELAYED, not advanced, bone age).**
- **The main long-term consequence is SHORT FINAL ADULT HEIGHT, from premature epiphyseal fusion despite a tall childhood stature** — which is why treatment is worthwhile.
- **BENIGN VARIANTS need no treatment: isolated PREMATURE THELARCHE (usually under 2 years, non-progressive) and PREMATURE ADRENARCHE (pubic/axillary hair and body odour without breast development).**
- **Normal puberty in girls: THELARCHE (10–11) → PUBARCHE → PEAK GROWTH VELOCITY → MENARCHE (12–13), staged by the TANNER system. In boys the first sign is TESTICULAR ENLARGEMENT (≥4 mL).**
- **DELAYED puberty: no breast development by 13, or no menarche by 15 (or within 3 years of thelarche).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Precocious menstruation is defined as" → **before 10 years**.

**REMEMBER:** *Eight for the breast, ten for the bleed — and check the gonadotrophins to find the level.*

![explanation](https://pub-8c30f7a873214da0837b787a0c529b95.r2.dev/830fc480377504e04be945fa34e5d855.webp)

`Q10499, Q10133`

## 42. Anovulatory Bleeding — Metropathia Haemorrhagica

**PEARL:** **6–8 WEEKS OF AMENORRHOEA followed by PROFUSE, PAINLESS bleeding in a woman of 40–45, with a THICK endometrium (21 mm) = METROPATHIA HAEMORRHAGICA (SCHRÖDER'S DISEASE)** — anovulatory bleeding with **CYSTIC GLANDULAR HYPERPLASIA ("SWISS CHEESE" pattern), myohyperplasia, NO secretory (corkscrew) glands, NO corpus luteum, and often an ovarian follicular cyst.**

**UNDERSTAND:** It is a disease of **UNOPPOSED OESTROGEN**. Persisting follicles secrete oestrogen with **no ovulation and therefore no progesterone**, so the endometrium proliferates unchecked until it **outgrows its blood supply and breaks down irregularly** — producing painless bleeding (no progesterone means no PGF2α, hence **no spasmodic dysmenorrhoea**) after a period of amenorrhoea. The same mechanism explains why anovulatory bleeding is the pathway to **endometrial hyperplasia and carcinoma.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **HALBAN'S DISEASE is the counterpart — IRREGULAR SHEDDING due to a PERSISTENT corpus luteum: REGULAR cycles with PROLONGED bleeding, and secretory endometrium still present on day 5–6.** **IRREGULAR RIPENING is inadequate corpus luteum function with premature bleeding.**
- **The FIRST INVESTIGATION IN ANY WOMAN OF REPRODUCTIVE AGE WITH ABNORMAL BLEEDING IS A URINE β-hCG** — to exclude pregnancy, miscarriage, ectopic and molar pregnancy — before ultrasound, Pap smear or biopsy.
- **Any woman over 40 (or with risk factors — obesity, PCOS, diabetes, tamoxifen, family history) with abnormal uterine bleeding, and EVERY woman with POSTMENOPAUSAL BLEEDING, needs ENDOMETRIAL SAMPLING to exclude hyperplasia and carcinoma.** A postmenopausal endometrial thickness >4–5 mm mandates biopsy.
- **The FIGO "PALM–COEIN" classification of AUB: STRUCTURAL — Polyp, Adenomyosis, Leiomyoma, Malignancy/hyperplasia; NON-STRUCTURAL — Coagulopathy (consider VON WILLEBRAND DISEASE in an adolescent with heavy menses since menarche), Ovulatory dysfunction, Endometrial, Iatrogenic, Not otherwise classified.**
- **ENDOMETRIAL ABLATION destroys the endometrium AND 2–3 mm of superficial myometrium — so FERTILITY IS NOT PRESERVED and contraception is still required; it is for women who have completed childbearing and failed medical therapy.**
- **DILATATION AND CURETTAGE is CONTRAINDICATED in acute PELVIC INFLAMMATORY DISEASE/lower genital tract infection (risk of ascending infection) and in a desired pregnancy;** its complications include perforation, cervical trauma, infection, and ASHERMAN'S SYNDROME — which presents as **HYPOMENORRHOEA or secondary amenorrhoea with infertility after a curettage.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Amenorrhoea then painless flooding at 45 with a thick endometrium → **metropathia haemorrhagica**.

**REMEMBER:** *No ovulation, no progesterone — so the bleeding is heavy, painless, and the glands look like Swiss cheese.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://pub-8c30f7a873214da0837b787a0c529b95.r2.dev/7bc2bfd9e29ec727895e93122c76db63.webp)

`Q10500, Q10501, Q10502, Q10503, Q10507`

## 43. Menstrual Terminology and Dysmenorrhoea

**PEARL:** **POLYMENORRHOEA (epimenorrhoea) — cycles ≤21 days. OLIGOMENORRHOEA — cycles >35–38 days. MENORRHAGIA — regular cycles with excessive (>80 mL) or prolonged (>8 days) flow. METRORRHAGIA — irregular, acyclical bleeding between periods. MENOMETRORRHAGIA — excessive AND prolonged bleeding at irregular, FREQUENT intervals (never "infrequent"). HYPOMENORRHOEA — regular but scanty flow.** **SPASMODIC dysmenorrhoea is caused by increased PGF2α**, which is secreted under the influence of **progesterone — so it occurs ONLY IN OVULATORY CYCLES.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The link between progesterone and PGF2α explains the whole clinical pattern of primary dysmenorrhoea: it **begins 6–12 months after menarche (once cycles become ovulatory), never occurs in anovulatory bleeding, and is relieved by anything that suppresses ovulation (COCs) or blocks prostaglandin synthesis (NSAIDs).** PGF2α causes **myometrial contraction and uterine ischaemia** — the pain is essentially uterine angina.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **PRIMARY dysmenorrhoea: adolescents, pain starting WITH or just BEFORE the flow and lasting 24–72 hours, cramping and suprapubic, with nausea, diarrhoea and headache; pelvic examination is NORMAL. Treat with NSAIDs (mefenamic acid, ibuprofen — most effective if started before the pain) and combined oral contraceptives.**
- **SECONDARY dysmenorrhoea: older women, pain beginning DAYS BEFORE the period and often continuing after it, with dyspareunia and abnormal findings — think ENDOMETRIOSIS, ADENOMYOSIS, fibroids, PID, IUCD, and cervical stenosis.**
- **Causes of menorrhagia: fibroids (especially submucous), adenomyosis, endometrial polyps and hyperplasia, IUCD, HYPOTHYROIDISM, coagulopathy (von Willebrand disease), and anovulation.**
- **Causes of hypomenorrhoea: ASHERMAN'S SYNDROME (after curettage), oral contraceptives, the perimenopause, and endometrial tuberculosis.**
- **MENOMETRORRHAGIA classically raises CERVICAL CARCINOMA and GRANULOSA CELL TUMOUR (an oestrogen-secreting ovarian tumour with CALL–EXNER bodies and raised INHIBIN).**
- **Assess menstrual loss objectively with a PICTORIAL BLOOD ASSESSMENT CHART and by measuring HAEMOGLOBIN AND FERRITIN — patient estimates are unreliable.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Pure definition-matching; **"not a feature of menometrorrhagia" → infrequent bleeding.**

**REMEMBER:** *Progesterone makes PGF2α, PGF2α makes cramps — so anovulatory bleeding never hurts.*

`Q10504, Q10505, Q10506`

---

## 44. Genitourinary Fistulae

**PEARL:** In developing countries the commonest cause is **PROLONGED OBSTRUCTED LABOUR (pressure necrosis)**; in developed countries it is **IATROGENIC INJURY AT HYSTERECTOMY (~90% of vesicovaginal fistulae).** **HYSTEROSCOPY does NOT cause them.** **URETEROVAGINAL, VESICOVAGINAL, VESICOUTERINE and URETHROVAGINAL fistulae all occur; a UTEROURETHRAL fistula has never been reported.** Repair is best **6 WEEKS after the inciting event** if there is significant inflammation (or within 24–48 hours if recognised immediately and the tissues are clean); a **failed repair is re-attempted only after 3 MONTHS.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The waiting period is about **tissue quality, not urgency**. Operating into oedematous, infected, devascularised tissue guarantees failure — so time is allowed for **inflammation to settle, infection to clear and neovascularisation to occur.** The corollary is equally important: **the FIRST repair has the best chance of success**, which is why an unhurried, well-planned operation by an experienced surgeon matters more than speed.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **VESICOUTERINE FISTULA follows CAESAREAN SECTION, uterine rupture or placenta accreta and produces YOUSSEF SYNDROME — the triad of AMENORRHOEA, CYCLICAL HAEMATURIA (MENOURIA) and URINARY CONTINENCE (no vaginal leak, because the cervix acts as a valve).** Many close with **prolonged bladder catheterisation**; otherwise repair is abdominal.
- **Distinguishing the leak clinically: VESICOVAGINAL — continuous leak, bladder empty. URETEROVAGINAL — continuous leak WITH normal voiding preserved. URETHROVAGINAL — leak only during voiding. The THREE-SWAB (dye) TEST localises the level, and cystoscopy with retrograde studies confirms it.**
- **Obstetric fistulae are accompanied by the wider "OBSTRUCTED LABOUR INJURY COMPLEX" — foot drop (lumbosacral trunk injury), vaginal stenosis, secondary infertility, amenorrhoea and profound social ostracism.** **Prevention is timely obstetric care — the partograph.**
- **Principles of repair: adequate exposure, wide mobilisation, TENSION-FREE closure in layers without overlapping suture lines, interposition of healthy tissue (MARTIUS fat pad, omentum), and PROLONGED POSTOPERATIVE BLADDER DRAINAGE (10–14 days) — the single most important postoperative factor.**
- **Preoperative preparation: treat infection, give oestrogen in postmenopausal women to improve tissue quality, and correct anaemia and nutrition.**
- **Radiation-induced fistulae appear late (months to years), are ischaemic and heal poorly — they usually need tissue interposition or urinary diversion.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Does not cause fistula" → **hysteroscopy**; cyclical haematuria with a catheter emerging from the os → **vesicouterine fistula**.

**REMEMBER:** *Wait six weeks for clean tissue — and the first repair is the best chance you will get.*

## 45. Identifying the Ureter at Surgery

**PEARL:** The ureter is recognised by its **PALE, WHITISH, GLISTENING appearance, its CORD-LIKE, NON-PULSATILE feel, and — the definitive sign — VERMICULAR PERISTALSIS when gently pinched.** The **UTERINE ARTERY CROSSES SUPERIOR (anterior) to it about 2 cm lateral to the cervix — "WATER UNDER THE BRIDGE"** — and the **OVARIAN vessels cross anterior to it at the pelvic brim.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The peristalsis test is decisive because **arteries pulsate rhythmically and passively, whereas only the ureter "crawls" in a slow worm-like wave when stimulated.** Anatomically, the ureter's danger is that it runs **retroperitoneally along the pelvic side wall, then medially in the base of the broad ligament DIRECTLY BENEATH the uterine artery, and then close to the vaginal fornix** — so it is at risk precisely where the gynaecologist is working.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The five classic sites of ureteric injury in gynaecological surgery: (1) at the PELVIC BRIM near the infundibulopelvic ligament (ligating ovarian vessels); (2) in the OVARIAN FOSSA; (3) where the uterine artery crosses it (the commonest site, during clamping of the cardinal ligament); (4) at the UTEROSACRAL ligament; (5) near the BLADDER/vaginal angle.**
- **The ureter is ~25 cm long with THREE physiological constrictions — the pelvi-ureteric junction, the crossing of the iliac vessels at the pelvic brim, and the vesico-ureteric junction (the narrowest).**
- **Prevention: identify the ureter early, stay close to the uterus when clamping, avoid blind clamping in bleeding fields, and consider preoperative stenting in difficult cases (endometriosis, large fibroids, malignancy, previous surgery, frozen pelvis).**
- **Presentation of missed injury: flank pain and fever, ILEUS, a urinoma, ascites and a rising creatinine, or a watery vaginal discharge (ureterovaginal fistula) days later. Diagnosis is by CT urography or retrograde pyelography.**
- **Management depends on the level: LOWER-third injuries → URETERONEOCYSTOSTOMY (with a Boari flap or psoas hitch if needed); MID/UPPER-third → ureteroureterostomy over a stent.**
- **BLADDER injury recognised at operation is repaired in layers immediately with prolonged catheter drainage — recognition at the time of surgery is what determines outcome for both organs.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Structure identification question → **glistening, peristalses on stimulation, uterine artery crosses above it**.

**REMEMBER:** *Arteries pulse; only the ureter crawls — and the water always runs under the bridge.*

![explanation](https://pub-8c30f7a873214da0837b787a0c529b95.r2.dev/71feeaa6744c458d10707e5175ac6814.webp)

## 46. Uteroplacental Circulation

**PEARL:** **Intervillous space blood flow at term is 500–600 mL/min** (compared with a NON-PREGNANT uterine blood flow of only **~50 mL/min**). The **mature placenta holds ~500 mL of blood, of which ~150 mL is in the intervillous space** — and this is why the uterus becomes the single greatest source of catastrophic haemorrhage in obstetrics.

**UNDERSTAND:** The intervillous space is a **low-resistance, high-flow sinusoid** created by **trophoblastic destruction of the spiral artery musculature**, which converts them into **flaccid, non-contractile channels unresponsive to vasoconstrictors.** That is precisely why **postpartum haemostasis depends entirely on MYOMETRIAL CONTRACTION ("LIVING LIGATURES" of the criss-crossing muscle fibres) rather than on vascular spasm** — and why an atonic uterus can lose a litre of blood per minute.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Uterine blood flow at term is ~500–750 mL/min, about 10–15% of maternal cardiac output. It is NOT AUTOREGULATED — it varies directly with maternal perfusion pressure, so maternal hypotension immediately becomes fetal hypoxia.** This is the physiological basis of aortocaval compression, of careful spinal anaesthesia management, and of avoiding overzealous antihypertensive treatment in pre-eclampsia.
- **Failure of the SECOND WAVE of trophoblastic invasion (16–20 weeks) leaves the spiral arteries narrow and responsive — the primary lesion of PRE-ECLAMPSIA and of fetal growth restriction.**
- **Placental transfer: SIMPLE DIFFUSION — oxygen, carbon dioxide, water, most electrolytes and lipid-soluble drugs. FACILITATED DIFFUSION — GLUCOSE. ACTIVE TRANSPORT — amino acids, calcium, iron, water-soluble vitamins. PINOCYTOSIS — IgG (the only immunoglobulin to cross) and other large proteins. INSULIN, HEPARIN and IgM DO NOT CROSS.**
- **The placenta at term weighs ~500 g, with a fetal:placental weight ratio of about 6:1.**
- **Fetal circulatory shunts: DUCTUS VENOSUS (umbilical vein to IVC, bypassing the liver), FORAMEN OVALE (right to left atrium), DUCTUS ARTERIOSUS (pulmonary artery to aorta) — the best-oxygenated blood is directed preferentially to the brain and heart.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight number recall — **500–600 mL/min**.

**REMEMBER:** *Half a litre a minute through vessels that cannot constrict — only the muscle can stop it.*

`Q11066`

## 47. Shoulder Dystocia

**PEARL:** **Shoulder dystocia is a HEAD-TO-BODY DELIVERY TIME >60 SECONDS** (the normal mean is ~24 seconds), or the failure of gentle downward traction to deliver the shoulders. The sign is the **"TURTLE SIGN"** — the head delivering and then retracting against the perineum.

**UNDERSTAND:** The anterior shoulder is **impacted behind the pubic symphysis** because the shoulders have failed to rotate into the oblique diameter. Every effective manoeuvre works by either **increasing the functional pelvic diameter (McRoberts, delivery on all fours)** or **rotating the shoulders out of the anteroposterior plane (Rubin, Woods screw)**. Nothing is gained by pulling harder — traction on the head is what produces **BRACHIAL PLEXUS INJURY**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The drill (HELPERR): call for Help; Evaluate for Episiotomy; LEGS — McROBERTS MANOEUVRE (hyperflexion and abduction of the maternal hips, which rotates the symphysis cephalad and flattens the sacral promontory — the SINGLE MOST EFFECTIVE first step); SUPRAPUBIC PRESSURE (never FUNDAL pressure, which worsens impaction and risks uterine rupture); Enter for internal rotational manoeuvres (RUBIN II, WOODS CORKSCREW, reverse Woods); Remove the posterior arm; Roll the patient onto all fours (GASKIN manoeuvre).**
- **Last-resort measures: ZAVANELLI manoeuvre (cephalic replacement then caesarean), symphysiotomy, and deliberate CLAVICULAR FRACTURE.**
- **AVOID the "three Ps": Panic, PULLING (excessive traction), PUSHING (fundal pressure) — and Pivoting (sharply angulating the head).**
- **Complications: FETAL — brachial plexus injury (ERB'S PALSY, C5–C6, "waiter's tip" — most resolve; KLUMPKE'S is rarer), clavicular and humeral fracture, hypoxic injury and death. MATERNAL — postpartum haemorrhage (the commonest), third/fourth-degree tears, symphyseal separation and uterine rupture.**
- **Risk factors: MACROSOMIA (especially diabetic macrosomia, which is truncal), maternal diabetes and obesity, previous shoulder dystocia, prolonged second stage and instrumental delivery — but MOST CASES OCCUR IN BABIES OF NORMAL WEIGHT WITH NO RISK FACTORS, so it is largely UNPREDICTABLE and UNPREVENTABLE.**
- **JOHNSON'S FORMULA estimates fetal weight clinically: weight (g) = (fundal height in cm − 12) × 155 if the head is AT OR ABOVE the ischial spines, and (fundal height − 11) × 155 if BELOW.** (So a fundal height of 35 cm at station −2 gives (35−12) × 155 ≈ 3.5 kg.)

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Head-to-body delivery time defining shoulder dystocia" → **>60 seconds**; Johnson's formula is pure calculation.

**REMEMBER:** *McRoberts and suprapubic pressure first — never fundal pressure, never harder traction.*

`Q11067, Q11068`

## 48. Deep Transverse Arrest

**PEARL:** **DEEP TRANSVERSE ARREST — the head deeply engaged with the SAGITTAL SUTURE lying in the TRANSVERSE (bispinous) diameter and no descent for ½–1 hour after full dilatation.** Causes: an **ANDROID or ANTHROPOID pelvis (prominent ischial spines, flat sacrum, convergent side walls), DEFLEXION of the head, UTERINE INERTIA, and a lax pelvic floor from EPIDURAL ANALGESIA.** **A TRANSVERSE LIE cannot cause it** — the presenting part is the shoulder, so there is no head in the pelvis to arrest.

**UNDERSTAND:** Internal rotation from the transverse to the anteroposterior diameter requires **three things: a well-flexed head, effective contractions, and a resilient pelvic floor to provide the rotational "gutter".** Deep transverse arrest occurs when **any one of the three fails** — which is exactly why the cause list mixes bony (android pelvis), dynamic (inertia) and soft-tissue (epidural) factors. The prominent ischial spines of the android pelvis physically block rotation.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Diagnosis: the head is engaged, the sagittal suture lies transversely, BOTH fontanelles are palpable at the same level (or the anterior fontanelle is easily felt because of deflexion), and caput and moulding are present.**
- **Management: if vaginal delivery is safe — MANUAL ROTATION followed by forceps, ROTATIONAL FORCEPS (KIELLAND'S), or VENTOUSE (which allows auto-rotation — but excessive traction must be avoided). If the pelvis is inadequate or the baby large — CAESAREAN SECTION.**
- **The four pelvic types (CALDWELL–MOLOY): GYNECOID (~50%, the ideal round pelvis, best for delivery), ANDROID (heart-shaped, narrow forepelvis, convergent side walls, prominent spines, NARROW subpubic arch — the worst for labour, causing deep transverse arrest and occipitoposterior arrest), ANTHROPOID (oval anteroposteriorly, favours DIRECT OCCIPITOPOSTERIOR delivery), PLATYPELLOID (flat, wide transverse, favours transverse arrest at the brim).**
- **Paradoxically, the ANDROID pelvis with its NARROW pubic arch is PROTECTIVE against childbirth pelvic floor trauma and later prolapse/incontinence, while the roomy GYNECOID pelvis permits the descent that stretches and damages the pelvic floor.**
- **OCCIPITOPOSTERIOR position causes a long, painful labour with early backache and a desire to push before full dilatation; most rotate spontaneously to occipitoanterior.**
- **Distinguish the arrest disorders: at the BRIM (non-engagement, cephalopelvic disproportion), in the CAVITY (deep transverse arrest), and at the OUTLET (narrow subpubic angle).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Deep transverse arrest seen in all EXCEPT" → **transverse lie**.

**REMEMBER:** *Rotation needs a flexed head, strong contractions and a firm pelvic floor — lose any one and the head sticks sideways.*

`Q11070, Q11077`

## 49. Gartner's Cyst versus Cystocele

**PEARL:** **GARTNER'S CYST is a retention cyst of the WOLFFIAN (mesonephric) duct remnant**, lying in the **ANTERIOR or anterolateral vaginal wall**. It is **NOT REDUCIBLE, has NO COUGH IMPULSE, has WELL-DEFINED margins, and the OVERLYING VAGINAL MUCOSA IS TENSE, SHINY AND HAS LOST ITS RUGAE.** A **CYSTOCELE is REDUCIBLE, HAS a cough impulse, has ill-defined margins, and RETAINS the vaginal rugae.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The distinction is between **a discrete cyst within the vaginal wall and a herniation of the bladder behind an intact vaginal wall.** Because the cystocele is simply the vaginal wall being pushed backwards, **its mucosa remains normal, rugose and mobile**, and it behaves like any hernia — reducible, with an impulse on coughing. The Gartner's cyst **stretches and thins the mucosa over it**, obliterating the rugae. The clinching test: **a metal catheter passed per urethra can be felt beneath the vaginal wall in a cystocele but NOT under a Gartner's cyst.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Gartner's duct runs alongside the vagina; cysts are usually small and asymptomatic, found incidentally, and are EXCISED only if large or symptomatic. Large ones may be associated with ipsilateral RENAL ANOMALIES (the mesonephric duct also forms the ureteric bud) — image the urinary tract.**
- **The vaginal wall lump differential: Gartner's cyst (anterolateral, Wolffian), inclusion/implantation dermoid cyst (posterior wall, at an old episiotomy or tear site), BARTHOLIN'S CYST (posterolateral at the introitus, at 4 and 8 o'clock — treated by MARSUPIALISATION, and biopsied in women over 40 to exclude carcinoma), SKENE'S duct cyst (periurethral), URETHRAL DIVERTICULUM (a tender anterior mass with post-void dribbling and purulent discharge on compression), and a urethrocele or cystocele.**
- **Anterior compartment prolapse: CYSTOCELE (upper anterior wall, bladder) and URETHROCELE (lower anterior wall). Posterior: RECTOCELE and ENTEROCELE (which contains small bowel and is the only one that may transilluminate/gurgle).**
- **MÜLLERIAN (paramesonephric) remnants form the fallopian tubes, uterus, cervix and upper vagina; WOLFFIAN remnants persist as Gartner's duct, the epoophoron and paroophoron.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Differentiated from cystocele by all EXCEPT" → **presence of rugae** (they are LOST in Gartner's cyst).

**REMEMBER:** *A cystocele reduces and keeps its rugae; a Gartner's cyst does neither.*

![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/6acbb8557a68426e82495020892da39bx346x394.PNG)

## 50. Müllerian Duct Anomalies (AFS Classification)

**PEARL:** **AFS/ASRM classes: I — MÜLLERIAN AGENESIS/hypoplasia (MRKH). II — UNICORNUATE. III — DIDELPHYS. IV — BICORNUATE. V — SEPTATE. VI — ARCUATE (a flat-topped fundus with loss of the fundal bulge — the mildest, and essentially a normal variant). VII — DES-RELATED (T-shaped uterus).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The classification follows **embryology in sequence: failure of DEVELOPMENT (I, II), failure of FUSION of the two Müllerian ducts (III, IV), and failure of RESORPTION of the intervening septum (V, VI).** That is why the crucial surgical distinction is between **SEPTATE (a normal external fundal contour with an internal septum — a RESORPTION defect, correctable by simple HYSTEROSCOPIC septal resection) and BICORNUATE (an INDENTED external fundal contour — a FUSION defect, requiring abdominal metroplasty if anything).** **MRI or 3D ultrasound assesses the external contour and settles it.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The SEPTATE uterus has the WORST reproductive outcome — recurrent first-trimester miscarriage, because the septum is poorly vascularised and cannot support implantation — and it is the ONE anomaly with a clearly effective, low-risk treatment.**
- **UNICORNUATE uterus with a non-communicating rudimentary horn causes CYCLICAL PAIN, HAEMATOMETRA and a risk of ECTOPIC PREGNANCY IN THE HORN WITH RUPTURE — the horn should be excised.**
- **Müllerian anomalies are strongly associated with RENAL TRACT ANOMALIES (unilateral renal agenesis in up to 30–40%) — always image the kidneys.**
- **MRKH (class I): 46,XX, absent uterus and upper two-thirds of the vagina with a blind vaginal pouch, NORMAL OVARIES (so normal breasts, normal pubic and axillary hair and normal testosterone), presenting as primary amenorrhoea. Treatment is vaginal dilatation or vaginoplasty; fertility requires surrogacy or uterine transplantation.** Contrast **ANDROGEN INSENSITIVITY (46,XY, absent/scanty pubic and axillary hair, male-range testosterone, testes requiring removal after puberty).**
- **OBSTRUCTIVE anomalies (imperforate hymen, transverse vaginal septum, OHVIRA/Herlyn–Werner–Wunderlich syndrome) present with CYCLICAL PAIN and a HAEMATOCOLPOS — a bluish bulging membrane at the introitus in the classic imperforate hymen; treat with cruciate incision.**
- **Obstetric consequences generally: recurrent miscarriage, preterm birth, malpresentation (especially breech and transverse lie), and a higher caesarean rate.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Flat-topped fundus without a fundal bulge → **arcuate uterus, class VI**.

**REMEMBER:** *Development, fusion, resorption — and only the septum is worth resecting.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://cdn1.dailyrounds.org/uploads/4ae41333268f43d9820ac1326d7c9965.PNG)

## 51. Female Genital Mutilation

**PEARL:** **WHO TYPE III = INFIBULATION** — partial or total excision of the labia minora and/or majora, with or without clitoridectomy, followed by **FUSION/narrowing of the vaginal opening.** **Type I — prepuce excision ± clitoridectomy. Type II — clitoral excision ± partial/total labia minora excision. Type IV — all other harmful procedures (pricking, piercing, incising, scraping, cauterisation, introduction of corrosive substances).**

**UNDERSTAND:** Type III is by far the most damaging because it does not merely remove tissue but **creates a physical seal**, so its complications are obstructive as well as ablative: **haematocolpos, urinary retention, recurrent infection, and — obstetrically — obstructed second stage requiring DEFIBULATION.** Grading is therefore not academic; it predicts exactly what will be needed in labour.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Immediate complications: haemorrhage, severe pain and shock, urinary retention, infection, TETANUS, HIV and hepatitis transmission from shared instruments, and death.**
- **Long-term: chronic pain and dyspareunia, KELOID and DERMOID INCLUSION CYSTS, urinary and menstrual obstruction, recurrent UTI, infertility, sexual dysfunction, and psychological trauma including PTSD and depression.**
- **Obstetric: prolonged and obstructed second stage, extensive perineal tears, PPH, fistula formation, higher caesarean rates and increased perinatal death. DEFIBULATION (anterior midline incision) is performed ideally ANTENATALLY in the second trimester, or in labour; RE-INFIBULATION AFTER DELIVERY IS UNETHICAL AND ILLEGAL.**
- **It has NO health benefits, is a violation of human rights, is usually performed on girls between infancy and 15 years, and is illegal in most countries. Health workers must never perform it ("medicalisation" is explicitly condemned by WHO).**
- **The term "female circumcision" is inaccurate and should be avoided** — the anatomy removed is not analogous to the male prepuce in the deforming types.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Infibulation is which type" → **Type III**.

**REMEMBER:** *Type three seals the opening — that is what makes it the one that obstructs labour.*

`Q11073`

---

## 52. Fetal Skull Diameters and Presentation

**PEARL:** **Palpating the ANTERIOR FONTANELLE AND THE SUPRAORBITAL RIDGES means a BROW PRESENTATION — engaging diameter MENTOVERTICAL (13.5–14 cm), the LARGEST diameter, which CANNOT deliver vaginally at term.** The key diameters: **SUBOCCIPITOBREGMATIC 9.5 cm (well-flexed VERTEX — the smallest and ideal); OCCIPITOFRONTAL 11.5 cm (deflexed/occipitoposterior); MENTOVERTICAL 13.5–14 cm (BROW); SUBMENTOBREGMATIC 9.5 cm (complete FACE presentation — which CAN deliver vaginally if MENTO-ANTERIOR).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The whole logic of labour mechanics is **presenting the SMALLEST possible diameter to the pelvis**. Flexion gives 9.5 cm and delivers easily; **complete extension (face) also gives 9.5 cm and can deliver; but the INTERMEDIATE, half-extended position (brow) presents the longest diameter of the skull and is therefore the ONE THAT CANNOT DELIVER.** Brow is unstable — it usually converts either to a vertex (by flexion) or to a face (by further extension).

**MUST KNOW:**
- **BROW PRESENTATION at term with a persistent brow = CAESAREAN SECTION. FACE PRESENTATION: MENTO-ANTERIOR can deliver vaginally by flexion; MENTO-POSTERIOR CANNOT (the neck would have to extend further, which is impossible) and requires caesarean.** In face presentation the **facial oedema and bruising are alarming but resolve**; avoid vacuum extraction.
- **Identifying the presentation on vaginal examination: VERTEX — posterior fontanelle (triangular, three sutures) easily felt with a flexed head; BROW — anterior fontanelle (diamond-shaped, four sutures) PLUS supraorbital ridges and the root of the nose; FACE — mouth, nose, malar eminences and orbital ridges (the mouth and the two malar prominences form a TRIANGLE, distinguishing it from the anus and ischial tuberosities of a breech, which lie in a STRAIGHT LINE).**
- **Causes of deflexed attitudes: contracted pelvis, prematurity, polyhydramnios, multiparity with a lax abdominal wall, congenital anomalies (ANENCEPHALY classically causes face presentation), fetal neck masses, and cord round the neck.**
- **ENGAGEMENT means the widest diameter (BIPARIETAL, 9.5 cm) has passed the pelvic brim — clinically, when only 2/5 or less of the head is palpable abdominally.**
- **The mechanism of normal labour: engagement, DESCENT, FLEXION, INTERNAL ROTATION, EXTENSION (delivery of the head), RESTITUTION, EXTERNAL ROTATION, and delivery of the shoulders and body.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Anterior fontanelle + supraorbital ridge → **brow → mentovertical diameter**.

**REMEMBER:** *Fully flexed or fully extended will deliver; half-way is a brow and will not.*
