## 53. Zygosity and Twin Placentation

**PEARL:** **DNA FINGERPRINTING is the ABSOLUTE PROOF of MONOZYGOSITY.** Other pointers: **DIFFERENT SEXES prove DIZYGOSITY; MONOCHORIONICITY (one placenta with communicating vessels and only 2 intervening amniotic layers) proves MONOZYGOSITY; and RECIPROCAL SKIN GRAFTS are ACCEPTED between monozygotic and REJECTED between dizygotic twins** (the classical, historical test).

**UNDERSTAND:** Only DNA identity is definitive because **all the other markers can mislead in one direction or the other**: monozygotic twins may be **DICHORIONIC** (if the split occurs within 3 days), so two placentas do NOT exclude monozygosity; and same-sex dizygotic twins with fused placentas can look monochorionic on gross inspection. **Skin grafting works because rejection depends on HLA identity** — which is exactly what DNA fingerprinting measures directly and non-invasively.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Monozygotic twinning occurs at a CONSTANT rate worldwide (~1 in 250) and is largely INDEPENDENT of race, age, parity and heredity — whereas DIZYGOTIC twinning VARIES with RACE (highest in African populations, lowest in Asian), INCREASING MATERNAL AGE AND PARITY, FAMILY HISTORY ON THE MOTHER'S SIDE, and — dominantly today — OVULATION INDUCTION AND ART.**
- **The rise in twin rates is almost entirely due to assisted reproduction; DIZYGOTIC twins account for about two-thirds of all twins.**
- **Superfetation (fertilisation of a second ovum in a later cycle) and SUPERFECUNDATION (two ova of the same cycle fertilised by sperm from different acts of coitus, potentially different fathers) are the classical rarities.**
- **All twin pregnancies need: earlier and more frequent scans, iron and folate supplementation, surveillance for pre-eclampsia and growth restriction, and anticipation of PRETERM LABOUR, MALPRESENTATION and POSTPARTUM HAEMORRHAGE (an overdistended uterus).**
- **Mode of delivery: if the FIRST twin is CEPHALIC, vaginal delivery may be attempted regardless of the second twin's presentation (with facilities for internal podalic version and breech extraction); if the first twin is NON-CEPHALIC, deliver by caesarean. Monoamniotic twins and conjoined twins always need caesarean.**
- **LOCKED TWINS is the rare catastrophe when the first twin is breech and the second cephalic — the chins interlock.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Absolute proof of monozygosity" → **DNA fingerprinting**.

**REMEMBER:** *Different sexes rule monozygosity out; only identical DNA rules it in.*

`Q11078`

---

## 54. Vulvar Malignancy

**PEARL:** **SQUAMOUS CELL CARCINOMA accounts for ~90% of vulval cancers**, followed by **MELANOMA (~5%, the second commonest), ADENOCARCINOMA (Bartholin's gland), BASAL CELL CARCINOMA, and sarcoma.** **CHORIOCARCINOMA is NOT a primary vulval tumour.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The vulva is **keratinised, hair-bearing skin**, so its tumours are **skin tumours** — which is why the histological list mirrors that of skin (squamous, melanoma, basal cell) rather than that of the cervix or endometrium. Its **two distinct pathways to squamous cancer** follow the same logic: an **HPV-driven pathway in younger women (usual-type VIN, HPV 16)** and an **HPV-INDEPENDENT pathway in older women arising in LICHEN SCLEROSUS with p53 mutation (differentiated VIN)** — the latter is more aggressive.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **It typically presents late, in a postmenopausal woman, with long-standing PRURITUS and a lump or ulcer, most often on the LABIUM MAJUS. Any persistent vulval lesion or non-healing ulcer must be BIOPSIED — delay is the rule and the reason for poor outcomes.**
- **Spread is predominantly LYMPHATIC, to the SUPERFICIAL then DEEP INGUINOFEMORAL nodes and then to the pelvic nodes. NODAL STATUS IS THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT PROGNOSTIC FACTOR.** Staging is **SURGICAL (FIGO)**.
- **Treatment: WIDE LOCAL EXCISION with a ≥1 cm margin for early disease; RADICAL VULVECTOMY WITH BILATERAL INGUINOFEMORAL LYMPHADENECTOMY for larger tumours; SENTINEL NODE BIOPSY is now standard for unifocal tumours <4 cm with clinically negative nodes, sparing the LYMPHOEDEMA and wound breakdown of full node dissection. Advanced disease is treated with chemoradiation.**
- **Lesions with STROMAL INVASION ≤1 mm (stage IA) do NOT require groin node dissection.**
- **LICHEN SCLEROSUS — porcelain-white atrophic "cigarette-paper" skin with a figure-of-eight distribution, intense pruritus and loss of architecture — is treated with POTENT TOPICAL STEROIDS (clobetasol) and needs long-term follow-up because of the ~5% malignancy risk.**
- **PAGET'S DISEASE OF THE VULVA presents as a red, eczematous, weeping plaque and is associated with underlying adenocarcinoma in a minority — biopsy is essential.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Common primary vulval tumours EXCEPT" → **choriocarcinoma**.

**REMEMBER:** *The vulva is skin — so its cancers are skin cancers, and squamous wins nine times out of ten.*

`Q11075`

---

## 55. Menopause

**PEARL:** **MENOPAUSE = 12 CONSECUTIVE MONTHS of amenorrhoea after the permanent cessation of ovarian function — a RETROSPECTIVE, clinical diagnosis** requiring no hormone assay. Average age ~50 years (45–55). **PREMATURE OVARIAN INSUFFICIENCY = before 40 years.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The 12-month rule exists because **the perimenopause is characterised by erratic, unpredictable ovarian activity** — cycles may cease for months and then resume, and an FSH level can be menopausal one month and normal the next. Only sustained amenorrhoea proves that **follicular depletion is complete.** This also has a practical corollary: **contraception must continue for 12 months after the last period if over 50, and for 2 YEARS if under 50** — because sporadic ovulation still occurs.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Endocrinology: FSH RISES FIRST and highest (loss of INHIBIN B feedback from the granulosa cells), then LH; oestradiol falls; the dominant post-menopausal oestrogen becomes OESTRONE, produced by peripheral AROMATISATION of adrenal androstenedione in ADIPOSE TISSUE — which is why obese women have fewer vasomotor symptoms but a HIGHER risk of ENDOMETRIAL CARCINOMA. ANTI-MÜLLERIAN HORMONE is the best marker of ovarian reserve.**
- **Symptoms: VASOMOTOR (hot flushes and night sweats — the commonest, mediated by KNDy neurons in the hypothalamus), GENITOURINARY SYNDROME OF MENOPAUSE (vaginal dryness, dyspareunia, urgency, recurrent UTI — this one PROGRESSES and does not resolve), sleep disturbance, mood change, and loss of libido.**
- **Long-term consequences: OSTEOPOROSIS (the most rapid bone loss occurs in the first 5–10 years) and increased cardiovascular risk.**
- **HRT: oestrogen alone ONLY if the uterus has been removed; oestrogen PLUS PROGESTOGEN if the uterus is present (unopposed oestrogen causes endometrial hyperplasia and carcinoma). Benefits — best relief of vasomotor symptoms, prevention of osteoporotic fracture. Risks — VTE (lower with TRANSDERMAL preparations), stroke, breast cancer with prolonged combined use, and gallbladder disease.** **The "TIMING HYPOTHESIS": HRT started within 10 years of menopause or before age 60 has a favourable risk–benefit balance.**
- **CONTRAINDICATIONS to HRT: oestrogen-dependent cancer (breast, endometrium), undiagnosed vaginal bleeding, active VTE or thrombophilia, active liver disease, and coronary or cerebrovascular disease.**
- **Non-hormonal options: SSRIs/SNRIs, gabapentin, clonidine, and — for genitourinary symptoms — LOW-DOSE VAGINAL OESTROGEN (minimal systemic absorption, so usable when systemic HRT is not).**
- **ANY POSTMENOPAUSAL BLEEDING IS ENDOMETRIAL CANCER UNTIL PROVED OTHERWISE.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Menopause is defined as" → **12 months of amenorrhoea**.

**REMEMBER:** *Twelve silent months, looking backwards — and FSH rises first because inhibin has gone.*

`Q10116`

---

## 56. Rectal Examination in Gynaecology

**PEARL:** Rectal examination is indicated in **CHILDREN and VIRGINS, when vaginal examination is painful or impossible, in VAGINAL AGENESIS/atresia, to assess PARAMETRIAL and uterosacral involvement in CARCINOMA CERVIX (the base of the broad ligament and uterosacral ligaments can ONLY be felt rectally), to corroborate pouch-of-Douglas findings, and to DIFFERENTIATE RECTOCELE FROM ENTEROCELE.** **It has NO role in STAGING OVARIAN MALIGNANCY** (which is staged SURGICALLY).

**UNDERSTAND:** The rectal finger reaches **posteriorly and laterally where the vaginal finger cannot** — the uterosacral ligaments, the parametrium out to the pelvic side wall, and the rectovaginal septum. That is precisely why **cervical cancer, which spreads laterally through the parametrium, is STAGED CLINICALLY with a rectal examination**, while ovarian cancer, which spreads transcoelomically throughout the abdomen, cannot be staged by any bedside examination at all.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Rectovaginal examination distinguishes RECTOCELE (the finger enters the bulge from the rectum) from ENTEROCELE (the finger feels bowel between the rectal and vaginal fingers, above the rectocele) — the classic bedside discrimination.**
- **CERVICAL CANCER IS STAGED CLINICALLY (FIGO), and the rectal examination determining parametrial extension is what separates stage IIB from IB. OVARIAN AND ENDOMETRIAL CANCERS ARE STAGED SURGICALLY.**
- **Rectal examination also detects: uterosacral NODULARITY of endometriosis, rectal involvement by tumour, haemorrhoids and fissures, and a rectovaginal septal mass.**
- **In children, examination is done under anaesthesia when needed; a hymen-sparing approach is essential.**
- **Bimanual examination assesses uterine size, position, mobility and tenderness, and adnexal masses; CERVICAL MOTION TENDERNESS ("chandelier sign") suggests PID, ectopic pregnancy or peritonitis.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Indications of rectal examination EXCEPT" → **staging of ovarian malignancy**.

**REMEMBER:** *Only the rectal finger reaches the parametrium — which is why cervical cancer is staged with it and ovarian cancer is not.*

`Q11080`

---

## 57. Neonatal Hypoglycaemia

**PEARL:** **Neonatal hypoglycaemia is a blood glucose <45 mg/dL** (some units use <40 in the first hours). The **infant of a diabetic mother is the classic case** — maternal hyperglycaemia drives fetal hyperinsulinaemia (PEDERSEN'S HYPOTHESIS), and when the placental glucose supply is cut at delivery, the **persisting hyperinsulinaemia causes a precipitous fall within 1–2 hours of birth.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The danger is that **the neonatal brain depends almost entirely on glucose, and hyperinsulinaemia simultaneously BLOCKS the alternative fuels** — insulin suppresses ketogenesis and lipolysis, so the baby cannot generate ketone bodies to protect the brain. That is why hyperinsulinaemic hypoglycaemia is **more dangerous at any given glucose level** than hypoglycaemia from other causes, and why it must be anticipated rather than waited for.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **At-risk babies requiring routine glucose screening: infants of DIABETIC mothers, LARGE-for-gestational-age and MACROSOMIC babies, SMALL-for-gestational-age and growth-restricted babies (low glycogen stores), PRETERM infants, those with perinatal asphyxia, hypothermia, sepsis or polycythaemia, and infants of mothers on beta-blockers.**
- **Many are ASYMPTOMATIC — hence screening. Symptoms are non-specific: jitteriness, lethargy, poor feeding, hypotonia, apnoea, temperature instability, a high-pitched cry, seizures.**
- **Management: EARLY AND FREQUENT FEEDING for asymptomatic mild hypoglycaemia (breast milk or formula), with repeat glucose measurement; IV DEXTROSE (a 2 mL/kg bolus of 10% dextrose followed by an infusion) if symptomatic, if there are seizures, or if levels are very low or unresponsive.** **Buccal dextrose gel is now used as a first-line adjunct.**
- **The other classic problems of the infant of a diabetic mother: macrosomia and birth trauma, RESPIRATORY DISTRESS SYNDROME (delayed surfactant), HYPOCALCAEMIA and hypomagnesaemia, POLYCYTHAEMIA with hyperbilirubinaemia, hypertrophic cardiomyopathy with septal hypertrophy, and — with pre-gestational diabetes — congenital anomalies.**
- **PERSISTENT hypoglycaemia beyond a few days demands a work-up for congenital hyperinsulinism, endocrine deficiency (cortisol, growth hormone) and inborn errors of metabolism — draw the "CRITICAL SAMPLE" during hypoglycaemia before treating.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight cut-off recall → **<45 mg/dL**.

**REMEMBER:** *Insulin stays high after the placenta goes — and it blocks the ketones the brain would otherwise use.*

`Q11081`

---

## 58. Prognosis in Eclampsia

**PEARL:** **ANTEPARTUM ECLAMPSIA carries the WORST prognosis** — particularly with a long fit-to-delivery interval. Other poor prognostic features: **a long delay between the first fit and treatment (late referral), MORE THAN 10 FITS, COMA BETWEEN FITS, TEMPERATURE >102 °F with PULSE >120/min, SYSTOLIC BP >200 mmHg, OLIGURIA (<400 mL/24 h) with proteinuria >5 g/24 h, JAUNDICE, and failure to respond to treatment.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Antepartum eclampsia is worst because **the placenta — the source of the disease — is still in situ**, so the pathological process continues to drive further seizures, organ injury and coagulopathy while the mother is at her most unstable. **Postpartum eclampsia has already had the cause removed** and therefore tends to be self-limiting. This is the same logic that makes **DELIVERY THE ONLY DEFINITIVE TREATMENT.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Immediate management of an eclamptic fit: protect the airway and turn the woman into the LEFT LATERAL position, give oxygen, prevent injury, and give MAGNESIUM SULPHATE (loading dose, and a further 2 g bolus for a recurrent fit while on therapy). Control BP if ≥160/110. Then STABILISE AND DELIVER — but NEVER deliver during a fit; stabilise the mother first.**
- **"Deliver the mother, not the fetus" — maternal stabilisation always precedes delivery, and mode of delivery is decided obstetrically (eclampsia is NOT by itself an indication for caesarean).**
- **Continue magnesium for 24 hours after delivery or after the last fit. Up to a third of eclamptic fits occur POSTPARTUM, and late postpartum eclampsia can occur up to 6 weeks.**
- **Causes of maternal death in eclampsia: cerebral haemorrhage (the commonest), pulmonary oedema, aspiration pneumonia, acute kidney injury, HELLP with hepatic rupture, DIC and cardiac failure.**
- **If seizures continue despite adequate magnesium, or there are focal signs or prolonged coma, IMAGE THE BRAIN — consider intracranial haemorrhage, cerebral venous thrombosis, and PRES (posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome, with parieto-occipital oedema).**
- **Eclampsia is usually GENERALISED TONIC–CLONIC, lasting about a minute, in four stages (premonitory, tonic, clonic, coma).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Worst prognosis" → **antepartum eclampsia**.

**REMEMBER:** *The placenta is still there in antepartum eclampsia — the disease keeps running until it is delivered.*

`Q11082`

---

## 59. Male Contraception

**PEARL:** **GOSSYPOL — a cottonseed extract — causes HYPOKALAEMIC PARALYSIS**, along with cardiac arrhythmias, fatigue, reduced libido and **irreversible azoospermia in some men**. It inhibits spermatogenesis and reduces epididymal sperm motility, and was abandoned largely because of these effects.

**UNDERSTAND:** Male hormonal contraception is difficult because **spermatogenesis takes ~74 days and requires suppression of intratesticular testosterone to near zero**, which simultaneously abolishes libido and secondary sexual function unless testosterone is replaced. Gossypol was attractive precisely because it acts **directly on the seminiferous epithelium rather than through the hypothalamic–pituitary axis** — but its potassium-channel and mitochondrial toxicity proved unacceptable.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Other male methods: CONDOMS (the only method that also prevents STIs), VASECTOMY (permanent; note that AZOOSPERMIA IS NOT IMMEDIATE — alternative contraception must be used until a semen analysis is clear, usually after ~3 months or 20 ejaculations), TESTOSTERONE ENANTHATE and other androgen/progestin combinations (trial stage), and RISUG (Reversible Inhibition of Sperm Under Guidance — an Indian polymer injected into the vas).**
- **VASECTOMY complications: haematoma, infection, SPERM GRANULOMA, CHRONIC POST-VASECTOMY PAIN, and recanalisation (failure ~1 in 2000). NO-SCALPEL vasectomy is the preferred technique.**
- **Female sterilisation: LAPAROSCOPIC (Falope ring, clips) or minilaparotomy (POMEROY technique); failure ~1 in 200, and when it fails the pregnancy is DISPROPORTIONATELY LIKELY TO BE ECTOPIC. Post-partum tubal ligation is done within 7 days or after 6 weeks.**
- **Contraceptive effectiveness ranking (typical use): implants and IUDs (most effective, "LARC") > injectables > pills > condoms > withdrawal and fertility awareness.**
- **The LACTATIONAL AMENORRHOEA METHOD is ~98% effective ONLY if ALL THREE conditions hold: fully/nearly fully breastfeeding, AMENORRHOEIC, and within 6 MONTHS of delivery.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Hypokalaemic paralysis" → **gossypol**.

**REMEMBER:** *Cottonseed stops sperm and drops the potassium.*

`Q11083`

---

## 60. Oral Contraceptive Preparations

**PEARL:** Among Indian preparations, **LOETTE has the LEAST oestrogen — ethinyl estradiol 20 µg with desogestrel; MALA-N, MALA-D and YASMIN all contain 30 µg.** **MALA-N (free, government supply) and MALA-D (subsidised social marketing) both contain LEVONORGESTREL 0.15 mg + EE 30 µg, supplied as 21 hormone tablets + 7 IRON tablets. YASMIN contains DROSPIRENONE 3 mg.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The historical trend has been **steadily reducing the oestrogen dose**, because **oestrogen is responsible for the venous thromboembolic risk** (it increases clotting factor synthesis) while contributing relatively little extra contraceptive efficacy. Modern "low-dose" pills contain **<50 µg**, and the trade-off for lower doses is **more breakthrough bleeding.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Generations: 1st — EE ≥50 µg. 2nd — EE 20–35 µg with LEVONORGESTREL or norgestimate. 3rd — EE 20–30 µg with DESOGESTREL or gestodene. 4th — DROSPIRENONE, dienogest or nomegestrol.** **Third- and fourth-generation progestogens carry a HIGHER VTE risk than second-generation levonorgestrel** — an important and counter-intuitive fact.
- **DROSPIRENONE is a spironolactone analogue — ANTI-ANDROGENIC (useful in acne, hirsutism and PCOS) and ANTI-MINERALOCORTICOID (less fluid retention, but a risk of HYPERKALAEMIA with ACE inhibitors, ARBs, NSAIDs or potassium-sparing diuretics).** **CYPROTERONE ACETATE is the most potently anti-androgenic progestogen.**
- **The iron tablets in Mala-N/D are placebo-plus-iron for the 7 hormone-free days, designed to maintain the daily-pill habit and address anaemia — a public-health design feature.**
- **Missed pills: one missed pill — take it at once and continue, no extra precaution. Two or more missed (or a delay >48 hours) — take the most recent pill, use barrier protection for 7 days, and consider emergency contraception if there was unprotected sex; if pills are missed in the LAST week, SKIP THE HORMONE-FREE INTERVAL.**
- **Drug interactions reducing efficacy: ENZYME-INDUCING drugs — RIFAMPICIN, phenytoin, carbamazepine, phenobarbitone, and ST JOHN'S WORT. Antiretrovirals interact too. Broad-spectrum antibiotics generally do NOT (an old myth), except rifamycins.**
- **DMPA (depot medroxyprogesterone acetate, 150 mg IM every 3 months) causes amenorrhoea, delayed return of fertility (up to 6–12 months) and REVERSIBLE BONE MINERAL DENSITY LOSS.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Brand-composition recall — **Loette = 20 µg, the lowest**.

**REMEMBER:** *Oestrogen carries the clot risk — so the dose has fallen from fifty to twenty.*

`Q11084`

---

## 61. Return of Fertility After Delivery

**PEARL:** **The mean time to the first menstruation after delivery is 7–9 WEEKS.** In **NON-LACTATING** women, ovulation can return **as early as 4 WEEKS** and 80% menstruate by 12 weeks; in **LACTATING** women ovulation returns at about **10 weeks or later.** **The "RULE OF 3s": start contraception in the 3rd POSTPARTUM WEEK if not (or only partially) breastfeeding, and in the 3rd POSTPARTUM MONTH if fully breastfeeding.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Lactation delays fertility because **suckling-induced PROLACTIN suppresses pulsatile GnRH**, and therefore LH — the basis of the lactational amenorrhoea method. But the suppression is **incomplete and unpredictable, and OVULATION PRECEDES THE FIRST PERIOD** — which is exactly why waiting for menstruation before starting contraception is unsafe, and why the rule of 3s exists.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Postpartum contraceptive choices: PROGESTOGEN-ONLY methods (POP, DMPA, implants) and the LNG-IUS are safe in breastfeeding and can be started early; COMBINED oestrogen-containing pills are AVOIDED for the first 6 WEEKS (VTE risk) and generally until 6 months in breastfeeding women (they reduce milk supply). PPIUCD (postpartum IUCD) may be inserted within 48 HOURS of delivery or after 6 weeks — insertion between 48 hours and 4 weeks carries a high expulsion and perforation risk.**
- **PUERPERIUM lasts 6 weeks. Involution: the uterus is at the umbilicus immediately after delivery, at the symphysis by 2 weeks, and non-palpable abdominally thereafter, returning to normal size by 6 weeks. LOCHIA: RUBRA (red, days 1–4), SEROSA (pale, days 5–9), ALBA (white, days 10–15).**
- **SUBINVOLUTION — a larger, softer uterus with persistent lochia — suggests RETAINED PRODUCTS, ENDOMETRITIS or fibroids.**
- **PUERPERAL PYREXIA is a temperature ≥38 °C on any 2 of the first 10 days (excluding the first 24 hours) — think endometritis, wound and perineal infection, UTI, mastitis, DVT and chest infection.**
- **POSITIVE HOMAN'S SIGN (calf pain on dorsiflexion) suggests DEEP VEIN THROMBOSIS — the postpartum period carries the highest VTE risk of pregnancy; investigate with COMPRESSION ULTRASOUND (D-dimer is unhelpful in pregnancy and the puerperium).**
- **Breastfeeding also reduces maternal risks of breast and ovarian cancer and speeds involution (oxytocin release).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Mean time for first menstruation postpartum" → **7–9 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *Ovulation comes before the first period — start contraception at three weeks, or three months if fully breastfeeding.*

`Q11085`

---

## 62. Placenta Praevia versus Abruption

**PEARL:** **PAINLESS, CAUSELESS, RECURRENT, APPARENT bright-red bleeding, often during sleep, with a RELAXED, NON-TENDER uterus and a normal fetal heart = PLACENTA PRAEVIA.** Its complications include **malpresentation, preterm labour, PPH (the lower segment contracts poorly), sepsis, birth asphyxia and low birth weight — but NOT PRECIPITATE LABOUR**, which belongs to **ABRUPTION and uterine tachysystole.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The two differ in **where the blood is and what the uterus is doing**. In praevia, the placenta separates from the **lower segment as it stretches — painlessly, with the blood escaping freely (revealed)**. In abruption, blood is forced **into the myometrium (Couvelaire uterus), which is irritant** — producing continuous pain, a **hard, tender, hypertonic "woody" uterus**, often concealed bleeding, and a uterus so irritable that labour may be **precipitate**. The classic error is expecting the amount of visible blood to match the degree of shock in abruption — it does not.

**MUST KNOW:**
- ***NEVER PERFORM A VAGINAL EXAMINATION IN SUSPECTED PLACENTA PRAEVIA*** — it can provoke torrential haemorrhage. Confirm with **TRANSVAGINAL ULTRASOUND** (safe and more accurate than transabdominal), or perform a "double set-up" examination in theatre if delivery is planned.
- **Praevia grading: I (low-lying) and II (marginal, reaching the os) are "minor"; III (partially covering) and IV (completely covering the os) are "major" and require CAESAREAN. Diagnosis before 28 weeks is provisional — most low placentas "migrate" upwards as the lower segment forms.**
- **PLACENTA ACCRETA SPECTRUM (accreta/increta/percreta) is strongly associated with placenta praevia PLUS PREVIOUS CAESAREAN — the risk rises steeply with each additional caesarean; suspect it on Doppler/MRI, and plan delivery with a multidisciplinary team and possible caesarean HYSTERECTOMY.**
- **ABRUPTION: risk factors are HYPERTENSION/pre-eclampsia (the commonest), trauma, smoking and cocaine, polyhydramnios with sudden decompression, previous abruption, and thrombophilia. Complications: DIC (from thromboplastin release — abruption is the commonest obstetric cause), acute kidney injury and RENAL CORTICAL NECROSIS, PPH from a COUVELAIRE UTERUS, and high perinatal mortality.**
- **Expectant (McAFEE–JOHNSON) management of praevia is used for a preterm, stable woman with a live fetus: hospitalisation, corticosteroids, anti-D if Rh-negative, and delivery at ~36–37 weeks.**
- **VASA PRAEVIA is the third cause of antepartum haemorrhage: painless bleeding at membrane rupture with rapid FETAL exsanguination.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Complication NOT anticipated in praevia" → **precipitate labour**.

**REMEMBER:** *Painless bleeding from a soft uterus is praevia; painful bleeding from a hard uterus is abruption.*

`Q11086`

---

## 63. Diagnosing Endometrial Carcinoma

**PEARL:** **The PAPANICOLAOU SMEAR IS NOT RELIABLE for endometrial carcinoma — it is positive in only ~30–50% of cases** (it samples the cervix, not the endometrium). Reliable methods are **ENDOMETRIAL BIOPSY (Pipelle/Sharman curette — ~90% accurate and the usual first step), HYSTEROSCOPY WITH TARGETED BIOPSY, and FRACTIONAL CURETTAGE (which also indicates whether the endocervix is involved).**

**UNDERSTAND:** Endometrial cells reach the cervix only sporadically, so **a normal Pap smear can never exclude endometrial cancer** — while the presence of **ENDOMETRIAL CELLS ON A PAP SMEAR IN A POSTMENOPAUSAL WOMAN is abnormal and demands endometrial sampling.** The corollary is the single most important rule in gynaecology: **postmenopausal bleeding requires TISSUE, not cytology.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Ultrasound (TVS) findings suggesting malignancy: ENDOMETRIAL THICKNESS >4–5 mm in a postmenopausal woman, an irregular hyperechoic endometrium, increased vascularity with LOW resistance, and intrauterine fluid. A thickness ≤4 mm has a high negative predictive value — but ANY persistent bleeding still warrants biopsy.**
- **Endometrial carcinoma is the commonest gynaecological malignancy in developed countries; ~90% present with POSTMENOPAUSAL BLEEDING, which is why it is usually diagnosed EARLY (stage I) and has a good prognosis.**
- **TYPE I (~80%): ENDOMETRIOID, OESTROGEN-DEPENDENT, arising in HYPERPLASIA, in younger/perimenopausal, obese women — risk factors are UNOPPOSED OESTROGEN, obesity, nulliparity, PCOS, late menopause, early menarche, diabetes, TAMOXIFEN, and LYNCH SYNDROME. Good prognosis; PTEN mutations. TYPE II (~10–20%): SEROUS and CLEAR CELL, OESTROGEN-INDEPENDENT, in older thin women with an ATROPHIC endometrium, p53 mutation, deeply invasive with early spread — POOR prognosis.**
- **Staging is SURGICAL: total hysterectomy with bilateral salpingo-oophorectomy, peritoneal washings and lymph node assessment; adjuvant radiotherapy/chemotherapy by risk group.**
- **HYPERPLASIA: without atypia — low progression risk, treat with progestogens (LNG-IUS is first-line). WITH ATYPIA (endometrioid intraepithelial neoplasia) — up to 30–40% harbour or progress to carcinoma; HYSTERECTOMY is standard, with high-dose progestogen only for fertility preservation under close surveillance.**
- **COMBINED ORAL CONTRACEPTIVES are PROTECTIVE against endometrial (and ovarian) cancer, for decades after use.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Reliable diagnosis EXCEPT" → **Pap smear**.

**REMEMBER:** *The Pap looks at the cervix — endometrial cancer needs a piece of endometrium.*

`Q11087`

---

## 64. Cervical Cytology Classifications

**PEARL:** **BETHESDA: LSIL = CIN 1. HSIL = CIN 2 and CIN 3/carcinoma in situ.** The categories are **NILM (negative), ASC-US, ASC-H, LSIL, HSIL, AGC and carcinoma.** The older **PAPANICOLAOU classification had FIVE GRADES (I normal → V frankly malignant cells)** and is obsolete. **COLPOSCOPY is indicated for an abnormal Pap smear, a visible cervical lesion, VAGINAL and VULVAL lesions, and FOLLOW-UP AFTER ABLATIVE THERAPY — but NOT for a UTERINE (endometrial) lesion**, which is beyond the colposcope's reach.

**UNDERSTAND:** The two-tier LSIL/HSIL split exists because it maps onto **biology and management, not just appearance**: **LSIL represents PRODUCTIVE HPV INFECTION, which regresses spontaneously in most women (especially adolescents), whereas HSIL represents a TRANSFORMING infection with integrated HPV, which is a genuine cancer precursor requiring treatment.** That is why LSIL in a young woman may simply be followed while HSIL is excised.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **CIN grading is HISTOLOGICAL (depth of epithelial involvement: CIN 1 lower third, CIN 2 two-thirds, CIN 3 full thickness with an intact basement membrane); LSIL/HSIL is CYTOLOGICAL. Do not mix the vocabularies.**
- **ASC-US is managed by REFLEX HPV TESTING — HPV-positive goes to colposcopy, HPV-negative returns to routine screening. ASC-H and AGC always require colposcopy (AGC also requires endometrial sampling in women over 35 or with risk factors).**
- **The TRANSFORMATION ZONE — between the original and the current squamocolumnar junction — is where virtually all cervical neoplasia arises, because that is where active SQUAMOUS METAPLASIA (and therefore vulnerability to HPV integration) occurs. SQUAMOUS METAPLASIA ITSELF IS NORMAL, not a lesion.**
- **Colposcopic assessment uses ACETIC ACID (aceto-white lesions), LUGOL'S IODINE (SCHILLER'S TEST — abnormal epithelium does not stain), and vascular patterns (PUNCTATION, MOSAIC, and — indicating invasion — ATYPICAL VESSELS).**
- **HPV testing is more SENSITIVE than cytology and is now the preferred primary screen from age 30; cytology is more SPECIFIC.**
- **Most CIN 1 regresses; ~30% of untreated CIN 3 progresses to invasive cancer over 10–30 years.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "LSIL includes" → **CIN 1**; "colposcopy indications EXCEPT" → **uterine lesion**.

**REMEMBER:** *Low-grade is one-third and usually regresses; high-grade is CIN 2 and 3 and must be treated.*

`Q11088, Q11095, Q11097`

---

## 65. Embryonic and Fetal Development Milestones

**PEARL:** **EXTERNAL GENITALIA develop at 8–12 WEEKS post conception.** The sequence: **days 14–21 — notochord and neural plate; days 21–28 — neural tube fusion, four cardiac chambers, FIRST HEARTBEAT ON DAY 21; weeks 4–6 — optic vesicles, LIMB BUDS, neural tube closure complete by day 30; weeks 6–8 — all major structures formed, "recognisably human"; weeks 8–12 — external genitalia; week 20 — lanugo and vernix; week 28 — VIABILITY, testes at the internal ring; week 36 — one testis descended; week 40 — both testes descended, nails present.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The **ORGANOGENETIC (teratogenic) window is weeks 3–8 post-conception (5–10 weeks from LMP)** — before it, the "all-or-none" phenomenon applies (the conceptus either dies or repairs completely); after it, teratogens cause **functional and growth deficits rather than gross malformations.** This single timeline explains why **peri-conceptional folic acid must be started BEFORE conception** (the neural tube closes by day 28, often before pregnancy is recognised) and why first-trimester exposures matter most.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Genital differentiation is DIRECTED BY THE TESTIS: SRY on the Y chromosome → testis → (a) SERTOLI cells make ANTI-MÜLLERIAN HORMONE, which REGRESSES the Müllerian ducts, and (b) LEYDIG cells make TESTOSTERONE (and DHT via 5α-reductase) which maintains the Wolffian ducts and masculinises the external genitalia. In the ABSENCE of a testis, development is FEMALE BY DEFAULT.**
- **Because the external genitalia masculinise at 8–12 weeks, ANDROGEN EXCESS BEFORE 12 WEEKS (as in congenital adrenal hyperplasia) causes LABIOSCROTAL FUSION and clitoromegaly, whereas exposure AFTER 12 weeks causes only clitoromegaly** — an elegant and heavily examined consequence of timing.
- **Ultrasound landmarks: gestational sac from ~4.5–5 weeks, yolk sac ~5.5 weeks, fetal pole with CARDIAC ACTIVITY by 6 weeks (or a CRL ≥7 mm), and reliable sex determination after 14–16 weeks (illegal in India under the PCPNDT Act).**
- **Fertilisation occurs in the AMPULLA; implantation at 6–7 days post-fertilisation; hCG becomes detectable in serum at ~8–9 days.**
- **Fetal lung maturity: the canalicular phase from 16–26 weeks, and surfactant production from type II pneumocytes rising from ~24 weeks and adequate by ~34–36 weeks.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight timeline recall → **8–12 weeks**.

**REMEMBER:** *Heart at three weeks, limbs at five, everything formed by eight, genitalia by twelve.*

`Q11089`

---

## 66. Fetal and Placental Haemodynamics

**PEARL:** **FETAL blood flow through the placenta is ~400 mL/min.** Pressures: **umbilical ARTERY ~60 mmHg, umbilical VEIN ~10 mmHg, villous capillaries 20–40 mmHg. Intervillous space pressure is 30–50 mmHg during a contraction and 10–15 mmHg at rest.** **Oxygen saturation: umbilical ARTERY 50–60% (PO2 20–25 mmHg); umbilical VEIN 70–80% (PO2 30–40 mmHg).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The fetus lives at **an arterial PO2 that would be lethal in an adult ("MOUNT EVEREST IN UTERO")** and compensates through three adaptations: **HbF has a higher oxygen affinity (a left-shifted curve, because it binds 2,3-DPG poorly), the fetal haemoglobin concentration is high (~17–18 g/dL), and the fetal cardiac output per kilogram is high.** The **DOUBLE BOHR and DOUBLE HALDANE effects** at the placenta further favour transfer.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **In the umbilical cord the VEIN carries OXYGENATED blood towards the fetus and the two ARTERIES carry deoxygenated blood away — the reverse of the systemic circulation. A SINGLE UMBILICAL ARTERY is associated with renal and cardiac anomalies and aneuploidy.**
- **During a uterine contraction the intervillous pressure rises above the pressure in the supplying spiral arteries, so INFLOW TEMPORARILY CEASES — placental exchange happens mainly BETWEEN contractions. This is why UTERINE HYPERSTIMULATION (tachysystole) causes fetal hypoxia, and why oxytocin must be titrated to no more than 5 contractions per 10 minutes.**
- **The fetal shunts direct the best-oxygenated blood to the brain and heart: DUCTUS VENOSUS → foramen ovale → left atrium → ascending aorta; the ductus arteriosus diverts right ventricular output away from the lungs.**
- **FUNIC (fetal) SOUFFLÉ is a soft blowing murmur SYNCHRONOUS WITH THE FETAL HEART, due to rushing blood in the UMBILICAL ARTERIES. UTERINE SOUFFLÉ is a soft systolic murmur heard low at the sides of the uterus, SYNCHRONOUS WITH THE MATERNAL PULSE, from the dilated uterine vessels — and is also heard over a large FIBROID.**
- **Umbilical artery Doppler measures downstream PLACENTAL resistance: rising S/D ratio → absent → REVERSED end-diastolic flow, in order of increasing severity.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Number recall — **400 mL/min**; souffle discrimination is a classic pairing.

**REMEMBER:** *The fetal vein carries the good blood in; the funic souffle beats with the baby, the uterine souffle with the mother.*

`Q11090, Q11109`

---

## 67. Maternal Serum Alpha-Fetoprotein

**PEARL:** **MSAFP is RAISED in: WRONG (underestimated) GESTATIONAL AGE, OPEN NEURAL TUBE DEFECTS, MULTIPLE PREGNANCY, ANTERIOR ABDOMINAL WALL DEFECTS (gastroschisis > omphalocele), Rh ISOIMMUNISATION, intrauterine fetal DEATH, renal anomalies (congenital nephrosis), and placental abnormalities.** It is **LOW in TRISOMIES (Down syndrome, trisomy 18) and in GESTATIONAL TROPHOBLASTIC DISEASE.**

**UNDERSTAND:** AFP is the **fetus's principal serum protein**, so **anything that lets fetal serum leak directly into the amniotic fluid raises it** (an open neural tube or abdominal wall defect), as does **more fetal tissue (twins) or a wrongly early estimate of dates**. Conversely, it is low when there is **less viable fetal tissue or impaired production/placental transfer** — hence trophoblastic disease (no fetus at all in a complete mole) and aneuploidy. **The commonest cause of an abnormal MSAFP is WRONG DATES — always re-date by ultrasound first.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **MSAFP is measured at 15–20 WEEKS as part of the QUADRUPLE TEST; results are expressed in MULTIPLES OF THE MEDIAN (MoM), with >2.0–2.5 MoM considered raised.**
- **The quadruple test pattern in DOWN SYNDROME: AFP LOW, unconjugated OESTRIOL LOW, hCG HIGH, INHIBIN A HIGH. In TRISOMY 18: AFP, oestriol AND hCG all LOW with normal inhibin.**
- **A raised MSAFP with a normal detailed anomaly scan still predicts adverse OBSTETRIC outcomes — growth restriction, pre-eclampsia, abruption, preterm birth and stillbirth — because it implies a leaky, dysfunctional placenta. Such pregnancies warrant closer surveillance.**
- **ACETYLCHOLINESTERASE in amniotic fluid is more SPECIFIC than AFP for an open neural tube defect (it is not present with abdominal wall defects or fetal blood contamination).**
- **Prevention: FOLIC ACID 400 µg periconceptionally, and 4–5 mg for high-risk women (previous affected child, diabetes, obesity, antiepileptics — VALPROATE and carbamazepine).**
- **Antenatal detection: the "LEMON" sign (frontal bone scalloping) and "BANANA" sign (curved cerebellum) with ventriculomegaly indicate an open spina bifida with the ARNOLD–CHIARI II malformation.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "MSAFP raised in all EXCEPT" → **gestational trophoblastic disease**.

**REMEMBER:** *AFP is fetal serum — it goes up when the fetus leaks and down when there is less fetus.*

`Q11091`

---

## 68. Ectopic Pregnancy

**PEARL:** The **CLASSIC TRIAD is AMENORRHOEA (~75%), ABDOMINAL PAIN (~100% — the most constant feature) and VAGINAL BLEEDING (~70%).** **SHOULDER-TIP PAIN (referred from diaphragmatic irritation by haemoperitoneum) occurs in ~25% and is NOT part of the classic triad**, though it is a valuable sign of intraperitoneal blood. The **AMPULLA is the commonest site.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The endometrium responds to the ectopic pregnancy's hormones by **decidualising**, and it may then **shed as a "DECIDUAL CAST"**, producing the bleeding — which is why the bleeding is typically **scanty, dark and continuous, unlike the bright, heavy bleeding of miscarriage.** The **ARIAS-STELLA REACTION** — hypersecretory glandular epithelium with enlarged, hyperchromatic, atypical nuclei — is the histological counterpart of this hormonal stimulation, and it can be mistaken for carcinoma.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The ARIAS-STELLA REACTION IS NOT DIAGNOSTIC OF ECTOPIC PREGNANCY — it occurs in intrauterine pregnancy, ectopic pregnancy, gestational trophoblastic disease, endometriosis, and with exogenous oestrogen/gonadotrophin exposure.** It indicates **a hormonal effect, not a location.**
- **Diagnosis: TRANSVAGINAL ULTRASOUND plus SERIAL β-hCG. Above the DISCRIMINATORY ZONE (β-hCG ~1500–3500 mIU/mL) an intrauterine sac should be visible — if it is not, suspect ectopic. In normal early pregnancy β-hCG rises by ≥66% in 48 hours; a SUB-OPTIMAL RISE OR PLATEAU suggests ectopic or failing pregnancy. A "PSEUDOGESTATIONAL SAC" (central, no yolk sac, no double decidual ring) is a trap.**
- **Risk factors: previous PID/*Chlamydia*, previous ectopic (the strongest), tubal surgery or STERILISATION, IUCD in situ (which prevents intrauterine far more than tubal pregnancy), assisted reproduction, endometriosis, and smoking.**
- **Management: METHOTREXATE (single dose 50 mg/m²) for a haemodynamically stable, unruptured ectopic with β-hCG <5000, no fetal cardiac activity and a mass <3.5 cm — monitor hCG on days 4 and 7, expecting a ≥15% fall. AVOID folic acid, NSAIDs, alcohol and intercourse during treatment. SURGERY (laparoscopic SALPINGECTOMY, or salpingostomy if the other tube is diseased) for rupture, instability, or failed medical therapy.**
- **A ruptured ectopic is the leading cause of first-trimester maternal death; classic findings are shock with a distended, tender abdomen, CERVICAL EXCITATION and a boggy fullness in the pouch of Douglas — CULDOCENTESIS yielding non-clotting blood is the historical bedside test.**
- **GIVE ANTI-D to Rh-negative women.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not part of the classic triad" → **shoulder-tip pain**; Arias-Stella → **seen in all of the above**.

**REMEMBER:** *Pain, amenorrhoea, bleeding — and the shoulder hurts only once the blood reaches the diaphragm.*

`Q11092, Q11094`

---

## 69. Hydrops Fetalis

**PEARL:** The **"BUDDHA POSITION"** — a fetus with a **halo of scalp oedema, ascites and limbs held in the Buddha posture — is HYDROPS FETALIS**, the most severe form of haemolytic disease of the fetus and newborn. **Excessive red cell destruction → severe ANAEMIA → tissue hypoxia and high-output CARDIAC FAILURE with hypoproteinaemia → generalised oedema, ascites, pleural and pericardial effusions and placentomegaly.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Hydrops is simply **fluid in two or more fetal compartments**, and in the immune form it arises from **profound anaemia**: the fetus compensates with **extramedullary haematopoiesis in the liver, which impairs hepatic protein synthesis and raises portal pressure**, while the failing high-output heart raises venous pressure. **Anaemia, hypoalbuminaemia and heart failure together defeat the fetal capacity to keep fluid in the vascular space.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **NON-IMMUNE hydrops now accounts for ~90% of cases: cardiac anomalies and arrhythmias (the commonest), chromosomal (TURNER SYNDROME, trisomy 21), thalassaemia (BART'S HYDROPS — homozygous alpha-thalassaemia, the commonest cause in Southeast Asia), infections (PARVOVIRUS B19, CMV, toxoplasmosis, syphilis), twin–twin transfusion, thoracic masses and lymphatic dysplasia. IMMUNE hydrops (Rh and other alloimmunisation) is now uncommon because of anti-D prophylaxis.**
- **Antenatal detection and management: MIDDLE CEREBRAL ARTERY PEAK SYSTOLIC VELOCITY >1.5 MoM predicts fetal anaemia and triggers CORDOCENTESIS and INTRAUTERINE TRANSFUSION** — which has transformed the outlook for treatable causes.
- **The severity spectrum of HDFN: congenital haemolytic anaemia → ICTERUS GRAVIS NEONATORUM (with a risk of KERNICTERUS from unconjugated bilirubin) → HYDROPS FETALIS.**
- **Investigation of non-immune hydrops: maternal blood group and antibody screen, KLEIHAUER test (fetomaternal haemorrhage), infection serology, HAEMOGLOBIN ELECTROPHORESIS (thalassaemia), fetal karyotype/microarray and detailed echocardiography.**
- **Neonatal management: resuscitation with pleural/ascitic drainage, ventilation, exchange transfusion and phototherapy. Prognosis in non-immune hydrops remains poor.**
- **MIRROR (BALLANTYNE) SYNDROME — the mother develops oedema and a pre-eclampsia-like illness "mirroring" the hydropic fetus.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Image of a fetus in the Buddha position with a scalp halo → **hydrops fetalis**.

**REMEMBER:** *Anaemia, failing heart, low albumin — fluid in every compartment and the Buddha posture.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://cdn1.dailyrounds.org/uploads/1e674f87892e41ab9d69cd43c7267f51.PNG)

`Q11096`

## 70. Retraction Ring, Constriction Ring and Uterine Rupture

**PEARL:** **The PHYSIOLOGICAL RETRACTION RING marks the junction of the thick upper and thin lower uterine segments. In OBSTRUCTED LABOUR it becomes prominent and rises progressively upward as a PATHOLOGICAL RETRACTION RING (BANDL'S RING) — an imminent sign of RUPTURE.** A **CONSTRICTION RING is different: a localised tetanic contraction at any level (often around the fetal neck), occurring in prolonged labour after membrane rupture, which does NOT move and is NOT a sign of obstruction.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The upper segment **RETRACTS — it shortens permanently after each contraction and does not return to its former length — while the lower segment stretches and thins passively.** In obstructed labour, this normal division becomes exaggerated: the upper segment thickens relentlessly and the lower segment is drawn out until it **tears**. That is why the ring **RISES** (a very useful sign) and why the lower segment is where rupture occurs.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **UTERINE RUPTURE: hypotension, tachycardia, severe abdominal pain, cessation of contractions, LOSS OF STATION with the presenting part receding, easily palpable FETAL PARTS in the abdomen, fetal bradycardia or death, and vaginal bleeding. Management is IMMEDIATE LAPAROTOMY — repair if the tear is clean and childbearing is desired, otherwise subtotal or total HYSTERECTOMY.**
- **The COMMONEST CAUSE of uterine rupture today is a PREVIOUS CAESAREAN SCAR**, followed by obstructed labour, injudicious oxytocin/prostaglandin use, instrumental delivery, internal podalic version and trauma.
- **SCAR DEHISCENCE versus RUPTURE: DEHISCENCE — only PART of the scar separates, the MEMBRANES REMAIN INTACT, the peritoneum (serosa) is NOT breached, bleeding is minimal or nil, and it is usually asymptomatic (found at repeat caesarean). RUPTURE — the ENTIRE scar separates, membranes rupture, and THE UTERINE AND PERITONEAL CAVITIES BECOME CONTINUOUS, with significant bleeding.**
- **Risk factors for scar rupture in VBAC: MORE THAN ONE previous caesarean, INTERPREGNANCY INTERVAL <24 MONTHS (not 36), INDUCTION (especially with prostaglandins) and high-dose oxytocin augmentation (>20 mU/min), SINGLE-LAYER uterine closure, a previous CLASSICAL or T-incision, and previous rupture.** Overall risk after one low transverse caesarean is **~0.5–0.7%**.
- **DÜHRSSEN'S INCISIONS — made at the 2 and 10 o'clock positions on an incompletely dilated cervix — allow delivery of the after-coming head in a breech or an entrapped head; complications include cervical laceration and extension into the lower segment.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Image of a ridge rising across the abdomen → **retraction (Bandl's) ring**; "false about dehiscence" → **the cavities become continuous** (that is rupture).

**REMEMBER:** *A ring that climbs means the lower segment is about to give way.*

## 71. Intrauterine Contraceptive Devices

**PEARL:** **NOVA-T contains SILVER added to the copper wire — the silver core prevents fragmentation of the copper and extends the lifespan from 3 to 5 YEARS.** **COPPER T 380A (ParaGard) lasts 10 YEARS (the longest); MIRENA (LNG-IUS, 52 mg levonorgestrel releasing 20 µg/day) lasts 5 YEARS; Multiload-375 lasts 5 years.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The **COPPER IUCD works chemically, not mechanically** — copper ions are **directly spermicidal and toxic to ova**, and they induce a sterile inflammatory endometrial reaction. So its effectiveness depends on **the surface area of copper exposed**, which is why devices are named by their copper surface (375, 380 mm²) and why fragmentation matters enough to justify a silver core. **The LNG-IUS works differently — by thickening cervical mucus and atrophying the endometrium** — which is why it REDUCES menstrual loss while the copper device INCREASES it.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Copper IUCD side effects: MENORRHAGIA and DYSMENORRHOEA (the commonest reasons for removal), expulsion (highest in the first year), and perforation (~1 in 1000, related to insertion technique). LNG-IUS instead causes irregular spotting initially and then AMENORRHOEA, which is a therapeutic benefit — it is FIRST-LINE MEDICAL TREATMENT FOR HEAVY MENSTRUAL BLEEDING, and is also used for adenomyosis, endometrial hyperplasia and endometrial protection during HRT.**
- **The copper IUCD is the MOST EFFECTIVE EMERGENCY CONTRACEPTIVE (within 5 days of unprotected intercourse or of the estimated ovulation) and then continues as long-term contraception.**
- **Contraindications: pregnancy, undiagnosed vaginal bleeding, active pelvic infection or current cervicitis, distorted uterine cavity (fibroids, anomalies), genital malignancy, and — for the LNG-IUS — breast cancer. WILSON'S DISEASE and copper allergy contraindicate copper devices.**
- **PREGNANCY WITH AN IUCD IN SITU: exclude ectopic; if intrauterine and the threads are visible, REMOVE the device (leaving it raises the risk of miscarriage, septic abortion and preterm birth).**
- **PID risk is confined largely to the first 20 days after insertion (from pre-existing infection carried up at insertion), not to the device itself.**
- **Timing: any time when reasonably certain the woman is not pregnant, immediately after delivery (within 48 hours) or after 6 weeks, and immediately after an uncomplicated abortion. In India, PPIUCD insertion is a major programme intervention.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Silver-containing IUCD" → **Nova-T**.

**REMEMBER:** *Silver keeps the copper from crumbling — three years becomes five.*

`Q11105`

## 72. Friedman's Divisions of Labour

**PEARL:** Friedman divided labour into three functional divisions: **PREPARATORY (the LATENT phase PLUS the ACCELERATION phase) — where little dilatation occurs but the cervix prepares, and which is SENSITIVE TO SEDATION AND ANALGESIA; DILATATIONAL (the phase of MAXIMUM SLOPE) — the fastest dilatation, and UNAFFECTED by sedation; and PELVIC (the DECELERATION phase plus the SECOND STAGE) — where the cardinal movements of descent occur.** **The acceleration phase belongs to the PREPARATORY, NOT the dilatational, division.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The divisions describe **what the uterus and cervix are actually doing**, which is why their pharmacological sensitivities differ. The **preparatory division depends on connective-tissue remodelling and coordinated contraction, both readily suppressed by sedation** — hence a "false labour" or prolonged latent phase after analgesia. Once **active dilatation is established, the process is mechanically self-sustaining** and drugs no longer arrest it. The **pelvic division depends on the passenger and the passage, not the powers alone** — which is why abnormalities here reflect malposition or disproportion.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The classic Friedman abnormalities: PROLONGED LATENT PHASE (>20 h in a nullipara, >14 h in a multipara) — treat with rest/analgesia or oxytocin; PROTRACTION DISORDERS (slow dilatation or descent); and ARREST DISORDERS (no dilatation for 2 hours, no descent for 1 hour) — which most often indicate CEPHALOPELVIC DISPROPORTION or malposition and are the commonest indication for caesarean.**
- **Modern (ZHANG) data show labour is SLOWER than Friedman described, that the active phase often does not begin until 5–6 cm, and that "1 cm/hour" is too strict — so contemporary guidance allows more time before diagnosing arrest, which has reduced primary caesarean rates.**
- **Stages of labour: FIRST — onset of true labour to full dilatation (latent then active). SECOND — full dilatation to delivery of the baby (up to 2 h in a nullipara, 1 h in a multipara, plus an hour with epidural). THIRD — delivery of the placenta (normally within 30 minutes; retained beyond that requires manual removal). FOURTH — the first hour postpartum, when PPH is most likely.**
- **The "3 Ps" of labour progress: POWERS (contractions), PASSAGE (pelvis and soft tissues) and PASSENGER (fetal size, lie, presentation and position).**
- **Adequate contractions: 3–5 in 10 minutes, each lasting 40–60 seconds, with normal resting tone; MONTEVIDEO UNITS >200 in 10 minutes indicate adequate uterine activity.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "All true EXCEPT" → **the dilatational division includes the acceleration phase**.

**REMEMBER:** *Sedation stops the preparatory division and nothing else — once maximum slope begins, labour runs on its own.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/1ecf286f5fd04815866ed8359c47c3c5x1280x1033.JPEG)

`Q11111`
