## 104. Why Lactation Waits for Delivery

**PEARL:** **Milk production is INHIBITED DURING PREGNANCY BY THE HIGH OESTROGEN (and progesterone) levels**, which block prolactin's action at the breast despite prolactin being very high. **Lactation begins when the placenta is delivered and OESTROGEN AND PROGESTERONE FALL ABRUPTLY, releasing prolactin's effect** — the basis of LACTOGENESIS II at 2–4 days postpartum.

**UNDERSTAND:** Pregnancy prepares the breast fully (**ductal growth by oestrogen, alveolar growth by progesterone, and secretory differentiation by prolactin and human placental lactogen**) but deliberately holds back secretion. **The placenta is therefore the brake, and its delivery is the trigger** — which explains why **retained placental fragments delay lactation**, why **oestrogen-containing contraceptives reduce milk supply**, and why **bromocriptine/cabergoline (dopamine agonists) suppress lactation by lowering prolactin.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **PROLACTIN drives MILK SECRETION (from the anterior pituitary, suppressed by DOPAMINE, stimulated by suckling and by TRH — hence galactorrhoea in hypothyroidism). OXYTOCIN drives MILK EJECTION (the "let-down" reflex, from the posterior pituitary, conditioned to the baby's cry and inhibited by pain, anxiety and embarrassment).**
- **COLOSTRUM (days 1–3) is rich in PROTEIN, IgA, lactoferrin, vitamin A and minerals and low in fat and lactose — it is the newborn's first immunisation and must not be discarded (a common harmful cultural practice).**
- **Breast milk is LOW IN VITAMIN K (hence intramuscular vitamin K at birth), and low in vitamin D and iron after 6 months (hence complementary feeding from 6 months).**
- **EXCLUSIVE BREASTFEEDING for 6 MONTHS, with continued breastfeeding to 2 years, is the WHO recommendation. Feeding on demand and correct ATTACHMENT (mouth wide, more areola visible above than below, chin touching the breast, lower lip everted) prevent nipple fissure and mastitis.**
- **Contraindications to breastfeeding are few: maternal HIV where safe replacement feeding is available (in India, ART plus exclusive breastfeeding is recommended), active untreated tuberculosis (express milk until treated), GALACTOSAEMIA in the infant, and certain drugs (cytotoxics, radioiodine, lithium, amiodarone).**
- **Human placental lactogen (SOMATOMAMMOTROPIN) prepares the breast but does NOT inhibit milk production — the inhibitor is oestrogen.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Milk production in pregnancy is inhibited by" → **high oestrogen**.

**REMEMBER:** *The placenta holds the brake — deliver it and the milk comes in.*

`Q11433`

---

## 105. Obstructive Genital Anomalies and Retrograde Menstruation

**PEARL:** A teenager with **DYSMENORRHOEA and chronic pelvic pain found to have a TRANSVERSE VAGINAL SEPTUM is at high risk of ENDOMETRIOSIS** — outflow obstruction forces menstrual blood **RETROGRADELY through the tubes into the peritoneal cavity**, the classic demonstration of Sampson's hypothesis.

**UNDERSTAND:** Obstructive anomalies are effectively a **natural experiment proving that retrograde menstruation causes endometriosis** — the prevalence of endometriosis in obstructed genital tracts is very high and **it often regresses once the obstruction is relieved.** The same reasoning explains the classic clinical sequence: **CYCLICAL PAIN with amenorrhoea → HAEMATOCOLPOS/HAEMATOMETRA → haematosalpinx → endometriosis and adhesions.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **IMPERFORATE HYMEN presents at puberty with primary amenorrhoea, cyclical pain, a lower abdominal mass, sometimes urinary retention, and a TENSE BLUISH BULGING MEMBRANE at the introitus — treated by CRUCIATE INCISION. A TRANSVERSE VAGINAL SEPTUM presents identically but with NO bulging membrane visible and a shorter vagina — it requires surgical excision and is at higher risk of stenosis and recurrence.**
- **OHVIRA (Herlyn–Werner–Wunderlich) syndrome: uterus didelphys + OBSTRUCTED HEMIVAGINA + IPSILATERAL RENAL AGENESIS — the girl menstruates normally (through the unobstructed side) but has severe cyclical pain and a paravaginal mass. ALWAYS IMAGE THE KIDNEYS in any Müllerian anomaly.**
- **A NON-COMMUNICATING RUDIMENTARY HORN of a unicornuate uterus obstructs similarly, causing cyclical pain, haematometra and a risk of ectopic pregnancy with rupture — excise it.**
- **SCAR ENDOMETRIOSIS is the other proof of implantation: CYCLICAL PAIN AND A NODULE AT AN EPISIOTOMY OR CAESAREAN SCAR, from endometrial tissue implanted at surgery. Treatment is WIDE LOCAL EXCISION.**
- **Endometriosis pain correlates with the DEPTH OF INFILTRATION (lesions >5 mm deep) rather than with the stage or the number of sites — deep infiltrating endometriosis of the uterosacrals and rectovaginal septum causes the worst dyspareunia and dyschezia.**
- **First-line imaging for suspected endometriosis is TRANSVAGINAL ULTRASOUND (which detects endometriomas and deep disease well); laparoscopy remains the gold standard but is no longer the first step.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Transverse vaginal septum with dysmenorrhoea → **endometriosis**.

**REMEMBER:** *Block the outflow and the blood goes backwards through the tubes — obstruction breeds endometriosis.*

![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/08/05/screenshot-2024-08-05-103004.png)

## 106. A High NTD Risk on Serum Screening

**PEARL:** With a **quadruple marker showing LOW risk of trisomy but HIGH risk of a NEURAL TUBE DEFECT (i.e. a raised AFP) at 18 weeks, the next step is a DETAILED ANOMALY SCAN (TIFA / targeted imaging for fetal anomalies)** — NOT amniocentesis, CVS or NIPT.

**UNDERSTAND:** Serum screening is only a **risk estimate for a STRUCTURAL problem here — and structural problems are diagnosed by IMAGING, not by karyotyping.** Amniocentesis would give a chromosome result that is already predicted to be normal; NIPT screens for aneuploidy and cannot detect a neural tube defect at all; and CVS is a first-trimester test. **Modern high-resolution ultrasound detects >95% of open neural tube defects**, which is why amniotic fluid AFP and acetylcholinesterase have been largely abandoned.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The cranial signs of an open spina bifida are more reliable than the spinal view itself: the "LEMON" SIGN (frontal bone scalloping), the "BANANA" SIGN (a curved, obliterated cerebellum), a small biparietal diameter, and VENTRICULOMEGALY — all consequences of the ARNOLD–CHIARI II malformation caused by CSF leak.**
- **The anomaly scan is best performed at 18–22 WEEKS (adequate fetal size, sufficient liquor, good visualisation).**
- **ANENCEPHALY can be detected as early as 11–14 weeks; it is incompatible with life and is an indication for termination at any gestation via the Medical Board route if late.**
- **A raised AFP with a NORMAL detailed scan still predicts placental dysfunction — growth restriction, pre-eclampsia, abruption, preterm birth and stillbirth — so increase surveillance.**
- **Diagnostic tests are for CHROMOSOMAL/genetic questions: CVS at 10–13 weeks, amniocentesis from 15 weeks; NIPT (cell-free DNA) is a highly accurate SCREEN for trisomies 21, 18 and 13 and sex chromosome aneuploidy, but is NOT diagnostic and does NOT detect structural defects.**
- **Prevention remains preconceptional FOLIC ACID.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Raised AFP → **anomaly scan** (imaging answers a structural question).

**REMEMBER:** *A structural risk needs a picture, not a karyotype.*

`Q11435`

---

## 107. The Planes of the Pelvis

**PEARL:** The **MID-PELVIS (plane of least dimensions, at the level of the ISCHIAL SPINES) is the narrowest plane of the pelvis** — its **INTERSPINOUS DIAMETER (~10.5 cm) is the SHORTEST diameter in the entire pelvis** and the commonest site of arrest. The **PELVIC INLET** is bounded by the sacral promontory, iliopectineal lines and upper symphysis; the **OBSTETRIC OUTLET** is bounded by the lower symphysis, ischial tuberosities and sacrococcygeal joint.

**UNDERSTAND:** The ischial spines matter clinically for three reasons that all follow from their position: they are **the reference point for STATION (station 0 = the leading part at the spines = ENGAGEMENT), the narrowest passage (hence DEEP TRANSVERSE ARREST occurs here), and the landmark for the PUDENDAL NERVE BLOCK.** Prominent spines (the ANDROID pelvis) reduce this already-shortest diameter further.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **INLET diameters: ANTEROPOSTERIOR — true conjugate (anatomical) 11 cm; OBSTETRIC CONJUGATE ~10 cm (the SHORTEST anteroposterior diameter and the one that matters, from the sacral promontory to the innermost point of the symphysis — it CANNOT be measured clinically); DIAGONAL CONJUGATE ~12 cm (measured clinically from the promontory to the lower border of the symphysis — subtract 1.5–2 cm to estimate the obstetric conjugate). TRANSVERSE 13 cm (the widest inlet diameter). OBLIQUE 12 cm.**
- **The fetal head therefore engages in the TRANSVERSE or oblique diameter at the inlet (widest transversely), rotates in the mid-pelvis, and delivers in the ANTEROPOSTERIOR diameter at the outlet (widest anteroposteriorly) — this is precisely why INTERNAL ROTATION must occur.**
- **OUTLET: anteroposterior ~13 cm (the widest outlet diameter), TRANSVERSE (bituberous) ~11 cm. The SUBPUBIC ANGLE should be ≥85–90°; a narrow angle (android) pushes the head backwards and causes perineal tears.**
- **Clinical pelvimetry assesses the diagonal conjugate, sacral curve, ischial spines, subpubic angle and intertuberous diameter. X-RAY PELVIMETRY IS NOT RECOMMENDED — it does not predict outcome and exposes the fetus; the true test of the pelvis is a TRIAL OF LABOUR.**
- **The TRUE pelvis lies below the pelvic brim and is the birth canal; the FALSE pelvis above it merely supports the gravid uterus.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Labelled pelvic diagram at the level of the spines → **mid-pelvis**.

**REMEMBER:** *The spines mark station zero and the narrowest plane — everything gets stuck there.*

## 108. Secondary Postpartum Haemorrhage

**PEARL:** **SECONDARY PPH = excessive bleeding between 24 HOURS AND 12 WEEKS after delivery.** Causes: **RETAINED PLACENTAL TISSUE (a retained cotyledon or a PLACENTAL POLYP), ENDOMETRITIS/infection, SUBINVOLUTION of the placental site, a caesarean scar dehiscence or uterine artery pseudoaneurysm, and rarely gestational trophoblastic disease.** **PLACENTA PRAEVIA causes ANTEPARTUM and PRIMARY PPH — never secondary PPH.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The distinction is temporal and mechanistic: **PRIMARY PPH is a failure of the immediate haemostatic mechanisms (tone, trauma, tissue, thrombin), while SECONDARY PPH is a failure of the placental site to INVOLUTE.** Normally the placental bed undergoes thrombosis, necrosis and re-epithelialisation over weeks; **retained tissue or infection prevents that process**, so the vessels remain open and bleed days later.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Assessment: ultrasound for retained products (though echogenic material is common and non-specific), high vaginal swab and blood cultures, full blood count and coagulation studies, and a β-hCG if trophoblastic disease is possible.**
- **Management: BROAD-SPECTRUM ANTIBIOTICS covering anaerobes (endometritis is usually polymicrobial), UTEROTONICS, and SURGICAL EVACUATION only if there is significant retained tissue — gentle suction evacuation under antibiotic cover, since the postpartum uterus perforates easily. Uterine artery EMBOLISATION is very effective for persistent bleeding, especially from a pseudoaneurysm.**
- **A PLACENTAL POLYP is retained placental tissue that becomes organised and fibrinous, presenting weeks later with irregular bleeding.**
- **PRIMARY PPH is within 24 hours; the causes are the FOUR Ts — TONE (atony, 70–80%), TRAUMA, TISSUE, THROMBIN.**
- **PUERPERAL SEPSIS is the accompanying diagnosis to consider: fever, offensive lochia, uterine tenderness and subinvolution; *Streptococcus pyogenes* infection can be fulminant. **Puerperal pyrexia is defined as a temperature >100.4 °F (38 °C) on any 2 of the first 10 days postpartum, EXCLUDING THE FIRST 24 HOURS.**
- **Prevention of secondary PPH begins in the third stage: careful inspection of the placenta and membranes for completeness.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not a cause of secondary PPH" → **placenta praevia**.

**REMEMBER:** *Secondary PPH is a placental site that never involuted — retained tissue or infection, not praevia.*

`Q11437, Q11448`

---

## 109. Disorders of Sex Development — 5α-Reductase Deficiency

**PEARL:** A **46,XY** individual raised female, presenting at puberty with **PRIMARY AMENORRHOEA, VIRILISATION (clitoromegaly), POOR BREAST DEVELOPMENT, a blind vaginal pouch and TESTOSTERONE IN THE MALE RANGE = 5α-REDUCTASE DEFICIENCY** — a **MALE PSEUDOHERMAPHRODITE (46,XY DSD: male gonads with female or ambiguous external genitalia).**

**UNDERSTAND:** **5α-reductase converts testosterone to DIHYDROTESTOSTERONE, and DHT — not testosterone — masculinises the EXTERNAL genitalia** (phallus, scrotum, prostate). So without it, the **external genitalia are female or ambiguous at birth**, while **testosterone-dependent INTERNAL Wolffian structures (epididymis, vas, seminal vesicles) develop normally** and **anti-Müllerian hormone still regresses the uterus and tubes.** At puberty, the **surge of testosterone itself produces virilisation** — the striking clinical phenomenon of a "girl" who masculinises at puberty ("penis at twelve").

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Contrast COMPLETE ANDROGEN INSENSITIVITY SYNDROME (46,XY): the androgen RECEPTOR is defective, so there is NO virilisation ever — the phenotype is unambiguously female with GOOD BREAST DEVELOPMENT (testosterone is aromatised to oestrogen and unopposed), SCANTY OR ABSENT PUBIC AND AXILLARY HAIR, a blind vagina, ABSENT uterus, and male-range testosterone. Gonads (testes, often inguinal) must be REMOVED after puberty because of the risk of germ cell malignancy.**
- **The two are distinguished by one word: 5α-reductase deficiency VIRILISES at puberty and has SOME pubic hair; androgen insensitivity does NEITHER but has BETTER breasts.**
- **Terminology: FEMALE pseudohermaphrodite (46,XX DSD) = ovaries with virilised external genitalia — almost always CONGENITAL ADRENAL HYPERPLASIA (21-hydroxylase deficiency), the commonest cause of ambiguous genitalia in a newborn and a salt-wasting EMERGENCY. TRUE HERMAPHRODITE (ovotesticular DSD) = both ovarian and testicular tissue.**
- **17α-HYDROXYLASE DEFICIENCY: reduced cortisol and sex steroids with EXCESS MINERALOCORTICOIDS — HYPERTENSION and HYPOKALAEMIA with sexual infantilism in both 46,XX and 46,XY.**
- **SWYER SYNDROME (46,XY complete gonadal dysgenesis): streak gonads, NORMAL female internal and external genitalia including a UTERUS, high FSH/LH, LOW testosterone — gonadectomy is mandatory, and pregnancy is possible with donor oocytes.**
- **Any DSD requires a multidisciplinary team, karyotype, pelvic imaging, and hormonal profile (testosterone, DHT, 17-OHP, AMH); sex of rearing is a considered decision, and irreversible surgery is deferred.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** 46,XY + male testosterone + virilisation at puberty → **5α-reductase deficiency / male pseudohermaphrodite**.

**REMEMBER:** *Testosterone builds the inside, DHT builds the outside — lose the enzyme and the outside stays female until puberty forces the issue.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/08/15/whatsapp-image-2023-06-12-at-190121011010201026_page_49.jpg)

`Q11444, Q11453`

## 110. Hysteroscopic Fluid Overload

**PEARL:** A **FLUID DEFICIT of ~2000 mL during operative hysteroscopy causes FLUID OVERLOAD → PULMONARY OEDEMA** (and, with HYPOTONIC media, DILUTIONAL HYPONATRAEMIA with cerebral oedema). **Maximum permitted deficits: HYPOTONIC fluid (glycine, sorbitol) 1000 mL in the healthy and 750 mL in the elderly or comorbid; ISOTONIC NORMAL SALINE 2500 mL in the healthy and 750–1000 mL with cardiovascular disease.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Distension media are absorbed through **open venous sinuses in the myometrium**, and absorption rises with **intrauterine pressure, operating time and the depth of myometrial resection** — which is why a submucous fibroid resection is the highest-risk procedure. The choice of medium determines the syndrome: **isotonic saline causes VOLUME overload alone (used with bipolar/mechanical instruments), whereas glycine (needed for monopolar energy) causes volume overload PLUS HYPONATRAEMIA and, through its metabolism to ammonia, encephalopathy and visual disturbance** — the gynaecological equivalent of TUR syndrome.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Management: STOP THE PROCEDURE, check SERUM ELECTROLYTES, give FUROSEMIDE and oxygen for volume overload; for SYMPTOMATIC HYPONATRAEMIA give 3% HYPERTONIC SALINE in an intensive care setting, correcting SLOWLY to avoid OSMOTIC DEMYELINATION. Asymptomatic mild hyponatraemia is treated by fluid restriction and monitoring.**
- **Prevention: use an AUTOMATED FLUID MANAGEMENT SYSTEM with continuous deficit monitoring, keep the intrauterine pressure at the MINIMUM that gives a view (ideally below mean arterial pressure), limit operating time, use BIPOLAR energy with SALINE, and pre-treat large fibroids with a GnRH analogue.**
- **Other hysteroscopic complications: UTERINE PERFORATION (the commonest — highest during cervical dilatation), bleeding, infection, gas embolism (with CO2 distension), and cervical laceration.**
- **The uterus is a small, thick-walled cavity — so any distension medium is under pressure against open vessels; this is a procedure in which the anaesthetist's fluid balance chart is as important as the surgeon's view.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Fluid deficit of 2000 mL → **pulmonary oedema**.

**REMEMBER:** *Watch the deficit, not the clock — saline drowns her, glycine drowns her and poisons the sodium.*

`Q11449`

---

## 111. Iron Prophylaxis and Anaemia Mukt Bharat

**PEARL:** Under **ANEMIA MUKT BHARAT, pregnant women receive one IRON–FOLIC ACID tablet (60 mg elemental iron + 500 µg folic acid) DAILY, starting at 14 WEEKS (after the first trimester), for at least 180 DAYS ANTENATALLY AND 180 DAYS (6 months) POSTPARTUM.** In **ANAEMIA (Hb <11 g/dL), the dose is DOUBLED to two tablets a day (therapeutic dose).**

**UNDERSTAND:** Iron is started **after the first trimester** for two reasons: **nausea and vomiting are worst then (and iron worsens them, destroying compliance)**, and the **fetal iron demand is small early and rises steeply in the second half.** The postpartum continuation exists because **pregnancy plus delivery blood loss depletes stores for months**, and the interval to the next pregnancy in India is often short.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Anaemia definitions in pregnancy (WHO): <11 g/dL. MILD 10–10.9; MODERATE 7–9.9; SEVERE <7; VERY SEVERE <4 g/dL. Iron deficiency is the commonest cause in India, followed by folate deficiency and haemoglobinopathies.**
- **Anaemia contributes to ~20–40% of maternal deaths in India (directly through cardiac failure and haemorrhage tolerance, and indirectly through sepsis) and causes preterm birth, low birth weight and perinatal loss.**
- **Route: ORAL iron is first-line. IV IRON (ferric carboxymaltose or iron sucrose) is used for moderate/severe anaemia after 12 weeks when oral iron fails, is not tolerated, or when time is short. BLOOD TRANSFUSION is reserved for Hb <7 g/dL near term, for decompensation, or for active bleeding.**
- **The six interventions of Anemia Mukt Bharat: prophylactic IFA supplementation, deworming (ALBENDAZOLE 400 mg after the first trimester), intensified year-round behaviour-change communication with delayed cord clamping, testing and treatment using digital methods (point-of-care haemoglobinometers), mandatory iron fortification of foods, and addressing non-nutritional causes such as malaria, fluorosis and haemoglobinopathies.**
- **Take iron with VITAMIN C (citrus) and AVOID tea, coffee, milk and calcium tablets at the same time (they inhibit absorption); warn about black stools and constipation.**
- **All pregnant women should also be screened for haemoglobinopathies (thalassaemia, sickle cell) in high-prevalence areas.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Correct statement regarding iron prophylaxis" → **6 months antepartum and 6 months postpartum**.

**REMEMBER:** *One tablet a day from fourteen weeks, six months before and six months after.*

`Q11452`

---

## 112. Perimortem Caesarean Section

**PEARL:** A **PERIMORTEM CAESAREAN uses a CLASSICAL (UPPER SEGMENT, VERTICAL) uterine incision, is performed AT THE SITE OF THE ARREST — NOT in an operating theatre — and is begun WITHIN 4 MINUTES of arrest with delivery by 5 MINUTES**, once CPR has failed to restore circulation. It is performed **PRIMARILY TO SAVE THE MOTHER.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The rationale is haemodynamic, not obstetric: **the gravid uterus obstructs the inferior vena cava and aorta, so effective CPR is impossible while it remains in place** — delivering it restores venous return and cardiac output and improves ventilation by relieving diaphragmatic splinting. **A classical vertical incision is used because it gives the fastest possible access without dissecting the bladder or forming a lower segment flap**; no anaesthesia, no antisepsis and no theatre transfer — moving the patient wastes the only minutes that matter.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Indicated when the uterus is at or above the UMBILICUS (roughly ≥20 weeks), where aortocaval compression is significant. Fetal survival is best if delivery occurs within 5 minutes of arrest and falls sharply thereafter, but the maternal indication stands regardless of fetal viability.**
- **CPR modifications in pregnancy: CONTINUOUS MANUAL LEFT UTERINE DISPLACEMENT (rather than tilting the table, which degrades compression quality), hand position slightly higher on the sternum, EARLY intubation (pregnant women desaturate rapidly and have a difficult airway), and standard defibrillation energies and drugs.**
- **Causes of maternal collapse — the "BEAU-CHOPS"/"A-H" list: Anaesthetic complications, Bleeding, Cardiac disease, Drugs, Embolism (thrombotic or AMNIOTIC FLUID), Fever/sepsis, General (the 4 Hs and 4 Ts), Hypertension (eclampsia, intracranial haemorrhage).**
- **Continue CPR during and after the delivery; the mother often improves dramatically as the uterus is emptied.**
- **Compare the CLASSICAL incision's other indications: extreme prematurity with an undeveloped lower segment, transverse lie with the back down, anterior placenta praevia/accreta, large lower-segment fibroids, and a densely adherent bladder. Its cost is a MUCH HIGHER RUPTURE RISK (4–9%) in a future pregnancy — so a previous classical incision mandates ELECTIVE REPEAT CAESAREAN and CONTRAINDICATES a trial of labour, whereas a previous LOWER SEGMENT incision carries only ~0.2–0.7% risk and permits TOLAC in a unit with facilities for IMMEDIATE emergency caesarean.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "True regarding perimortem caesarean" → **upper segment (classical) caesarean section**.

**REMEMBER:** *Cut where she lies, cut vertically, and do it to save the mother.*

`Q11592, Q11599`

---

## 113. The Cardinal Movements of Labour

**PEARL:** The sequence is **ENGAGEMENT → DESCENT → FLEXION → INTERNAL ROTATION → EXTENSION (with CROWNING) → RESTITUTION → EXTERNAL ROTATION → EXPULSION.** So the order asked in exams — **ENGAGEMENT, then CROWNING, then RESTITUTION, then EXTERNAL ROTATION** — follows directly.

**UNDERSTAND:** Each movement solves one geometric problem. **FLEXION presents the smallest diameter (suboccipitobregmatic, 9.5 cm). INTERNAL ROTATION aligns the head's anteroposterior diameter with the pelvis's widest OUTLET diameter (which is anteroposterior). EXTENSION is forced by the upward-and-forward curve of the birth canal (the CURVE OF CARUS) as the occiput pivots under the symphysis. RESTITUTION simply UNDOES the twist of the neck**, and **EXTERNAL ROTATION reflects the SHOULDERS internally rotating into the anteroposterior diameter** — the head merely follows them.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **CROWNING is the moment the head no longer recedes between contractions and the widest diameter distends the vulval ring — the point at which an episiotomy, if needed, is made, and at which the perineum is guarded (RITGEN'S manoeuvre).**
- **RESTITUTION is a 45° untwisting; EXTERNAL ROTATION is a further 45° in the SAME direction. The anterior shoulder is then delivered by gentle DOWNWARD traction, and the posterior shoulder by UPWARD traction — reversing this order is a common cause of tears.**
- **The head engages in the TRANSVERSE or oblique diameter and delivers in the ANTEROPOSTERIOR — internal rotation of 45–135° accomplishes this. Occipitoposterior positions must rotate the full 135°.**
- **BURNS–MARSHALL and MAURICEAU–SMELLIE–VEIT are the equivalent manoeuvres for the after-coming head in BREECH delivery; LOVSET'S manoeuvre delivers extended arms.**
- **Signs of full dilatation and imminent delivery: the urge to bear down, anal gaping and perineal bulging, and the head visible at the vulva.**
- **Watch for the fetal heart after each contraction (intermittent auscultation for 60 seconds), and never rush the head — controlled delivery prevents perineal trauma and intracranial injury.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Sequence questions — memorise the order and where crowning falls (during extension).

**REMEMBER:** *Engage, descend, flex, rotate in, extend and crown, restitute, rotate out, expel.*

![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/09/13/screenshot-2024-09-12-124357.jpg)

`Q11604, Q11603`

## 114. Thyroid Function in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **TOTAL T3 and TOTAL T4 RISE throughout pregnancy** (oestrogen increases THYROXINE-BINDING GLOBULIN), while **FREE T3 and FREE T4 remain essentially UNCHANGED** — so **only FREE hormone levels should be used to assess thyroid function in pregnancy.** **TSH FALLS slightly in the FIRST TRIMESTER** because **hCG cross-reacts with the TSH receptor**, then normalises.

**UNDERSTAND:** Oestrogen increases the **sialylation of TBG, prolonging its half-life**, so the carrier pool expands; the thyroid compensates by making more hormone to keep the free fraction constant. Meanwhile **hCG shares a common α-subunit with TSH** and weakly stimulates its receptor — which is why **TSH is lowest exactly when hCG peaks (8–11 weeks)**, and why **hCG-driven thyrotoxicosis occurs in hyperemesis gravidarum and molar pregnancy.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Trimester-specific TSH reference ranges must be used** (roughly <2.5 mIU/L in the first trimester); applying non-pregnant ranges over-diagnoses and under-diagnoses.
- **IODINE requirement RISES in pregnancy (250 µg/day) because of increased renal clearance and fetal demand** — deficiency causes maternal goitre and, critically, **CRETINISM (severe irreversible neurodevelopmental impairment) in the child.**
- **HYPOTHYROIDISM: increase the levothyroxine dose by 25–30% as soon as pregnancy is confirmed, and monitor TSH every 4–6 weeks. Untreated hypothyroidism causes miscarriage, pre-eclampsia, abruption, preterm birth, low birth weight and impaired child IQ. THE FETAL THYROID DOES NOT FUNCTION UNTIL 12 WEEKS — until then the fetal brain depends entirely on MATERNAL T4, which is why first-trimester treatment matters most.**
- **HYPERTHYROIDISM: PROPYLTHIOURACIL in the FIRST trimester (carbimazole/methimazole causes APLASIA CUTIS and choanal/oesophageal atresia — "methimazole embryopathy"), then SWITCH TO CARBIMAZOLE from the second trimester (PTU carries a risk of hepatotoxicity). Use the LOWEST dose that keeps free T4 in the HIGH-NORMAL range — the drugs cross the placenta and can cause fetal hypothyroidism and goitre.** **RADIOIODINE IS ABSOLUTELY CONTRAINDICATED.**
- **In GRAVES' DISEASE, maternal TSH RECEPTOR ANTIBODIES CROSS THE PLACENTA and can cause FETAL/NEONATAL THYROTOXICOSIS even in a mother rendered euthyroid or previously ablated — measure TRAb in the second trimester and monitor the fetal heart rate and growth.**
- **POSTPARTUM THYROIDITIS affects ~5%: a transient thyrotoxic phase followed by a hypothyroid phase, associated with anti-TPO antibodies.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "What is altered throughout pregnancy" → **total T3 (and total T4)**.

**REMEMBER:** *More carrier protein, more total hormone, the same free hormone — and hCG nudges the TSH down early.*

`Q11606`

---

## 115. External Cephalic Version

**PEARL:** **PRIMIGRAVIDITY IS NOT A CONTRAINDICATION to ECV** (only technically harder). **Contraindications: PLACENTA PRAEVIA or other antepartum haemorrhage, RUPTURED MEMBRANES/oligohydramnios, MULTIPLE PREGNANCY, any indication for caesarean anyway, a non-reassuring fetal heart, major uterine anomaly, severe pre-eclampsia, and recent (<1 week) bleeding.** It is performed at **≥36–37 weeks**, in a unit with **facilities for immediate caesarean.**

**UNDERSTAND:** ECV is timed at 36–37 weeks for two reasons: **spontaneous version still occurs before then (so earlier attempts are often unnecessary), and if the procedure precipitates a complication, the baby is mature enough to be delivered safely.** Its success (~50–60%) depends on **adequate liquor, a non-engaged breech, a relaxed uterus and multiparity** — hence the routine use of **TOCOLYSIS (terbutaline)** and, in some units, regional analgesia.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **ANTI-D MUST BE GIVEN to Rh-negative women after ECV** (attempted or successful), with a Kleihauer test.
- **Complications (~1–2%): transient fetal heart rate abnormalities (common and usually self-limiting), placental abruption, fetomaternal haemorrhage, cord accident and, rarely, emergency caesarean — so a CTG is done before and after.**
- **Breech types: FRANK/extended (hips flexed, knees extended — the commonest and best for vaginal delivery), COMPLETE/flexed, and FOOTLING (highest risk of CORD PROLAPSE — deliver by caesarean).**
- **The TERM BREECH TRIAL established that PLANNED CAESAREAN reduces perinatal mortality and morbidity for term breech — but vaginal breech delivery remains reasonable in selected cases with an experienced operator: frank/complete breech, estimated weight 2.5–3.5 kg, flexed head, adequate pelvis and no fetal compromise.**
- **In vaginal breech delivery: "HANDS OFF THE BREECH" until the scapulae are visible; LOVSET'S manoeuvre for extended arms; BURNS–MARSHALL or MAURICEAU–SMELLIE–VEIT (or PIPER FORCEPS) for the after-coming head. NEVER pull on the breech.**
- **Causes of breech: prematurity (the commonest overall), placenta praevia, polyhydramnios and oligohydramnios, uterine anomalies and fibroids, multiple pregnancy, fetal anomalies (hydrocephalus, anencephaly) and contracted pelvis.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Not a contraindication to ECV" → **primigravida**.

**REMEMBER:** *Thirty-six weeks, tocolysis, anti-D and a theatre next door — and being a first pregnancy is no barrier.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/09/13/screenshot-2024-09-12-123650.jpg)

`Q11607`

## 116. Sertoli-Cell-Only Syndrome

**PEARL:** A testicular biopsy showing **SEMINIFEROUS TUBULES LINED ONLY BY SERTOLI CELLS, with COMPLETE ABSENCE OF GERM CELLS and a thickened tubular basement membrane, is SERTOLI-CELL-ONLY SYNDROME (DEL CASTILLO syndrome)** — a cause of **NON-OBSTRUCTIVE AZOOSPERMIA** in men aged 20–40 presenting with primary infertility. **FSH is HIGH, testosterone and LH are usually NORMAL, and testicular volume is small-to-normal.**

**UNDERSTAND:** FSH is raised because **Sertoli cells deprived of germ cells produce little INHIBIN B**, removing the negative feedback — so **FSH is effectively a serum assay of germ cell presence.** Testosterone stays normal because **Leydig cells are unaffected**, which is why these men are **normally virilised with normal libido and no clinical hypogonadism** — the only abnormality is infertility.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Causes: congenital (germ cell aplasia), Y-CHROMOSOME AZFa MICRODELETIONS (which predict NO sperm retrieval — the one genetic result that makes surgery futile), Klinefelter syndrome, cryptorchidism, previous chemotherapy or radiotherapy, mumps orchitis, and toxins/heat.**
- **The pattern may be FOCAL, so MICRO-TESE (microdissection testicular sperm extraction) can still retrieve sperm in ~20–40% for ICSI — except with AZFa/AZFb deletions, where retrieval rates are essentially zero.**
- **ALWAYS PERFORM A KARYOTYPE AND Y-MICRODELETION ANALYSIS before ICSI in non-obstructive azoospermia — the abnormality is transmitted to male offspring.**
- **Distinguish histologically: OBSTRUCTIVE azoospermia — NORMAL, complete spermatogenesis on biopsy. MATURATION ARREST — germ cells present but stopping at a particular stage. HYALINISATION/tubular sclerosis — end-stage, as in Klinefelter syndrome.**
- **KLINEFELTER SYNDROME (47,XXY) is the commonest genetic cause of male infertility: small firm testes, tall stature with long limbs, gynaecomastia, high FSH and LH with low testosterone, and hyalinised tubules — sperm can still sometimes be retrieved by micro-TESE.**
- **There is no medical treatment that restores spermatogenesis in germ cell aplasia — the options are ICSI with retrieved sperm, donor sperm, or adoption.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Biopsy with tubules containing only Sertoli cells → **Sertoli-cell-only syndrome**.

**REMEMBER:** *No germ cells means no inhibin — the FSH rises while the testosterone stays normal.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/04/02/picture9_PMXBtsB.jpg)

`Q11608`

## 117. Fibroid Polyp

**PEARL:** A woman with **HEAVY MENSTRUAL BLEEDING and "something coming down", with a PALPABLE UTERUS on bimanual examination and a mass protruding through a cervix that admits one finger, has a FIBROID (submucous) POLYP** — a pedunculated submucous fibroid extruded through the cervical canal. **The palpable uterus and the identifiable cervix around the mass EXCLUDE UTERINE INVERSION.**

**UNDERSTAND:** A submucous fibroid on a pedicle is **squeezed towards the cavity by myometrial contractions**, progressively dilating the cervix until it delivers into the vagina — a process indistinguishable in symptoms from prolapse, which is why the **bimanual finding is decisive**: in **INVERSION there is NO uterus felt abdominally (a cup-shaped depression instead) and the patient is shocked out of proportion to blood loss**, whereas here the uterus is present and the cervical rim is felt all around the mass.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Complications of a fibroid polyp: torrential bleeding, infection and necrosis (offensive discharge), ulceration, and rarely inversion of the uterus if the pedicle is fundal.**
- **Management: TWIST-AND-AVULSION or VAGINAL MYOMECTOMY for a small pedunculated polyp with a thin pedicle; HYSTEROSCOPIC RESECTION for intracavitary fibroids; hysterectomy if childbearing is complete and the uterus is bulky.**
- **The differential of a mass at the introitus: fibroid polyp, ENDOMETRIAL POLYP, cervical polyp, uterovaginal PROLAPSE, chronic UTERINE INVERSION, a Gartner's cyst, and — in a postmenopausal woman — carcinoma or sarcoma.**
- **SUBMUCOUS fibroids are the most SYMPTOMATIC type (menorrhagia, infertility, recurrent miscarriage) despite being the least common; INTRAMURAL is the commonest type; SUBSEROSAL fibroids cause pressure effects and may become PEDUNCULATED or PARASITIC.**
- **A rapidly growing fibroid, especially POSTMENOPAUSAL growth, raises LEIOMYOSARCOMA — but this is rare (<0.5%).**
- **Always examine for anaemia and treat it — chronic menorrhagia from fibroids is a leading cause of iron deficiency in Indian women.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Mass through the cervix with a palpable uterus → **fibroid polyp** (not inversion).

**REMEMBER:** *Feel the uterus abdominally and the cervical rim vaginally — then it is a polyp, not an inversion.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/09/13/screenshot-2024-09-12-124438.jpg)

`Q11610`

## 118. Tubal Sterilisation — Site and Technique

**PEARL:** The **ISTHMUS is the commonest site of tubal ligation** — chosen because it is the **NARROWEST part of the tube**, making occlusion secure while **preserving the ampulla and fimbria** so that **REVERSAL (recanalisation) remains possible with the best success rates.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Tubal segments differ functionally: the **AMPULLA is where fertilisation occurs and the FIMBRIAE capture the ovum**, so destroying them precludes any future reversal. The **isthmus is narrow, thick-walled and muscular**, so a small excision reliably occludes it while leaving **long healthy segments on both sides for later anastomosis** — the principle that governs the choice of technique as well as the site.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Techniques: POMEROY (a loop of isthmus ligated with plain catgut and excised — simple, effective, and the standard in minilaparotomy and postpartum sterilisation), MODIFIED POMEROY, PARKLAND, IRVING and UCHIDA (the last two with the lowest failure rates), and MADLENER (obsolete, high failure). Laparoscopic: FALOPE RING (Yoon), FILSHIE CLIP and HULKA CLIP — the CLIPS DESTROY THE LEAST TUBE and are the most reversible.**
- **FAILURE RATE ~1 in 200 (0.5%) over 10 years; when it fails, the pregnancy has a DISPROPORTIONATELY HIGH CHANCE OF BEING ECTOPIC — so any pregnancy after sterilisation is ectopic until proved otherwise.**
- **Timing: INTERVAL (any time, ideally the follicular phase after excluding pregnancy), POSTPARTUM (within 7 days — the tube is easily reached just below the umbilicus), or with caesarean/concurrent with MTP.**
- **Counselling is essential: sterilisation must be regarded as PERMANENT and IRREVERSIBLE; written informed consent from the woman alone is sufficient (the SPOUSE'S CONSENT IS NOT REQUIRED under Indian law).**
- **Complications: bleeding, injury to bowel/bladder/vessels, anaesthetic risk, failure, and POST-TUBAL LIGATION SYNDROME (disputed). It does NOT cause menstrual disturbance or early menopause.**
- **REVERSAL by microsurgical anastomosis succeeds best after CLIP or ring occlusion of the ISTHMUS with ≥4 cm of residual tube; otherwise IVF is preferred.**
- **Tubal ligation is PROTECTIVE against ovarian cancer, and OPPORTUNISTIC SALPINGECTOMY is increasingly performed instead, since high-grade serous cancers arise in the fimbria.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Most common site and reason" → **isthmus, because it is the narrowest part**.

**REMEMBER:** *Tie the narrow isthmus and leave the ampulla alone — that is what keeps reversal possible.*

`Q11611`

---

## 119. Fetal Testosterone and Sexual Differentiation

**PEARL:** **hCG FROM THE PLACENTA is the INITIAL stimulus for testosterone production by the fetal Leydig cells** (hCG and LH share a receptor); **from about the 8th–12th week, FETAL PITUITARY LH takes over.** Sex determination itself depends on the **SRY gene on the Y chromosome**, which converts the indifferent gonad into a testis; **female development occurs in its ABSENCE — the "default" pathway.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The fetal pituitary is not yet functional when masculinisation must begin, so **the placenta supplies the trophic signal** — an elegant example of the fetoplacental unit acting as one endocrine organ. The consequence is examinable: **the SECOND-TRIMESTER genital development is hCG-dependent, whereas THIRD-TRIMESTER penile growth and TESTICULAR DESCENT depend on fetal pituitary LH** — which is why boys with **hypogonadotrophic hypogonadism (Kallmann's) are born with a MICROPENIS and undescended testes but NORMAL male genital structures.**

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The two testicular products and their targets: SERTOLI cells make ANTI-MÜLLERIAN HORMONE, which REGRESSES the Müllerian ducts (uterus, tubes, upper vagina); LEYDIG cells make TESTOSTERONE, which maintains the WOLFFIAN ducts (epididymis, vas, seminal vesicles), and — after conversion by 5α-REDUCTASE to DHT — masculinises the EXTERNAL genitalia and prostate.**
- **Timing rules everything: external genital masculinisation occurs at 8–12 weeks, so ANDROGEN EXCESS BEFORE 12 WEEKS (congenital adrenal hyperplasia) causes LABIOSCROTAL FUSION, while exposure after 12 weeks causes only clitoromegaly.**
- **Ovarian development requires TWO X chromosomes for maintenance — in Turner syndrome (45,X) the ovary forms but degenerates into a STREAK by birth.**
- **hCG is also the basis of pregnancy testing (detectable in serum ~8–9 days post-fertilisation, doubling every 48 hours in early normal pregnancy, peaking at 8–10 weeks), maintains the CORPUS LUTEUM until the luteoplacental shift at 7–9 weeks, and — because it shares the α-subunit with TSH — can cause thyroid stimulation.**
- **AMH is also produced by the granulosa cells of the adult ovary and is the best marker of OVARIAN RESERVE.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "What stimulates fetal testosterone production" → **hCG from the placenta**.

**REMEMBER:** *The placenta starts the testis working; the fetal pituitary takes over later.*

`Q11612`

---

## 120. Tumour Markers in Ovarian Masses

**PEARL:** In evaluating an ovarian mass, the markers used together are **CA-125 (epithelial, especially serous), LDH (DYSGERMINOMA), AFP (YOLK SAC/endodermal sinus tumour, and embryonal carcinoma), hCG (CHORIOCARCINOMA, embryonal carcinoma, dysgerminoma with syncytiotrophoblastic cells), and CA 19-9 (MUCINOUS tumours and mature cystic teratoma)** — plus **INHIBIN and AMH (GRANULOSA cell tumour)** and **CEA (mucinous/metastatic gastrointestinal).**

**UNDERSTAND:** The panel exists because **the ovary can give rise to tumours of THREE different tissue lineages** — surface epithelium, germ cells and sex-cord/stroma — and each secretes different products. **A young woman with a solid ovarian mass needs germ-cell markers (AFP, hCG, LDH); a postmenopausal woman with a complex cystic mass and ascites needs CA-125.** Age plus imaging directs which markers matter.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **CA-125 IS NOT A SCREENING TEST — it is raised in endometriosis, fibroids, PID, pregnancy, menstruation, cirrhosis with ascites, tuberculosis, heart failure and any peritoneal irritation, and it is NORMAL in up to 50% of early-stage ovarian cancers. Its value is in MONITORING treatment response and detecting recurrence.**
- **The RISK OF MALIGNANCY INDEX (RMI) = ultrasound score × menopausal score × CA-125; an RMI >200 warrants referral to a gynaecological oncologist. ROMA combines CA-125 and HE4.**
- **Ultrasound features suggesting malignancy: solid areas, thick septa, papillary projections, bilaterality, ascites and increased vascularity with low resistance on Doppler.**
- **STRUMA OVARII (thyroid tissue in a teratoma) can cause hyperthyroidism; a MEIGS SYNDROME (benign FIBROMA + ascites + pleural effusion, with a raised CA-125) is the great mimic of malignancy; KRUKENBERG TUMOUR is a bilateral metastasis from gastric/colonic signet-ring carcinoma.**
- **MATURE CYSTIC TERATOMA (DERMOID) is the commonest OVARIAN TUMOUR IN THE REPRODUCTIVE AGE GROUP, the commonest GERM CELL tumour, the commonest ovarian tumour DIAGNOSED IN PREGNANCY, BILATERAL IN 10–15%, and the tumour MOST LIKELY TO UNDERGO TORSION. It contains ROKITANSKY'S PROTUBERANCE (with hair, teeth, sebaceous material) and is treated by CYSTECTOMY, avoiding spillage (chemical peritonitis).**
- **Malignant transformation of a dermoid (usually squamous carcinoma) is rare (~1–2%) and occurs in older women.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Which markers for this ovarian mass" → in practice **all of them**, because the panel covers all three lineages.

**REMEMBER:** *Three lineages, three sets of markers — age and imaging tell you which to send.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/09/13/screenshot-2024-09-12-124341.jpg)

`Q11613, Q11632`

---

## 121. Postmenopausal Bleeding

**PEARL:** The **COMMONEST CAUSE of postmenopausal bleeding is ATROPHIC (SENILE) ENDOMETRITIS/VAGINITIS — endometrial and vaginal atrophy — accounting for ~60%.** But **ENDOMETRIAL CARCINOMA accounts for ~10% and must be EXCLUDED IN EVERY CASE**: **any postmenopausal bleeding requires ENDOMETRIAL SAMPLING**, whatever the presumed cause.

**UNDERSTAND:** The two facts are not in conflict — **"commonest" and "must exclude" answer different questions.** Atrophic tissue bleeds because **oestrogen deficiency thins the epithelium, leaving superficial vessels exposed to minor trauma**. But because early endometrial cancer is curable and presents almost exclusively with bleeding, **the entire clinical policy is built around the 10%, not the 60%** — which is why exams ask both versions of the question.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Causes in order: atrophic vaginitis/endometritis (~60%), exogenous OESTROGEN/HRT and TAMOXIFEN (~15–20%), ENDOMETRIAL CANCER (~10%), endometrial or cervical POLYPS (~5%), endometrial hyperplasia, cervical carcinoma, and rarely ovarian oestrogen-secreting tumours (GRANULOSA CELL TUMOUR) or a bleeding disorder.**
- **Evaluation: speculum and bimanual examination, TRANSVAGINAL ULTRASOUND (endometrial thickness ≤4 mm has a high negative predictive value for cancer), and ENDOMETRIAL BIOPSY; HYSTEROSCOPY if bleeding persists or the biopsy is inadequate, since focal lesions are missed by blind sampling.**
- **TAMOXIFEN is an antagonist in the breast but a PARTIAL AGONIST in the endometrium — it increases endometrial polyps, hyperplasia and CARCINOMA (2–3-fold) and also raises VTE risk. RALOXIFENE, by contrast, is an antagonist in the endometrium as well as the breast and does NOT increase endometrial cancer.** **Women on tamoxifen need prompt investigation of ANY bleeding, but routine ultrasound surveillance of asymptomatic users is NOT recommended (tamoxifen causes subendometrial cystic change that mimics thickening).**
- **PYOMETRA — pus in a closed uterine cavity in an elderly woman — presents with discharge and pain and REQUIRES exclusion of malignancy after drainage.**
- **Postmenopausal women on HRT may have expected withdrawal bleeding on sequential regimens; ANY bleeding on a CONTINUOUS COMBINED regimen after the first 6 months requires investigation.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Most common cause of postmenopausal bleeding" → **senile/atrophic endometritis**; "increases the risk of endometrial cancer" → **tamoxifen**.

**REMEMBER:** *Sixty per cent is atrophy — but you biopsy for the ten per cent that is cancer.*

`Q11617, Q11627`

---

## 122. Septic Abortion

**PEARL:** **Fever, offensive purulent discharge, uterine tenderness and shock a week after an unsafe abortion = SEPTIC ABORTION with ENDOTOXIC SHOCK — and *ESCHERICHIA COLI* is the commonest causative organism.** Infection is usually **POLYMICROBIAL**, including anaerobes (*Bacteroides*, *Peptostreptococcus*), streptococci, and — in the most feared form — ***CLOSTRIDIUM PERFRINGENS*.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Unsafe abortion introduces **the woman's own vaginal and bowel flora into a necrotic, blood-filled uterine cavity — an ideal anaerobic culture medium** — and retained products act as a persistent nidus. The Gram-negative **ENDOTOXIN then produces the classic septic shock cascade**, which is why the treatment has two inseparable halves: **antibiotics AND EVACUATION.** Antibiotics alone fail while the nidus remains.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Grading: GRADE I — infection confined to the uterus. GRADE II — spread to the parametrium, tubes and pelvic peritoneum. GRADE III — generalised peritonitis, septicaemia, endotoxic shock and acute kidney injury.**
- **Management: resuscitation, blood and high vaginal cultures, BROAD-SPECTRUM TRIPLE ANTIBIOTICS (a beta-lactam + gentamicin/aminoglycoside + METRONIDAZOLE), and EVACUATION of the uterus once antibiotics are started; laparotomy/hysterectomy for perforation, gangrene, abscess or unresponsive sepsis. Give ANTI-D if Rh-negative and TETANUS PROPHYLAXIS.**
- **Complications: endotoxic shock, ACUTE KIDNEY INJURY and RENAL CORTICAL NECROSIS, DIC, ARDS, pelvic abscess, thrombophlebitis, and later ASHERMAN'S SYNDROME and tubal infertility.**
- ***CLOSTRIDIUM PERFRINGENS* causes the most fulminant form: gas in the tissues, a bronze discoloration of the skin, massive INTRAVASCULAR HAEMOLYSIS with haemoglobinuria and jaundice, and rapid renal failure — it demands HYSTERECTOMY.**
- **Unsafe abortion remains a major cause of maternal death in India, and its prevention is SAFE, LEGAL, ACCESSIBLE ABORTION SERVICES plus contraception — a public-health point as much as a clinical one.**
- **The differential for postpartum/post-abortal fever also includes retained products, endometritis, wound and urinary infection, mastitis and thrombophlebitis.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Most common organism in septic abortion" → ***E. coli***.

**REMEMBER:** *Antibiotics and evacuation together — the retained tissue is the infection.*

`Q11619`

---

## 123. Tocolytics

**PEARL:** **RITODRINE (a β2-agonist) causes maternal TACHYCARDIA, palpitations, tremor, HYPERGLYCAEMIA (not hypoglycaemia), hypokalaemia, hypocalcaemia, hypomagnesaemia and — the dangerous one — PULMONARY OEDEMA.** **Maternal HYPERGLYCAEMIA then causes fetal hyperinsulinaemia, so the NEONATE develops HYPOGLYCAEMIA.**

**UNDERSTAND:** All the effects are simply **β2-receptor stimulation outside the uterus**: β2 receptors on the heart and vessels cause tachycardia and vasodilatation; on the liver and muscle they drive **GLYCOGENOLYSIS AND GLUCONEOGENESIS (hyperglycaemia)** and **drive potassium into cells (hypokalaemia)**. **Pulmonary oedema results from fluid retention, tachycardia and increased capillary permeability — and is much more likely with concurrent corticosteroids, multiple pregnancy and excessive IV fluids**, which is why fluid balance must be strict.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The purpose of tocolysis is NOT to prolong pregnancy indefinitely but to BUY 48 HOURS — enough for ANTENATAL CORTICOSTEROIDS to act and for IN-UTERO TRANSFER to a unit with neonatal facilities. That is the single most important concept.**
- **Current first-line agents: NIFEDIPINE (a calcium channel blocker — cheap, oral, effective; avoid combining with magnesium) and ATOSIBAN (an oxytocin receptor antagonist — the best side-effect profile, but expensive). INDOMETHACIN is effective below 32 weeks but risks PREMATURE CLOSURE OF THE DUCTUS ARTERIOSUS and OLIGOHYDRAMNIOS. β-agonists are now largely abandoned because of maternal toxicity.**
- **CONTRAINDICATIONS to tocolysis: chorioamnionitis, severe pre-eclampsia/eclampsia, significant antepartum haemorrhage or abruption, intrauterine fetal death or lethal anomaly, non-reassuring fetal status, and advanced cervical dilatation.**
- **ANTENATAL CORTICOSTEROIDS (betamethasone 12 mg × 2, 24 hours apart, or dexamethasone 6 mg × 4, 12-hourly) are given between 24 and 34 weeks (and up to 36 weeks in selected cases) — they reduce RESPIRATORY DISTRESS SYNDROME, INTRAVENTRICULAR HAEMORRHAGE, NECROTISING ENTEROCOLITIS and NEONATAL DEATH. Maximum benefit is 24 hours to 7 days after the course.**
- **MAGNESIUM SULPHATE below 32 weeks is given for FETAL NEUROPROTECTION (reducing cerebral palsy), not primarily as a tocolytic.**
- **Also give GROUP B STREPTOCOCCUS PROPHYLAXIS in preterm labour, and consider antibiotics only if membranes are ruptured (not for intact-membrane preterm labour, where they do not help).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Ritodrine causes all EXCEPT" → **hypoglycaemia** (it causes maternal HYPERglycaemia; the NEONATE gets hypoglycaemia).

**REMEMBER:** *Tocolysis buys forty-eight hours for the steroids — and beta-agonists flood the lungs while raising the sugar.*

`Q11624`

---

## 124. Oogenesis — Where the Arrests Occur

**PEARL:** **After OVULATION the released egg is a SECONDARY OOCYTE ARRESTED IN METAPHASE OF MEIOSIS II** — it completes meiosis II **ONLY IF FERTILISED** (extruding the second polar body). Before that, the **PRIMARY oocyte has been arrested in PROPHASE OF MEIOSIS I since fetal life**, completing meiosis I only in the hours just before ovulation (triggered by the LH surge, extruding the FIRST polar body).

**UNDERSTAND:** The two arrests explain the two great features of female reproduction. **The prophase I arrest from fetal life means a woman's oocytes are as old as she is** — decades of arrest allow accumulated damage, which is why **NON-DISJUNCTION and aneuploidy rise steeply with maternal age.** The **metaphase II arrest** ensures the oocyte is **poised to complete division instantly on fertilisation**, without wasting resources beforehand.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **The timeline: OOGONIA multiply by mitosis in fetal life, peaking at ~6–7 MILLION at 20 weeks; ~1–2 MILLION remain at BIRTH (all arrested in prophase I as primary oocytes); ~400,000 at PUBERTY; and only ~400 will ever ovulate. NO NEW OOCYTES ARE FORMED AFTER BIRTH — the opposite of spermatogenesis.**
- **Each meiotic division in the female is UNEQUAL, conserving cytoplasm in one cell and discarding the rest as POLAR BODIES — so one primary oocyte yields ONE ovum (versus four spermatozoa from one primary spermatocyte).**
- **Prophase I arrest is at the DIPLOTENE (dictyate) stage, maintained by oocyte maturation inhibitor from granulosa cells; the LH surge releases it.**
- **FERTILISATION occurs in the AMPULLA; the sperm must undergo CAPACITATION in the female tract and then the ACROSOME REACTION; ZP3 on the zona pellucida is the sperm receptor, and the CORTICAL REACTION then blocks polyspermy.**
- **Landmarks after fertilisation: zygote → morula (day 3–4) → blastocyst (day 5) → IMPLANTATION begins day 6 and is complete by day 8–10 → hCG detectable in serum ~day 8–9.**
- **Spermatogenesis takes ~74 days and continues throughout life, which is why a repeat semen analysis is done after 3 months.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "After ovulation the egg is" → **a secondary oocyte arrested in meiosis II**.

**REMEMBER:** *Arrested in prophase I since before she was born, and in metaphase II until the sperm arrives.*

`Q11630`

---

## 125. Unexplained Infertility

**PEARL:** When the **semen analysis is normal, ovulation is documented, and the tubes are patent on HSG, the diagnosis is UNEXPLAINED INFERTILITY — which accounts for about 25% (10–30%) of infertile couples.** Overall causation: **male factor ~30–40%, ovulatory ~25%, tubal/peritoneal ~25–35%, uterine/cervical ~5–10%, and unexplained ~25%** (with overlap, since many couples have more than one factor).

**UNDERSTAND:** "Unexplained" means **the standard tests were normal, not that nothing is wrong.** The routine work-up cannot detect **oocyte quality, fertilisation capacity, sperm function despite normal morphology, subtle endometriosis, implantation failure or gamete transport problems** — which is exactly why treatment is **EMPIRICAL and works by INCREASING THE NUMBER OF GAMETES BROUGHT TOGETHER** (superovulation and IUI) or by **BYPASSING the unknown step altogether** (IVF/ICSI).

**MUST KNOW:**
- **INFERTILITY is defined as failure to conceive after 12 MONTHS of regular unprotected intercourse — but evaluate EARLIER (after 6 months) if the woman is over 35, or immediately with obvious pathology (amenorrhoea, known tubal disease, previous chemotherapy, or severe male factor).**
- **The three essential tests: SEMEN ANALYSIS, confirmation of OVULATION (mid-luteal progesterone), and TUBAL PATENCY (HSG). Add TVS for ovarian reserve (antral follicle count, AMH) and uterine anatomy. Routine laparoscopy is no longer first-line.**
- **Management of unexplained infertility is a stepwise escalation: EXPECTANT management with timed intercourse and lifestyle optimisation (weight, smoking, alcohol, folic acid) for 6–12 months in young couples → OVARIAN STIMULATION (clomiphene/letrozole) WITH INTRAUTERINE INSEMINATION for 3–6 cycles → IVF.** **Female AGE is the single strongest predictor of success at every step.**
- **Ovulation induction agents: LETROZOLE (an aromatase inhibitor — now first-line in PCOS, with higher live-birth rates than clomiphene), CLOMIPHENE CITRATE, gonadotrophins, and metformin as an adjunct in PCOS. The main risks are MULTIPLE PREGNANCY and OHSS.**
- **Lifestyle matters and is examinable: obesity, smoking, alcohol, excessive caffeine, heat exposure and anabolic steroid use all reduce fertility, and correcting them is free.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** All tests normal → **unexplained infertility, ~25%**.

**REMEMBER:** *Normal tests do not mean normal fertility — a quarter of couples have no answer, and age drives everything.*

`Q11791`

---

## 126. Broad Ligament

**PEARL:** The **ovarian ligament lies in the POSTERIOR fold**; the **ureter runs in the inferomedial part**, crossed above by the **uterine artery** — "**water under the bridge**". The broad ligament is **NOT a support of the uterus**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The broad ligament is only a **double fold of peritoneum draped over the tube, round ligament and ovarian ligament** — it has no fibromuscular strength. The true supports are fibromuscular condensations of pelvic fascia and the muscular floor beneath.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **True supports of the uterus:** **levator ani (most important)** + **cardinal (Mackenrodt's) ligament**, **uterosacral ligament**, **pubocervical ligament**. The **round ligament maintains anteversion** but is not a support.
- **The ureter is ~1.5–2 cm lateral to the cervix at the level of the internal os**, where the uterine artery crosses **anterior and above** it — the single commonest site of ureteric injury in **hysterectomy** and in **caesarean section for a broad-ligament haematoma**.
- **Parts:** **mesosalpinx** (above the ovarian ligament, containing the tube and the vestigial **epoophoron/paroophoron**), **mesovarium**, **mesometrium** (largest), and the **infundibulopelvic (suspensory) ligament** carrying the **ovarian vessels**.
- **Clamping the infundibulopelvic ligament** (as in oophorectomy) also risks the ureter, which lies just beneath it at the pelvic brim.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "False about contents of the broad ligament" → the ovarian ligament in the **anterior** fold (it is posterior).

**REMEMBER:** *Water (ureter) under the bridge (uterine artery); the broad ligament drapes but does not support.*

`Q10485`

---

## 127. Peg Cells

**PEARL:** **Peg (intercalary) cells are the non-ciliated SECRETORY cells of the fallopian tube** — narrow, dark cells wedged between the pale ciliated cells.

**UNDERSTAND:** The tube needs both: **ciliated cells sweep the ovum toward the uterus** and **peg cells secrete the nutrient fluid** that sustains the ovum and capacitates the sperm. Both populations are **oestrogen-dependent** and vary through the cycle — ciliated cells predominate at ovulation.

**MUST KNOW:**
- The **ampulla is the site of fertilisation** and has the most abundant ciliation and the most complex mucosal folds (**plicae**).
- **Damage to the ciliated epithelium — by PID (Chlamydia, gonorrhoea), endometriosis, or tubal surgery — is the leading cause of ECTOPIC PREGNANCY**; the ampulla is the commonest site of implantation (~70%).
- **Kartagener syndrome** (immotile cilia) → subfertility and increased ectopic risk in women.
- **Smoking** impairs ciliary function and independently raises ectopic risk.
- The tube's four parts, lateral → medial: **infundibulum (fimbriae) → ampulla (longest and widest) → isthmus (narrowest) → interstitial/intramural**. **Interstitial (cornual) ectopics rupture latest (~8–12 weeks) but bleed most catastrophically.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Peg cells are seen in" → **fallopian tube**.

**REMEMBER:** *Pale ciliated cells sweep, dark peg cells feed — damage them and you get an ectopic.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://pub-8c30f7a873214da0837b787a0c529b95.r2.dev/b6b0ae93fe9a283a4a8edd72673502b2.webp)

`Q10486`

## 128. Space of Retzius

**PEARL:** The **retropubic = prevesical space of Retzius** is the extraperitoneal space entered in **Burch colposuspension** for stress urinary incontinence.

**UNDERSTAND:** It is a **potential space between the pubic bone and the bladder**, filled with loose areolar tissue and fat — which is exactly why it can be developed bluntly and why bladder rupture or pelvic fracture haematoma tracks into it.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Burch colposuspension** places non-absorbable sutures from the **paravaginal fascia to Cooper's (iliopectineal) ligament**, elevating the bladder neck. It is the classical open operation; the **mid-urethral sling (TVT/TOT)** is now first-line surgery.
- The space contains the **prostatic/vesical venous plexus of Santorini** — the source of torrential bleeding in radical prostatectomy.
- **Extraperitoneal bladder rupture** (pelvic fracture) fills this space → managed by **catheter drainage alone**; **intraperitoneal rupture** (blow to a full bladder) requires **surgical repair**.
- Do not confuse with the **presacral space** (between rectum/peritoneum and sacrum), which contains the **hypogastric plexuses** and is the site of catastrophic presacral venous bleeding in rectal surgery.
- **Stress incontinence workup:** demonstrate leak on coughing, **Q-tip test** for urethral hypermobility, and **urodynamics** if mixed symptoms.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Space of Retzius is also known as" → **prevesical / retropubic** (the trap options pair it with "presacral").

**REMEMBER:** *Retzius is in front of the bladder, behind the pubis — the incontinence surgeon's space.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://pub-8c30f7a873214da0837b787a0c529b95.r2.dev/3477f43bee00156d36cee3bf0202b5ad.webp)

`Q10489`

## 129. Caldwell–Moloy Pelvic Types

**PEARL:** **Gynaecoid** — round, wide subpubic angle (~85°), non-prominent spines: the **normal female pelvis, best for delivery**. **Anthropoid** — AP oval, head engages AP, **face-to-pubis (direct OP) delivery is commonest**. **Android** — heart/triangular, prominent spines, narrow angle, convergent walls: **arrest of descent and deep transverse arrest**. **Platypelloid** — flat/transversely oval: head engages transversely, **the rarest**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The classification is based on the **shape of the posterior segment** of the inlet (behind the widest transverse diameter). The engaging diameter of the fetal head simply matches the **roomiest diameter of that inlet** — which then predicts the position, the mechanism of labour and the likely complication.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Gynaecoid ~50%, android ~20%, anthropoid ~25%, platypelloid ~3%** (approximate; gynaecoid is commonest).
- **Android pelvis carries the worst prognosis** for vaginal delivery — the funnel shape causes **deep transverse arrest** and increases the need for caesarean section.
- **Obstetric conjugate (~10 cm)** is the true limiting AP diameter of the inlet and **cannot be measured clinically** — estimate it as **diagonal conjugate (~12 cm) minus 1.5–2 cm**.
- **The inlet's widest diameter is TRANSVERSE (13 cm); the outlet's widest is ANTEROPOSTERIOR (11–13 cm)** — which is why the head **engages transversely and delivers anteroposteriorly**, and why internal rotation must occur.
- Ischial spines = **station 0** and the landmark for a pudendal block.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Asked as "classification given by" (Caldwell and Moloy) and as feature-matching (face-to-pubis → anthropoid).

**REMEMBER:** *Gynaecoid delivers, android arrests, anthropoid goes face-to-pubis, platypelloid stays transverse.*

`Q10497`

---

## 130. Endometrial Thickness — The Numbers

**PEARL:** ~**11 mm at ovulation (day 14)**; **1–2 mm** in the early proliferative phase just after menstruation. In a **POSTMENOPAUSAL** woman, endometrium **>4 mm is abnormal and requires biopsy**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The cut-off exists because **endometrial carcinoma is the diagnosis you cannot afford to miss** in postmenopausal bleeding. A thin (≤4 mm) stripe has a **negative predictive value >99%** for cancer, so it can safely spare a woman a biopsy.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Postmenopausal bleeding = endometrial carcinoma until proven otherwise** — the single most important rule in gynaecology.
- **Investigation of choice: transvaginal ultrasound first**; if **ET >4 mm, or bleeding recurs regardless of thickness**, proceed to **endometrial biopsy (Pipelle) or hysteroscopy + biopsy** — the gold standard.
- **Tamoxifen** users get subendometrial cysts and a thickened stripe, so the 4 mm rule does not apply; investigate on **symptoms**, not thickness.
- In the reproductive years, an **ET <7 mm at ovulation** is associated with poor implantation in IVF.
- **Endometrial hyperplasia with atypia** carries ~**25–30% risk of concurrent/progression to carcinoma** → hysterectomy in the woman who has completed her family.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Cycle-day thickness matching, and the postmenopausal 4 mm cut-off.

**REMEMBER:** *11 mm at ovulation; over 4 mm after menopause means biopsy.*

`Q10484`

---

## 131. Vaccines in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** Give **inactivated INFLUENZA** (any trimester) and **Tdap** (ideally **27–36 weeks**). **All LIVE vaccines are contraindicated** (MMR, varicella, live attenuated influenza, oral polio, BCG, yellow fever), and **HPV is deferred** until after delivery.

**UNDERSTAND:** Two separate principles are at work. **Live vaccines are avoided** because of a theoretical risk of fetal infection. **Tdap is timed late** deliberately — giving it at 27–36 weeks maximises **transplacental IgG transfer**, protecting the newborn against **pertussis** in the months before its own primary immunisation begins.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Tdap is given in EVERY pregnancy**, regardless of previous doses — maternal antibody wanes quickly.
- **Tetanus toxoid / Td** is given in Indian practice as **2 doses 4 weeks apart** (or a single booster if immunised within 3 years) — a core UIP/antenatal-care point.
- **Rubella and varicella vaccines are given POSTPARTUM** if the woman is seronegative — and pregnancy should be avoided for **1 month** afterward. **Inadvertent MMR in pregnancy is NOT an indication for termination** (no proven teratogenicity).
- **Safe in pregnancy:** influenza (inactivated), Tdap, hepatitis B, rabies (post-exposure — never withhold), **tetanus**, COVID-19 vaccines.
- **Rho(D) immunoglobulin** is not a vaccine but the other essential antenatal injection — **at 28 weeks and within 72 hours of delivery** in Rh-negative women.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Multi-select — the correct combination is always **inactivated influenza + Tdap**, with live vaccines and HPV as the distractors.

**REMEMBER:** *Killed yes, live no; Tdap late so the baby gets the antibody.*

`Q11344`

---

## 132. Chorionicity: What Zygosity Allows

**PEARL:** **Dizygotic twins are ALWAYS dichorionic diamniotic** — two separate
fertilisations, two placentae. Only **monozygotic** twins can be monochorionic, and
**"monoamniotic dichorionic" does not exist.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Chorionicity is decided entirely by **when a single zygote splits**.
Dizygotic twins never shared anything, so each has its own chorion and amnion.
Monozygotic outcome depends on timing: the later the split, the more membranes are
already formed and therefore shared. This is why an amnion can never be shared while
chorions are separate — the chorion forms first.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Monozygotic splitting:** **0–3 days → DCDA** (~30%); **4–8 days → MCDA** (commonest, ~70%); **8–13 days → MCMA**; **>13 days → conjoined twins**.
- **Ultrasound signs (best assessed in the first trimester, 6–14 weeks):** **lambda / twin-peak sign = DICHORIONIC**; **T-sign (inverted T) = MONOCHORIONIC diamniotic**; a **thick (>2 mm) dividing membrane with 4 layers = DCDA**, thin 2-layer = MCDA; no membrane at all = MCMA.
- **Monochorionic = the dangerous placentation:** **twin-to-twin transfusion syndrome** (treated with fetoscopic laser ablation), TAPS, selective growth restriction, and if one twin dies, acute exsanguination into the dead co-twin causing neurological injury in the survivor.
- **MCMA** adds **cord entanglement** → delivery by caesarean at 32–34 weeks.
- Monochorionic twins are always **same sex**; **discordant sex on scan proves dizygosity and therefore DCDA**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Either the chorionicity of a dizygotic pregnancy ("only 4 — DCDA")
or an ultrasound image asking for the sign. **Lambda = two chorions; T = one.**

**REMEMBER:** *Two eggs can only make two placentas; one egg can make anything depending
on when it splits.*

![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/06/picture001.jpg)
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/06/picture2.jpg)

`Q9584, Q9585`

## 133. Diameters of the Fetal Skull

**PEARL:** The **mentovertical (13.5 cm) is the LONGEST** fetal skull diameter — presented
in **brow presentation**, which is why brow is undeliverable vaginally at term. The
**suboccipitobregmatic (9.5 cm)** is the smallest engaging diameter and belongs to the
ideal **fully flexed vertex**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Every degree of deflexion swaps the presenting diameter for a longer one.
Complete flexion presents the smallest circumference; military (partial deflexion)
presents occipitofrontal; further extension reaches the mentovertical worst case; and
**complete extension (face presentation) paradoxically improves it again** to
submentobregmatic 9.5 cm — which is why face presentation with a mento-anterior position
can deliver vaginally but brow cannot.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Diameter | Size | Presentation |
|---|---|---|
| **Suboccipitobregmatic** | **9.5 cm** | Fully flexed **vertex** |
| Suboccipitofrontal | 10 cm | Partly deflexed vertex |
| **Occipitofrontal** | **11.5 cm** | Military/deflexed vertex (occipito-posterior) |
| **Mentovertical** | **13.5 cm (longest)** | **Brow** |
| **Submentobregmatic** | **9.5 cm** | **Face** (mento-anterior) |
| **Biparietal** | **9.5 cm** | Widest transverse — used for dating/growth |
| Bitemporal | 8 cm | Smallest transverse |

- **Brow presentation must convert** (to vertex or face) or be delivered by **caesarean**.
- **Persistent mento-posterior face** is also undeliverable → caesarean.
- Engagement in a primigravida occurs when the **biparietal diameter passes the pelvic inlet**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straight recall of the longest/shortest diameter, or a matching-grid
image question. Learn the four sagittal diameters with their numbers — that answers every
version.

**REMEMBER:** *Flex to 9.5, deflex to 13.5 — the brow is the worst place to be.*

![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/13/screenshot-2024-01-13-154601_pOH5OXq.jpg)

`Q9589, Q9592`

## 134. Breech: Naming by Hip and Knee Position

**PEARL:** **Frank (extended) breech = FLEXION at the hip, EXTENSION at the knee** — legs
straight up alongside the trunk. It is the commonest breech and the safest for vaginal
delivery.

**UNDERSTAND:** All breech nomenclature reduces to two joints. Frank breech is safest
because the buttocks plus extended thighs form a **well-fitting, dilating presenting
part** that fills the lower segment; footling breech leaves gaps, so the cord can
prolapse and the cervix can trap the after-coming head.

**MUST KNOW:**

| Type | Hip | Knee | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Frank / extended** | **Flexed** | **Extended** | **Commonest (~65%)**, best prognosis |
| **Complete / flexed** | Flexed | Flexed | Cross-legged; more common in multiparas |
| **Footling** | **Extended** | Extended | **Highest cord prolapse risk** → caesarean |
| Kneeling | Extended | Flexed | Rare |

- **ECV (external cephalic version)** is offered at **36 weeks in nulliparas, 37 weeks in multiparas**; contraindications include APH, ruptured membranes, multiple pregnancy, major uterine anomaly, previous classical caesarean and any indication for caesarean anyway. Give **anti-D to Rh-negative women** after ECV.
- Vaginal breech manoeuvres: **Løvset** (delivery of extended arms by rotation), **Mauriceau–Smellie–Veit** and **Burns–Marshall** (after-coming head), **Pinard** (bringing down a leg), **Piper forceps** for the after-coming head.
- **Internal podalic version** is now essentially confined to the **delivery of a second twin in transverse lie**.
- Breech is associated with prematurity, placenta praevia, uterine anomalies, polyhydramnios/oligohydramnios and fetal anomalies; **hip dysplasia screening (USS) is indicated for all breech babies**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The hip/knee matching question, or "which breech carries the highest
risk of cord prolapse" (**footling**).

**REMEMBER:** *Frank is folded at the hip and straight at the knee — the "candlestick" baby.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/04/30/1_9cbh8wE.jpg)

## 135. The WHO Modified Partograph

**PEARL:** **Plotting begins at 4 cm** — the start of the **active phase**. The **alert
line** runs at **1 cm/hour** from 4 cm to 10 cm (7 hours); the **action line** is drawn
**4 hours to the right** of it, and crossing it demands intervention.

**UNDERSTAND:** The latent phase is highly variable in duration and cannot be judged
against a fixed slope, so plotting it would generate false alarms and unnecessary
caesareans. Once the cervix is 4 cm and contracting effectively, progress becomes
predictable enough for a straight line to be a valid standard.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Recorded on the partograph: **cervical dilatation (X) and descent of head in fifths (O)**, fetal heart rate, membranes/liquor colour (**I intact, C clear, M meconium, B blood, A absent**), moulding (0/+/++/+++), maternal pulse, BP, temperature, urine output, contractions per 10 min and oxytocin/drugs.
- Crossing the **alert line** → transfer from a peripheral unit to a facility with caesarean capability; crossing the **action line** → assess and intervene (augmentation, caesarean).
- **Descent is assessed abdominally in fifths palpable**, not by vaginal examination, to avoid infection.
- WHO's newer **Labour Care Guide (2020)** uses **5 cm** as the start of the active phase and drops the alert/action lines, but Indian PG exams still ask the classic **4 cm**.
- **Uterine tachysystole = more than 5 contractions in 10 minutes** (averaged over 30 min); **hyperstimulation = tachysystole PLUS a non-reassuring fetal heart trace** → stop the oxytocin, left lateral position, oxygen, IV fluids, consider **terbutaline** tocolysis.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Numbers are the whole question: **4 cm, 1 cm/hr, 4 hours to the
right, >5 contractions in 10 minutes.**

**REMEMBER:** *Four to start, one an hour, four to the right.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/17/picture5_x3sMtFz.jpg)

`Q9591, Q9595`

---

## 136. Early Pregnancy Ultrasound and the Threatened Abortion

**PEARL:** Bleeding with a **closed cervix and a viable intrauterine pregnancy** =
**threatened abortion**. On TVS, the **gestational sac appears at 4.5–5 weeks, the yolk
sac at 5.5 weeks, and cardiac activity at 6 weeks**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The milestones exist so you can decide whether an "empty" sac is
abnormal or simply early. A 5-week scan showing only a gestational sac is entirely
normal, so an empty sac at that gestation is not evidence of failure — which is why the
answer to a 5-week bleed with an intrauterine sac is threatened, not missed, abortion.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Types of abortion:** threatened (closed os, bleeding, viable) → inevitable (open os) → incomplete (retained products, open os) → complete (empty uterus, os closing) → **missed** (no cardiac activity, closed os, no expulsion) → septic.
- **Discriminatory zone:** with **β-hCG >1500–2000 mIU/mL**, an intrauterine sac **should** be visible on TVS; absence suggests **ectopic**.
- **Ectopic on TVS:** empty uterus (± **pseudosac**, a fluid collection in the midline cavity), **blob sign** (adnexal heterogeneous mass), **bagel/tubal ring sign**, free fluid in the pouch of Douglas. A **true gestational sac is eccentric with a double decidual ring**.
- **WHO/CDC define abortion as loss before 20 weeks or fetal weight <500 g** (India's MTP Act permits termination to 20 weeks, extendable to 24 weeks for defined categories).
- **Method of induced abortion by gestation:** **medical (mifepristone + misoprostol) up to 9 weeks; suction/vacuum aspiration 7–12 weeks**; 12–20 weeks → medical induction (misoprostol) or **D&E**. Ethacridine lactate and concentrated oxytocin are historical second-trimester methods.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The "10 weeks, best method" question → **vacuum aspiration** (medical
efficacy falls after 9 weeks). The 5-week bleeding scan → **threatened abortion**.

**REMEMBER:** *Sac 5, yolk 5.5, heart 6 — and a closed os means only threatened.*

![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/06/14/4_WiEESAd.jpg)
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/18/picture1_ZHsbau5.jpg)
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2024/01/18/screenshot-2024-01-18-105412_GeIfj1d.jpg)

## 137. Myomectomy: Reducing Blood Loss

**PEARL:** Every recognised measure reduces blood loss by **occluding the uterine blood
supply before the incision** — GnRH agonists, **Bonney's clamp/tourniquet at the level of
the uterine arteries**, uterine artery ligation or embolisation, and **vasopressin along
the planned serosal incision**. **Ligating vessels on the surface of the myoma itself does
NOT help** — the myoma has no capsule of its own vessels worth ligating and the bleeding
comes from the uterine bed.

**UNDERSTAND:** A fibroid is supplied by a pseudocapsule of vessels derived from the
myometrium; once enucleation begins, the bleeding is from the **myometrial bed**, not from
the tumour surface. Control therefore has to be **proximal** (inflow) or **local
vasoconstriction**, never at the tumour's own surface.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Preoperative:** **GnRH agonists** for 3 months shrink the fibroid ~35–50%, correct anaemia and reduce vascularity — but make the plane harder to find and can mask small fibroids. Ulipristal acetate (SPRM), iron and transfusion also used; **uterine artery embolisation** is an alternative to surgery entirely (not for women wanting future fertility, per most guidance).
- **Intraoperative:** Bonney's myomectomy clamp, a Foley catheter/rubber tourniquet around the lower segment, **vasopressin** injection (caution — reports of bradycardia and cardiac arrest), tranexamic acid, and a **single anterior midline vertical incision** through which multiple fibroids are removed.
- The **myoma screw** is the instrument that grasps and provides traction on the fibroid during enucleation.
- Complications: haemorrhage, adhesions, recurrence (~15–30%), and **uterine rupture in a subsequent pregnancy** if the cavity was breached → these women deliver by **elective caesarean**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "All reduce blood loss EXCEPT" — the odd one out is always the local
measure at the myoma surface. Also image identification of the **myoma screw**.

**REMEMBER:** *Control the inflow, not the tumour's own skin.*


**IMAGES:**
![question](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/06/15/2.jpg)
![explanation](https://media.cerebellumacademy.com/media/public/images/2023/06/15/2.jpg)

`Q9587, Q9588`
