## 171. Managing an Abnormal Pap Smear

**PEARL:** **LSIL in a woman over 25 → COLPOSCOPY**; if the lesion **extends into the
endocervical canal and cannot be fully visualised (an UNSATISFACTORY/inadequate colposcopy),
perform ENDOCERVICAL CURETTAGE** to exclude occult high-grade disease. **LEEP is not
performed for LSIL** — it carries avoidable obstetric risk.

**UNDERSTAND:** Colposcopy is only as good as **what can be seen**. The **transformation zone
recedes into the canal with age and after treatment**, so a lesion running out of sight makes
the examination **inadequate to exclude the worst possibility**. Endocervical curettage
samples exactly that blind area. Treating instead of sampling would mean **excising cervical
stroma on an unproven low-grade lesion**, and excisional procedures increase the risk of
**preterm birth, PPROM and cervical stenosis** in future pregnancies — an unacceptable trade
for a lesion that regresses spontaneously in most young women.

**MUST KNOW — management by cytology result:**
- **ASC-US: <25 years → repeat cytology in 12 months; ≥25 years → REFLEX HPV DNA testing (colposcopy if high-risk HPV positive).**
- **LSIL: <25 years → repeat cytology in 12 months (high spontaneous regression); ≥25 years → COLPOSCOPY ± endocervical curettage.**
- **HSIL and ASC-H: COLPOSCOPY + ENDOCERVICAL CURETTAGE** (or immediate "see-and-treat" excision in HSIL).
- **AGC (atypical glandular cells): colposcopy + endocervical curettage + ENDOMETRIAL BIOPSY** (in women ≥35 or with abnormal bleeding) — glandular abnormalities may originate above the cervix.
- **Colposcopy findings: ACETOWHITE epithelium, punctation, mosaicism and atypical vessels; iodine-negative (SCHILLER test positive) areas. Biopsy the worst area.** **CIN 1 → observe; CIN 2/3 → excision (LEEP/LLETZ) or ablation.**
- **Endocervical curettage is CONTRAINDICATED IN PREGNANCY**; colposcopy is safe, and definitive treatment is deferred until after delivery unless invasive cancer is suspected.
- **Primary HPV DNA testing every 5 years is now the preferred screening strategy**; India's national programme uses **VIA every 5 years for women aged 30–65**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem describes a lesion **disappearing up the canal** — the answer
is the test that samples the canal, not an excisional treatment.

**REMEMBER:** *If you cannot see the top of the lesion, curette the canal.*

`Q11817`

---

## 172. FIGO Staging of Ovarian Carcinoma: Stage III

**PEARL:** **Retroperitoneal (para-aortic) lymph node metastasis ONLY = STAGE IIIA1** —
subdivided by node size: **IIIA1(i) ≤10 mm; IIIA1(ii) >10 mm.** So a 9 mm nodal deposit is
**IIIA1(i)**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Ovarian cancer is staged by **how far tumour has travelled through the
peritoneal cavity**, because that is what determines the surgical effort required and the
prognosis. **Nodal-only disease is placed at the BOTTOM of stage III** — despite being
"metastatic" — because it has **not seeded the peritoneum**, which is the biologically and
surgically decisive event. This is why nodal-only stage III has a distinctly better outcome
than peritoneal stage IIIB/IIIC.

**MUST KNOW — Stage III (spread beyond the pelvis to the peritoneum and/or retroperitoneal
nodes):**
- **IIIA1 — POSITIVE RETROPERITONEAL NODES ONLY** [(i) ≤10 mm, (ii) >10 mm].
- **IIIA2 — MICROSCOPIC extrapelvic peritoneal involvement ± positive nodes.**
- **IIIB — MACROSCOPIC peritoneal deposits ≤2 cm ± nodes.**
- **IIIC — macroscopic peritoneal deposits >2 cm ± nodes** (includes extension to the **capsule** of the liver or spleen without parenchymal invasion).
- **Stage IV: distant metastasis. IVA — MALIGNANT PLEURAL EFFUSION with positive cytology; IVB — parenchymal (liver, spleen) metastases and extra-abdominal organs, INCLUDING INGUINAL and other extra-abdominal LYMPH NODES.**
- **Stage I confined to the ovaries (IC1 surgical spill, IC2 capsule ruptured before surgery or tumour on the surface, IC3 malignant cells in ascites or washings); Stage II pelvic extension.**
- **Ovarian cancer is SURGICALLY staged** — a full staging laparotomy includes washings, TAH-BSO, omentectomy, peritoneal biopsies and pelvic/para-aortic node sampling. **Optimal cytoreduction (no residual macroscopic disease, "R0") is the single strongest modifiable prognostic factor**, followed by platinum–taxane chemotherapy; **neoadjuvant chemotherapy with interval debulking** is used when primary complete cytoreduction is not achievable.
- **CA-125 for monitoring; BRCA1/2 testing for all epithelial ovarian cancers** — it guides **PARP inhibitor** maintenance and family screening.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The nodal size and the absence of peritoneal disease are the two facts
that fix the stage. **Nodes only = IIIA1; the 10 mm cut-off decides (i) vs (ii).**

**REMEMBER:** *Nodes alone put you at the bottom of stage III — peritoneum is what makes it
worse.*

`Q11807`

---

## 173. Cervical Mucus Ferning and Vaginal Cytology

**PEARL:** **FERNING (arborisation) of cervical mucus indicates a high-OESTROGEN, PRE-
OVULATORY state** — and vaginal cytology in the same phase shows **SUPERFICIAL CELL
PREDOMINANCE** (acidophilic pink cells with small pyknotic nuclei).

**UNDERSTAND:** Both findings measure the **same hormone through different tissues**.
Oestrogen makes cervical mucus **copious, thin, watery and rich in sodium chloride**, so
when it dries the salt crystallises into a fern pattern — and that same thin mucus is what
allows sperm penetration (**spinnbarkeit** stretching to 10 cm at ovulation). Oestrogen
simultaneously drives the **vaginal epithelium to mature fully to superficial cells**.
Progesterone does the opposite: mucus becomes **thick, scanty and cellular (no ferning)** and
maturation halts at **intermediate cells**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Vaginal cytology as a hormonal assay: SUPERFICIAL cells = oestrogen (proliferative phase, ovulation, oestrogen-secreting tumours); INTERMEDIATE cells (basophilic, navicular in pregnancy) = PROGESTERONE (secretory phase, pregnancy); PARABASAL and BASAL cells = NO hormonal stimulation (menopause, prepuberty, lactation, hypogonadism).** The **maturation index** expresses these three as a ratio (parabasal : intermediate : superficial).
- **Ferning is ABSENT in the luteal phase and in pregnancy** — its persistence after mid-cycle suggests anovulation.
- **Amniotic fluid ferns too** — the classic bedside test for **premature rupture of membranes**, alongside the **nitrazine/pH test** (amniotic fluid is alkaline, turning the paper blue) and pooling.
- Other ovulation markers: **basal body temperature rise of 0.3–0.5 °C (progesterone, thermogenic), mid-luteal (day 21) SERUM PROGESTERONE >3 ng/mL confirming ovulation, LH surge on urinary kits, and ultrasound follicular tracking**; endometrial biopsy shows **secretory changes**.
- **Cervical mucus at ovulation: maximum quantity, watery, alkaline, low viscosity, high spinnbarkeit, positive ferning** — all designed to admit sperm; the **postcoital (Sims–Huhner) test** exploits this.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** A microscopy image of ferning fixes the phase; the question then asks
for a **parallel oestrogenic finding** — superficial cells.

**REMEMBER:** *Oestrogen makes the mucus fern and the vaginal cells mature to the surface.*

![question](https://cdn1.dailyrounds.org/uploads/c2302f084c6d4529a61d263324a87621.JPEG)
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/2134856a735e49468f837401430009cdx1280x1259.JPEG)

## 174. Adenomyosis on MRI

**PEARL:** The most specific MRI sign of **adenomyosis** is **JUNCTIONAL ZONE THICKENING
≥12 mm** (normal <8 mm; 8–12 mm equivocal), with a globular uterus and myometrial cysts.
Clinically: a **multiparous woman in her 40s with menorrhagia, PROGRESSIVE dysmenorrhoea and
a symmetrically enlarged, boggy, TENDER uterus.**

**UNDERSTAND:** The junctional zone is the **inner myometrium**, and it is exactly where
endometrial glands invade in adenomyosis. On T2-weighted MRI it is normally a **thin dark
band** (compact, low-water-content smooth muscle); invasion by endometrial tissue causes
**hyperplasia of that inner layer**, thickening it and studding it with **bright cystic
foci** (trapped endometrial glands and haemorrhage). So the sign is a direct picture of the
pathology, which is why it is the most specific criterion.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Ultrasound (first-line) features: a globular, asymmetrically thickened uterus, heterogeneous myometrium, myometrial cysts, "VENETIAN BLIND" shadowing, indistinct endometrial–myometrial border and a subendometrial echogenic halo.** MRI is the most accurate **non-invasive** test; **HISTOPATHOLOGY after hysterectomy remains the gold standard.**
- **Adenomyosis vs FIBROID: adenomyosis is DIFFUSE, ill-defined, tender and symmetrically enlarging, with NO pseudocapsule; a leiomyoma is a well-defined mass WITH a pseudocapsule (which is why it can be enucleated).**
- **Treatment: LNG-IUS (Mirena) is first-line for the woman who wants to preserve fertility**; also NSAIDs, tranexamic acid, combined pills, GnRH agonists, and uterine artery embolisation. **HYSTERECTOMY is definitive** in the woman who has completed her family.
- The distractor options are worth knowing on their own: **"necklace/string-of-pearls" of peripherally arranged follicles = POLYCYSTIC OVARY**; **a T1-hyperintense adnexal cyst with T2 SHADING = ENDOMETRIOMA (chocolate cyst)**; **a well-defined pseudocapsule = leiomyoma**.
- **Endometriosis and adenomyosis often coexist** — but endometriosis is **outside** the uterus (dysmenorrhoea, dyspareunia, infertility, "powder-burn" lesions, laparoscopy is the gold standard).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Most specific MRI finding" — the number **12 mm** and the structure
(**junctional zone**) are the whole answer.

**REMEMBER:** *Glands invade the inner myometrium, so the dark junctional zone swells past
12 mm.*

## 175. Acute Fatty Liver of Pregnancy vs HELLP

**PEARL:** The discriminators are the markers of **HEPATIC SYNTHETIC FAILURE**, present in
**AFLP** and absent in HELLP: **HYPOGLYCAEMIA, markedly LOW FIBRINOGEN with a prolonged
PT/INR, and RAISED AMMONIA**. **Thrombocytopenia, raised LDH and raised transaminases occur
in BOTH** — so they cannot separate them.

**UNDERSTAND:** HELLP is a **microangiopathy**: platelets are consumed and red cells are
sheared, and the liver enzymes rise from periportal necrosis — but the **hepatocytes are
still working**, so they keep making glucose and clotting factors. AFLP is **true acute
liver FAILURE**: microvesicular fatty infiltration halts hepatocyte function, so
**gluconeogenesis fails (hypoglycaemia), clotting factor and fibrinogen synthesis fails
(coagulopathy), and urea-cycle detoxification fails (hyperammonaemia and encephalopathy)**.
Ask "is the liver still manufacturing?" and the two separate cleanly.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **AFLP: third trimester, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, jaundice, polyuria/polydipsia (transient diabetes insipidus) and encephalopathy; SWANSEA CRITERIA for diagnosis. It is the commonest cause of acute liver failure in pregnancy.** Associated with **fetal LCHAD (long-chain 3-hydroxyacyl-CoA dehydrogenase) deficiency** — test the baby.
- **Management of BOTH is PROMPT DELIVERY plus supportive care** — glucose infusion, fresh frozen plasma/cryoprecipitate and blood products, correction of coagulopathy, and transfer to a unit with liver support; **transplantation is occasionally needed in AFLP.**
- **HELLP: Haemolysis (schistocytes, raised LDH and indirect bilirubin, low haptoglobin), Elevated Liver enzymes, Low Platelets (<100,000)** — may occur with **only mildly raised blood pressure**, and 15–20% have neither hypertension nor proteinuria. Complications include **subcapsular liver haematoma (severe RUQ pain — beware rupture), DIC, abruption, ARDS and acute kidney injury.**
- Other pregnancy liver diseases: **hyperemesis gravidarum (first trimester, mild transaminitis), INTRAHEPATIC CHOLESTASIS OF PREGNANCY (pruritus of palms and soles with raised BILE ACIDS, risk of stillbirth — treat with ursodeoxycholic acid and deliver at 37 weeks), and viral hepatitis (HEPATITIS E carries a 20% mortality in pregnancy).**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Statement sets deliberately include the features shared by both.
Select only the three **synthetic-failure** items.

**REMEMBER:** *HELLP breaks the blood; AFLP stops the factory — glucose, fibrinogen and
ammonia tell you which.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/f077ca247cd04557a853146956d7aa68x926x1164.PNG)

`Q11837`

## 176. Parvovirus B19 in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** After a confirmed acute maternal parvovirus B19 infection (**IgM positive, IgG
negative**), the next step is **SERIAL MIDDLE CEREBRAL ARTERY PEAK SYSTOLIC VELOCITY
(MCA-PSV) DOPPLER every 1–2 weeks for 10–12 weeks** to detect fetal anaemia **before**
hydrops develops.

**UNDERSTAND:** Parvovirus B19 infects and lyses **erythroid progenitors (its receptor is the
P antigen/globoside on red cell precursors)**, causing a pure red cell aplasia. Fetal anaemia
lowers blood viscosity and raises cardiac output, so blood **flows faster** through the
middle cerebral artery — which is exactly what MCA-PSV measures. A value **>1.5 multiples of
the median** predicts moderate-to-severe anaemia **non-invasively**, allowing intervention
before **hydrops fetalis** (high-output cardiac failure) sets in. Invasive fetal blood
sampling is therefore reserved for when the Doppler is abnormal.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Treatment of established fetal anaemia: INTRAUTERINE TRANSFUSION** — which is usually curative, since the aplasia is transient and the fetus recovers its own erythropoiesis.
- **Risk of vertical transmission ~30%; risk of fetal loss is highest with infection BEFORE 20 WEEKS.** Parvovirus B19 is **NOT teratogenic** — there is no congenital syndrome, unlike rubella or CMV.
- **Maternal/childhood disease: ERYTHEMA INFECTIOSUM ("fifth disease") — SLAPPED-CHEEK rash with a lacy reticular body rash in children (who are no longer infectious once the rash appears); in adults, fever, malaise and a symmetrical ARTHROPATHY of the hands, wrists and knees.**
- **Parvovirus B19 causes APLASTIC CRISIS in chronic haemolytic anaemias (sickle cell disease, hereditary spherocytosis, thalassaemia) and chronic pure red cell aplasia in the immunocompromised (treated with IVIG).**
- **Amniocentesis for fetal PCR is not required for management**, and IVIG has no established role in fetal treatment.
- **Other causes of non-immune hydrops: severe anaemia (parvovirus, alpha-thalassaemia major/Bart hydrops, fetomaternal haemorrhage), cardiac arrhythmia and structural disease, twin-to-twin transfusion, aneuploidy, and congenital infection.** **Immune hydrops = Rh alloimmunisation**, monitored by the same MCA Doppler.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The answer is the **non-invasive surveillance** test, not immediate
invasive sampling or treatment.

**REMEMBER:** *Thin blood flows fast — MCA velocity finds fetal anaemia before hydrops.*

`Q11839`

## 177. POP-Q System

**PEARL:** In **POP-Q**, the **hymen is the fixed reference point (zero)**. Points **above the hymen are NEGATIVE**, points **below are POSITIVE**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Earlier systems graded prolapse against the introitus or ischial spines, which vary between examiners. The **hymenal ring is a fixed, visible landmark**, so measurements from it are reproducible — that objectivity is why the International Continence Society adopted it.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Nine measurements:** six points (**Aa, Ba** anterior vaginal wall; **C, D** apex — C is the cervix/vaginal cuff, D the posterior fornix, **absent after hysterectomy**; **Ap, Bp** posterior wall) plus three lengths — **genital hiatus (gh), perineal body (pb), total vaginal length (tvl)**.
- **Staging: 0** no descent; **1** leading edge **> 1 cm above** the hymen; **2** leading edge **within 1 cm above or below** the hymen; **3** leading edge **> 1 cm below** hymen but not complete eversion; **4** **complete eversion** (procidentia).
- Stage 2 is the crucial boundary — **prolapse reaching the hymen** — because symptoms typically begin there.
- Older grading (Shaw's) for uterine descent: **first degree** cervix in vagina, **second degree** cervix at introitus, **third degree** cervix outside, **procidentia** whole uterus outside.
- Support: **level 1 — cardinal/uterosacral (apical)**; **level 2 — arcus tendineus and paravaginal (lateral)**; **level 3 — perineal body and urogenital diaphragm (distal)**. **Level 1 failure causes apical/uterine prolapse and vault prolapse.**

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Diagram identification, then staging. Remember that **the sign convention is set by the hymen**, and stage 2 straddles it.

**REMEMBER:** *Above the hymen is minus, below is plus.*

![question](https://cdn1.dailyrounds.org/uploads/8147be931ab0473799ed315b00581c74.JPEG)

## 178. Chorionic Villus Sampling — Timing

**PEARL:** **CVS is performed at 11–13 weeks** (transcervical 10–13 weeks; transabdominal from 10 weeks to term). **Never before 10 weeks** — risk of **limb-reduction and oromandibular defects**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Before 10 weeks the **limb buds and mandible are still forming**, and villus sampling causes **vascular disruption** in a critically vascular period. After 10 weeks organogenesis of these structures is complete, so the same procedure is safe.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Samples **trophoblast (chorionic villi)** → gives **fetal karyotype, DNA and enzyme analysis**; ideal for **haemoglobinopathies (thalassaemia, sickle cell), inborn errors of metabolism, and single-gene disorders** — and for **early diagnosis**, allowing first-trimester termination.
- **Cannot diagnose neural tube defects** — no amniotic fluid, so **no AFP or acetylcholinesterase**.
- **Miscarriage risk is higher than amniocentesis** (~0.5–1% vs ~0.1–0.5%).
- **Confined placental mosaicism** (~1%) is a specific pitfall — the placenta's karyotype may differ from the fetus, requiring amniocentesis to confirm.
- **Amniocentesis: 15–20 weeks** (early amniocentesis before 14 weeks increases talipes and fetal loss). **Cordocentesis (fetal blood sampling): after 18 weeks**.
- **Anti-D prophylaxis** is required after any invasive procedure in an Rh-negative woman.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Number recall. The distractors are all **earlier** than 10 weeks, testing whether you know why early CVS was abandoned.

**REMEMBER:** *Wait until the limbs are formed.*

`MB9797`

---

## 179. Trophoblast Invasion and Pre-eclampsia

**PEARL:** Pre-eclampsia results from **failure of the SECOND wave of EXTRAVILLOUS (endovascular) trophoblast invasion into the SPIRAL arteries** at 16–18 weeks.

**UNDERSTAND:** Normally the endovascular trophoblast **replaces the endothelium and destroys the muscular media** of the spiral arteries, converting them into **flaccid, low-resistance, high-flow conduits** that cannot vasoconstrict. If the second wave fails, the **myometrial segments retain their muscular wall and remain responsive to vasoconstrictors** — so placental perfusion falls, the ischaemic placenta releases anti-angiogenic factors (**sFlt-1, soluble endoglin**), and maternal **endothelial dysfunction** produces hypertension, proteinuria and oedema.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **First wave (10–12 weeks)** — invasion up to the **decidual** segments; **second wave (16–18 weeks)** — up to the **myometrial** segments. Only the second fails in pre-eclampsia.
- The persistent muscular spiral arteries show **"acute atherosis"** — fibrinoid necrosis with lipid-laden macrophages.
- Consequence for Doppler: **persistent uterine artery notch and high resistance at 20–24 weeks** predicts pre-eclampsia and IUGR.
- **Villous** trophoblast handles exchange; **extravillous** trophoblast does the invading — the distinction the question turns on.
- Prevention: **low-dose aspirin 150 mg at night, started before 16 weeks** in high-risk women (before the second wave), plus **calcium** where dietary intake is low.
- Delivery of the **placenta** is the definitive cure — confirming the placenta as the origin.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Four options permute **villous vs extravillous** and **spiral vs radial vs arcuate**. Both halves must be right: **extravillous + spiral**.

**REMEMBER:** *The second wave never reaches the myometrium.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/86fef835bafd42e686f42bb3043e1266x1092x1198.JPEG)
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/c4932fbb82fa41379426e3247e9630d1x1280x1211.JPEG)

## 180. Eclampsia — Management and the Magpie Trial

**PEARL:** **Magnesium sulphate is the anticonvulsant of choice** in eclampsia — established by the **Magpie trial** — but the **FIRST step is airway, breathing and oxygenation**, not MgSO₄. **Diazepam should NOT be used** to shorten a convulsion.

**UNDERSTAND:** An eclamptic fit is usually **self-limiting**; the immediate threats are **hypoxia and aspiration**, which is why airway comes first. Magnesium's role is to **prevent the NEXT seizure**, and it does so better than phenytoin or diazepam because it acts on **cerebral vasospasm and NMDA receptors** rather than sedating the mother.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Order: **airway/oxygen and left lateral position → MgSO₄ → control blood pressure (labetalol, hydralazine, nifedipine) → deliver the baby**. Delivery is the definitive treatment, but **stabilise the mother first**.
- **Maternal ACIDOSIS (not alkalosis) occurs** after a fit and is corrected if severe.
- **Pritchard regimen:** 4 g IV + 10 g IM loading, then 5 g IM every 4 h. **Zuspan/Sibai:** 4 g IV then 1–2 g/h infusion.
- **Magnesium toxicity by level:** **loss of patellar reflex at 10–12 mEq/L**, **respiratory depression 12–13**, **cardiac arrest > 25**. Monitor **patellar reflex, respiratory rate ≥ 12/min, urine output ≥ 30 mL/h**. Antidote **10% calcium gluconate 10 mL IV**.
- **Thiopentone** (rather than diazepam) may be used for refractory seizures; diazepam causes **thrombophlebitis and neonatal respiratory depression**.
- The **Magpie trial** showed MgSO₄ **halves the risk of eclampsia** in pre-eclampsia and probably reduces maternal death.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The stem offers "first step is IV MgSO₄" as a plausible trap — the airway always precedes it. Note the deliberate **alkalosis/acidosis** switch in another option.

**REMEMBER:** *Airway first, magnesium second, delivery last.*

`MB2040, MB2068`

---

## 181. Methylergometrine — Contraindications

**PEARL:** **Methylergometrine is absolutely contraindicated in organic heart disease** (including rheumatic mitral stenosis), **hypertension/pre-eclampsia/eclampsia, twin pregnancy before delivery of the second twin, and Rh-negative mothers**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Ergometrine causes **sustained tetanic uterine contraction plus generalised vasoconstriction**. In mitral stenosis this **squeezes ~500 mL of uterine blood into a circulation that cannot cope**, precipitating pulmonary oedema. In hypertension the vasoconstriction raises blood pressure further; in twins it strangles the undelivered second baby; in Rh-negative women it forces **fetomaternal transfusion**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Choice of uterotonic in **cardiac disease and hypertension: oxytocin** (given slowly — rapid bolus causes hypotension); **carboprost is contraindicated in ASTHMA**; **misoprostol** is safe in cardiac disease but causes pyrexia and shivering.
- **PPH prophylaxis (active management of third stage): oxytocin 10 IU IM/IV is first line**; **carbetocin 100 µg IM/IV** — a heat-stable long-acting oxytocin analogue, preferred **where the cold chain cannot be guaranteed**; **misoprostol 400–600 µg orally** where neither is available.
- Treatment ladder for atonic PPH: **uterine massage and bimanual compression → oxytocin → ergometrine → carboprost → misoprostol → tranexamic acid → balloon tamponade → B-Lynch → uterine/internal iliac artery ligation → hysterectomy**.
- **Tranexamic acid within 3 hours** of onset reduces death from bleeding (WOMAN trial).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The comorbidity in the stem selects the answer. **Heart disease → avoid ergometrine; asthma → avoid carboprost.**

**REMEMBER:** *Ergometrine squeezes the uterus and the arteries — bad for a stiff heart.*

`MB9625, MF2220`

---

## 182. Disorders of Sex Development in 46,XY

**PEARL:** **Swyer syndrome (complete gonadal dysgenesis)** — 46,XY, **female phenotype, NORMAL uterus, streak gonads, absent breast development, high FSH**. **Complete androgen insensitivity** — 46,XY, female phenotype, **NO uterus, good breasts, scanty pubic hair**. **5α-reductase deficiency** — 46,XY, ambiguous/female external genitalia at birth that **VIRILISE at puberty**, normal male testosterone.

**UNDERSTAND:** Work forwards through the developmental sequence and each syndrome falls out. **SRY makes the testis**; the testis makes **AMH (regresses Müllerian ducts)** and **testosterone (builds Wolffian ducts)**; **5α-reductase converts testosterone to DHT (builds external male genitalia)**. Block SRY → no testis → **no AMH (uterus present)** and no androgen. Block the androgen receptor → AMH still works (**no uterus**) but no virilisation. Block only 5α-reductase → internal male structures normal, **external female until puberty's testosterone surge**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Swyer:** point mutation or deletion of **SRY** (or DAX1, SF1, CBX2); **most cases idiopathic**. Presents with **primary amenorrhoea and absent secondary sexual characteristics**; **FSH > 40 mIU/mL, oestradiol < 25 pg/mL**. Needs **cyclical oestrogen–progestogen** and can carry a pregnancy with **oocyte donation**. **Gonadectomy is mandatory** — dysgenetic gonads with a Y chromosome carry a high risk of **gonadoblastoma/dysgerminoma**.
- **CAIS:** breasts develop (peripheral aromatisation of testosterone), **blind vaginal pouch**, testes intra-abdominal or inguinal; gonadectomy **after puberty**.
- **5α-reductase deficiency:** raised **testosterone:DHT ratio**; often raised female, then virilise ("penis at twelve").
- **Turner (45,X)** — short stature, streak ovaries, **high FSH**, uterus present.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Two features decide everything: **is the uterus present** (AMH acted or not) and **do breasts develop** (oestrogen present or not).

**REMEMBER:** *Uterus present means AMH never worked; breasts present means oestrogen did.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/536987446eb543f386274c52bc9e5974x1280x2560.JPEG)

`MC5259, MF9070, MF9076`

## 183. Second-Trimester Quadruple Screen

**PEARL:** **Down syndrome (T21): β-hCG ↑, Inhibin A ↑, AFP ↓, unconjugated estriol ↓.** **Trisomy 18: ALL FOUR LOW.** **Neural tube defect: AFP HIGH with the others normal.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Down syndrome placentas are **immature and hyperfunctional**, oversecreting the placental products (hCG, inhibin), while the **less-developed fetal liver and adrenal** produce less AFP and estriol. Trisomy 18 causes **global placental and fetal underfunction**, so everything falls together — an easy pattern to remember as "the all-low trisomy."

**MUST KNOW:**
- Quadruple test is performed at **15–21 weeks** (optimally 16–18); detection rate ~80% for T21.
- **First-trimester dual test (11–14 weeks): PAPP-A ↓ and free β-hCG ↑ in Down syndrome**, both ↓ in trisomy 18/13; combined with **nuchal translucency** (≥ 3 mm abnormal).
- **Cell-free fetal DNA (NIPT)** from 10 weeks has the highest detection rate but is a **screening, not diagnostic**, test — confirm with **CVS or amniocentesis**.
- **Raised MSAFP** — open neural tube defects, abdominal wall defects (**omphalocele, gastroschisis**), multiple pregnancy, **underestimated gestational age**, fetal death, placental abnormality.
- **Anencephaly** ultrasound signs: **frog-eye sign** (absent calvaria with prominent orbits) and **shower-cap sign**; detectable from **10–11 weeks**. Prevent with **preconceptional folic acid — 4–5 mg/day for a previously affected pregnancy** (vs 400 µg routinely).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Memorise the T21 pattern as **"two up, two down"** — the two placental markers up, the two fetal markers down.

**REMEMBER:** *Down has a hyperactive placenta and a lazy fetal liver.*

## 184. HPV Serotypes and Vaccines

**PEARL:** **High-risk (oncogenic): 16, 18, 31, 33, 45, 52, 58** and others. **Low-risk (warts): 6 and 11.** **Nonavalent Gardasil-9 covers 6, 11, 16, 18, 31, 33, 45, 52, 58.**

**UNDERSTAND:** High-risk types encode **E6 and E7 oncoproteins** that degrade **p53 and Rb** respectively — abolishing both major cell-cycle checkpoints. Low-risk types encode weaker versions that drive proliferation without transformation, producing **warts** rather than cancer.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Cervarix — bivalent (16, 18)**, given at **0, 1, 6 months**. **Gardasil / Cervavac — quadrivalent (6, 11, 16, 18)**, at **0, 2, 6 months**. **Gardasil-9 — nonavalent**, also covering vulvar/vaginal/anal intraepithelial neoplasia and **oropharyngeal and head-and-neck cancers**.
- **Ideal age 9–14 years** (two doses, 6 months apart); **three doses if started at ≥ 15 years or immunocompromised**. Given to **both sexes**.
- **16 and 18 together cause ~70% of cervical cancers**; **16 is the commonest in squamous cell carcinoma, 18 in adenocarcinoma**.
- Cervical cancer **does NOT run in families** — the daughter of an affected woman needs **vaccination and routine screening**, not BRCA/PTEN testing or biopsy.
- Detection of HPV DNA: **PCR, hybrid capture, Southern blot**. HPV testing is now the preferred primary screening method from age 30.
- **Stage IIB (parametrial involvement, not reaching the pelvic side wall) → concurrent CHEMORADIATION** (external beam + brachytherapy + weekly cisplatin), **not surgery**. Surgery is for **stage IIA1 and below**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Numbers are asked directly. Anchor **6, 11 = warts**; **16, 18 = cancer**; nonavalent adds **31, 33, 45, 52, 58**.

**REMEMBER:** *Six and eleven make warts; sixteen and eighteen make cancer.*

`MF8241, MB4976, MB9222, MB2883, MF8243`

## 185. Nutrition in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **NO additional calories are needed in the first trimester.** Add approximately **340 kcal/day in the second** and **450 kcal/day in the third** trimester.

**UNDERSTAND:** Total pregnancy energy cost is about **80,000 kcal**, but nearly all of it is spent in the **last 20 weeks**, when fetal growth and maternal fat deposition accelerate. Fetal mass in the first trimester is negligible — adding calories then simply produces maternal obesity.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **ICMR (Indian) recommendation** adds **~350 kcal/day** and **~23 g extra protein/day** through pregnancy — know both the Institute of Medicine and ICMR figures.
- **Weight gain by pre-pregnancy BMI:** underweight **12.5–18 kg**; normal **11.5–16 kg**; overweight **7–11.5 kg**; obese **5–9 kg**.
- If calories are inadequate, **protein is burned for energy rather than spared for fetal growth** — the rationale for adequate carbohydrate.
- **Iron and folic acid:** 1 tablet daily (**60 mg elemental iron + 500 µg folic acid**) for at least 180 days in pregnancy and 180 days postpartum (Anaemia Mukt Bharat).
- **Vitamin B12 deficiency** in pregnancy (macrocytosis, hypersegmented neutrophils, neurological features — clumsiness, forgetfulness) must be treated with **IM cyanocobalamin 1000 µg plus folic acid** — **folate alone corrects the anaemia but allows the neuropathy to progress**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Additional calories in the first trimester" → **none / continue the same diet**. The B12 question tests dose (**1000 µg IM, not 100 µg**) and the need to give **both** B12 and folate.

**REMEMBER:** *The baby's calories are spent in the last half of pregnancy.*

`MB9165, MB9548`

---

## 186. Semen Analysis — WHO 2021 Criteria

**PEARL:** **Strict MORPHOLOGY is the single best discriminator of male fertility** on semen analysis. Lower reference limit: **> 4% normal forms**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Count and motility tell you **how many sperm arrive**; morphology tells you **how many are capable of fertilising**. Structural defects of the acrosome and head prevent zona binding and penetration regardless of how many sperm reach the egg — so morphology correlates best with fertilisation rates, including in IVF.

**MUST KNOW:**
- WHO reference limits: **volume ≥ 1.4 mL**; **pH 7.2–7.8**; **concentration ≥ 16 million/mL**; **total count ≥ 39 million/ejaculate**; **total motility ≥ 42%**, **progressive motility ≥ 30%**; **normal forms > 4%**; **vitality ≥ 54%**; **leucocytes < 1 million/mL**.
- Collect after **2–7 days abstinence**; examine within 1 hour; **repeat abnormal samples after 3 months** (a spermatogenic cycle is ~74 days).
- Terminology: **oligozoospermia** (low count), **asthenozoospermia** (low motility), **teratozoospermia** (abnormal forms), **azoospermia** (none), **aspermia** (no ejaculate), **necrozoospermia** (all dead).
- **Azoospermia**: **obstructive** (normal FSH, normal testicular volume — vas agenesis with **CFTR mutation**, post-infective) vs **non-obstructive** (**high FSH, small testes** — Sertoli-cell-only, Klinefelter, Y-microdeletion).
- **Leucocytes > 1 million/mL** suggests genital tract infection (pyospermia).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Most important marker" → **morphology**. The parameter values are asked separately as pure numbers.

**REMEMBER:** *Shape decides whether a sperm can do its job.*

`MC5262`

---

## 187. Sentinel Lymph Node Biopsy in Gynaecology

**PEARL:** **Sentinel lymph node biopsy is most useful in carcinoma of the VULVA**, where it spares the morbidity of full inguinofemoral lymphadenectomy.

**UNDERSTAND:** SLNB pays off where **nodal status changes management, the drainage is predictable and superficial, and full dissection is morbid**. Vulval cancer satisfies all three: nodes may be involved **without being palpable**, drainage runs to the **superficial inguinal nodes** in an orderly fashion, and complete groin dissection causes **lymphoedema, wound breakdown and lymphocysts** in a majority of women.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Indications: **unifocal tumour < 4 cm**, **no clinically or radiologically suspicious groin nodes**, and depth of invasion **> 1 mm** (below which nodal spread is negligible and no groin surgery is needed).
- Technique: **technetium-99m radiocolloid plus blue dye** (or indocyanine green); **a positive sentinel node mandates completion lymphadenectomy or groin radiotherapy**.
- **Lateral tumours (> 2 cm from the midline)** need **ipsilateral** groin assessment; **midline tumours need bilateral**.
- SLNB is also standard in **breast carcinoma, melanoma and penile carcinoma**, and is increasingly used in **endometrial and early cervical cancer**.
- Vulval carcinoma is usually **squamous**; two pathways — **HPV-related (younger, VIN)** and **HPV-independent (older, lichen sclerosus, TP53 mutation)**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Straightforward — among the gynaecological cancers listed, **vulva** is the established indication.

**REMEMBER:** *Predictable drainage plus morbid dissection equals sentinel node.*

`MB3734`

---

## 188. Septate Uterus — Hysteroscopic Resection

**PEARL:** **Transcervical hysteroscopic resection of the septum** gives the best obstetric outcome — it avoids a **uterine scar** and therefore avoids mandatory caesarean delivery.

**UNDERSTAND:** The abdominal metroplasties (**Jones, Tompkins, Strassmann**) all **incise the uterine wall** to reach the septum, leaving a scar that risks **rupture in labour**. Hysteroscopy reaches the septum **from inside the cavity**, dividing avascular fibrous tissue without ever breaching the myometrium — hence better subsequent pregnancy outcomes and shorter recovery.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Septate uterus (AFS class V) is the commonest Müllerian anomaly** and the one **most associated with recurrent pregnancy loss** — because the septum is poorly vascularised fibrous tissue that cannot support implantation.
- Distinguish from **bicornuate uterus** by the **external fundal contour** (indented in bicornuate, smooth/flat in septate) on **3D ultrasound or MRI** — the distinction matters because a bicornuate uterus is **not** treated hysteroscopically.
- **Unicornuate (class II)** — risk of **uterine rupture in a rudimentary horn pregnancy**; excise a non-communicating cavitated horn. Associated with **renal anomalies** — always image the kidneys.
- **Class I (agenesis) — MRKH syndrome**; **class VI arcuate** — a normal variant needing no treatment; **class VII — DES-related T-shaped uterus**.
- Postoperative: oestrogen and/or intrauterine device/balloon may be used to prevent **adhesions (Asherman's)**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** All four options are real operations; three are the abdominal metroplasties. Pick the **hysteroscopic** one.

**REMEMBER:** *Cut the septum from inside and leave the wall intact.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/b5daabaa46584f8ca9566b45a6d7c54dx1280x4923.JPEG)

`MD0696`

## 189. Cord Presentation vs Cord Prolapse

**PEARL:** **Cord felt as a "bag of worms" beneath the presenting part with INTACT membranes = cord PRESENTATION → emergency caesarean section with membranes kept intact.** Cord **prolapse** means the membranes have ruptured and the cord is below the presenting part.

**UNDERSTAND:** While the membranes are intact, the **fluid cushions the cord** and there is no compression — so the entire strategy is to **deliver before the membranes rupture**. Deliberately rupturing them to "confirm" the diagnosis converts a controllable situation into an acute emergency with immediate cord compression and fetal hypoxia.

**MUST KNOW:**
- While preparing for surgery: **exaggerated Sims' or knee–chest/Trendelenburg position** to relieve pressure on the cord; **avoid vaginal examination and never rupture the membranes**.
- **Cord prolapse** management depends on **fetal viability, gestational age and cervical dilatation**: if the fetus is alive and delivery is not imminent → **elevate the presenting part manually or by bladder filling, give a tocolytic, and perform immediate caesarean**; if fully dilated with the head low → **instrumental vaginal delivery**; if the fetus is dead → **await vaginal delivery**.
- Risk factors: **malpresentation (transverse lie, breech, especially footling), polyhydramnios, prematurity, multiple pregnancy, long cord, high presenting part, artificial rupture of membranes with an unengaged head**.
- **Thromboprophylaxis around caesarean**: **pneumatic compression devices for women NOT on pharmacological prophylaxis**, removed **once the patient is ambulant**; **add heparin/LMWH** when additional risk factors exist (thrombophilia, obesity, heart failure, previous VTE).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** The critical phrase is **"membranes are felt"** — intact. That converts the answer from prolapse management to **immediate caesarean with membranes preserved**.

**REMEMBER:** *Intact membranes are protecting the cord — do not break them.*

![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/775c094e466e45c8a7d14e02518f0bb5x310x190.JPEG)
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/1c9d0b81ed434fa7bcbe1655a25cff42x1280x1870.JPEG)

`MG1167, MB9873`

## 190. Emergency Contraception with Levonorgestrel

**PEARL:** **Levonorgestrel 0.75 mg within 72 hours, repeated after 12 hours** — or a **single 1.5 mg dose**. Effective up to **120 hours**, with declining efficacy.

**UNDERSTAND:** LNG works primarily by **delaying or inhibiting ovulation**, which is why it must be taken **as early as possible** and why it is ineffective once ovulation has occurred. It does **not disrupt an established pregnancy** — an important counselling and ethical point.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Advantages: **no oestrogen**, so it can be given to **hypertensive, diabetic, cardiac and lactating** women; fewer side effects than the Yuzpe regimen; single-dose option.
- **Ulipristal acetate 30 mg** (a selective progesterone receptor modulator) is more effective than LNG, especially **between 72 and 120 hours** and in obese women.
- **Copper IUCD is the MOST effective emergency contraceptive** — insert within **5 days** of unprotected intercourse or of expected ovulation; effectiveness **> 99%**, and it provides ongoing contraception.
- Indications: unprotected intercourse, **condom rupture**, missed pills, **delay of > 3 hours in a progestogen-only pill**, sexual assault.
- LNG is **less effective with BMI > 30** — offer ulipristal or a copper IUCD instead.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Dose-schedule recall. The two acceptable regimens (0.75 mg × 2 twelve hours apart, or 1.5 mg once) are the answer set.

**REMEMBER:** *Two doses twelve hours apart, or one double dose.*

`MB9158`

## 191. Trichomoniasis — The Strawberry Cervix

**PEARL:** **Frothy, foul-smelling, greenish discharge + intense itching + "strawberry cervix" (colpitis macularis) = Trichomonas vaginalis.** Vaginal **pH > 4.5**.

**UNDERSTAND:** The strawberry appearance is caused by **punctate subepithelial haemorrhages** from the parasite's direct cytopathic contact with the cervical epithelium. It is present in only **2–5% of cases on naked-eye examination** (far more on colposcopy) — so its absence never excludes the diagnosis, but its presence is near-diagnostic.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Trichomonas is the only sexually transmitted one** of the three common vaginitides — so **partner treatment is mandatory**.
- Diagnosis: **motile flagellated trophozoites on saline wet mount**; NAAT is most sensitive. Treat with **metronidazole 2 g single dose or 500 mg BD for 7 days** (both partners).
- **Bacterial vaginosis:** thin grey **fishy** discharge, **NO itching or inflammation**, **pH > 4.5**, **positive whiff (amine) test**, **clue cells**; **Amsel's criteria** (3 of 4). Not sexually transmitted — no partner treatment. Treat with metronidazole.
- **Candidiasis:** **curdy white** discharge, itching and burning, **pH < 4.5 (normal)**, inflamed epithelium, **pseudohyphae on KOH**. Not sexually transmitted.
- **pH is the fastest bedside discriminator: normal pH points to candida; raised pH to BV or trichomonas.**
- Trichomoniasis in pregnancy is associated with **preterm birth and low birth weight**.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Three vaginitides, three discharges, three pH values. The strawberry cervix image is unique to trichomonas.

**REMEMBER:** *Frothy, itchy, strawberry, sexually transmitted — trichomonas.*


**IMAGES:**
![explanation](https://dhmbxeygs57ff.cloudfront.net/uploads/d04e4e069cc2400695a2a719f54c5dcfx450x598.JPEG)

`MD0690`

## 192. IADPSG Criteria for GDM

**PEARL:** **IADPSG: 75 g OGTT in the FASTING state — fasting > 92, 1 hour > 180, 2 hour > 153 mg/dL.** **Any ONE abnormal value diagnoses GDM.**

**UNDERSTAND:** These thresholds were derived from the **HAPO study**, set at the glucose level conferring a **1.75-fold increased risk of adverse perinatal outcome** — macrosomia, cord C-peptide and neonatal fat. They are therefore **outcome-based**, not statistically arbitrary, which is why only one abnormal value is required.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Contrast **DIPSI (India)**: **non-fasting 75 g glucose, 2-hour value ≥ 140 mg/dL** — a single-step, field-friendly test requiring no fasting, designed for India's programme setting.
- **Carpenter–Coustan (100 g, 3 h)** requires **two or more** abnormal values.
- Screen at the **first antenatal visit** in high-risk women and at **24–28 weeks** universally.
- Maternal complications: **pre-eclampsia, polyhydramnios, increased operative delivery, future type 2 diabetes** (~50% within 10 years — hence a **postpartum OGTT at 6–12 weeks**).
- Fetal/neonatal: **macrosomia and shoulder dystocia**, congenital anomalies (with pre-gestational, not gestational, diabetes), **neonatal hypoglycaemia, hypocalcaemia, hypomagnesaemia, hyperbilirubinaemia, polycythaemia, RDS**, and later **childhood obesity**.
- **Insulin is the drug of choice** in pregnancy; metformin is used in selected cases.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Pure numbers, and the options cluster around 92 vs 95 (the Carpenter–Coustan fasting value). Learn **92/180/153**.

**REMEMBER:** *Ninety-two, one-eighty, one-fifty-three — any one is enough.*

`MF2214`

---

## 193. Respiratory Changes in Pregnancy

**PEARL:** **Functional residual capacity FALLS** in pregnancy (along with **expiratory reserve volume, residual volume and total lung capacity**). **Tidal volume, inspiratory capacity and minute ventilation RISE.** **Respiratory rate and vital capacity are UNCHANGED.**

**UNDERSTAND:** Two forces act in opposite directions. The **enlarging uterus elevates the diaphragm**, mechanically reducing the resting end-expiratory volume — hence a lower FRC. Meanwhile **progesterone directly stimulates the respiratory centre**, increasing **tidal volume** (not rate). Minute ventilation therefore rises purely by depth of breathing, producing a **compensated respiratory alkalosis** that favours placental CO₂ transfer.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Minute ventilation rises ~40%**, driven by tidal volume; **respiratory rate is essentially unchanged** — a fact frequently tested.
- Blood gases: **PaCO₂ falls to ~28–32 mmHg**, **PaO₂ rises to ~104–108**, **bicarbonate falls to ~18–21 mEq/L**, pH slightly alkalotic (**7.40–7.45**).
- **Reduced FRC + increased oxygen consumption = rapid desaturation on apnoea** — the reason **pre-oxygenation is critical before intubating a pregnant woman**, and why rapid sequence induction is standard.
- **Dyspnoea of pregnancy** occurs in 60–70%, is physiological, and begins in the first/second trimester before the uterus is large — it is driven by hypocapnia, not mechanics.
- The diaphragm rises **~4 cm** but chest circumference increases and the subcostal angle widens, so **vital capacity is preserved**.
- Mnemonic: **TIMM increases** (Tidal volume, Inspiratory capacity, Minute ventilation, Minute O₂ uptake).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Which decreases" → **FRC**. Respiratory rate and vital capacity are the classic "unchanged" traps.

**REMEMBER:** *Breathe deeper, not faster — and rest at a lower lung volume.*

`MB9755`

---

## 194. Twin Pregnancy — Timing and Mode of Delivery

**PEARL:** **The presentation of the FIRST twin decides the mode of delivery. Vertex → attempt vaginal; breech or transverse → caesarean section.**

**UNDERSTAND:** A non-vertex first twin risks **interlocking of twins** (the after-coming head of twin 1 locking with the head of a vertex twin 2), **head entrapment above an incompletely dilated cervix**, and **cord prolapse**. None of these can be reliably managed once labour is under way, so the decision is made before delivery begins.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Timing by chorionicity: DCDA 38 weeks; MCDA 36–37+6 weeks; MCMA 32–34 weeks by caesarean** (risk of cord entanglement).
- **Second twin:** if breech → **assisted breech delivery**; if transverse → **internal podalic version under anaesthesia followed by breech extraction**.
- Complications of twins: **preterm labour (commonest), pre-eclampsia, anaemia, polyhydramnios, IUGR, malpresentation, PPH**, and in monochorionic pairs, **twin-to-twin transfusion syndrome, TAPS and twin reversed arterial perfusion**.
- **Chorionicity is best determined in the first trimester** — **lambda/twin-peak sign = dichorionic; T sign = monochorionic**.
- Superimposed **pre-eclampsia at 38 weeks in a DCDA twin pregnancy with a breech first twin** is an indication to **deliver now, by caesarean** — the pregnancy is already at term, so there is nothing to gain by waiting.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Read the **first twin's presentation** and the **gestation**; those two facts determine the answer regardless of the other detail in the stem.

**REMEMBER:** *Twin one decides the route.*

`MD0691`

---

## 195. Pelvic Inflammatory Disease

**PEARL:** **Chlamydia trachomatis is the commonest cause of PID**, ahead of gonococcus. PID is usually **polymicrobial**.

**UNDERSTAND:** Chlamydia leads because it is far more prevalent and, crucially, **often asymptomatic** — so it ascends untreated. Gonorrhoea causes more florid acute symptoms and is treated earlier, so it accounts for fewer silent tubal infections. That silence is exactly why chlamydia causes the majority of **tubal factor infertility**.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Organisms: sexually transmitted — **Chlamydia (commonest), gonococcus, Mycoplasma genitalium, Trichomonas**; pyogenic aerobes — staphylococci, streptococci, *E. coli*; anaerobes — **Bacteroides, Peptostreptococcus**; **Actinomyces — commonest in IUCD users**.
- **Genital tuberculosis is the only PID that does not spread sexually** — it is **haematogenous**, and is therefore the **commonest PID in virgins**. It typically presents with **infertility and hypomenorrhoea**, not pain.
- Diagnosis is clinical: **cervical motion, uterine or adnexal tenderness** in a woman with lower abdominal pain; treat empirically because of the cost of missing it.
- Sequelae: **tubal infertility, ectopic pregnancy, chronic pelvic pain, tubo-ovarian abscess**, and **Fitz-Hugh–Curtis syndrome** (perihepatitis, "violin-string" adhesions).
- Treatment: **ceftriaxone + doxycycline + metronidazole**; treat partners; remove an IUCD only if there is no response.
- **Pyometra** — pus in the uterus — is most often caused by **senile endometritis** among benign causes; among malignancies, **carcinoma cervix** is commonest, followed by endometrial carcinoma.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "Most common cause of PID" → **Chlamydia**. The genital-TB-in-virgins point is a separate favourite.

**REMEMBER:** *The silent infection causes the most damage.*

`MB3567, MC0271`

---

## 196. Non-Invasive Prenatal Testing

**PEARL:** **NIPT is a SCREENING test, not a diagnostic test.** A positive result must be confirmed by **CVS or amniocentesis**.

**UNDERSTAND:** NIPT measures **cell-free DNA in maternal plasma that derives from the PLACENTA (apoptotic trophoblast), not directly from the fetus**. Because of **confined placental mosaicism, a vanishing twin, and maternal mosaicism or malignancy**, the placental genotype can differ from the fetal one — so however high the detection rate, it can never be diagnostic.

**MUST KNOW:**
- Performed from **10 weeks** (enough fetal fraction), **after the routine first-trimester ultrasound** — dating, viability and NT must be established first, and multiple pregnancy or a demised twin affects interpretation.
- Screens for **trisomy 21, 18, 13 and sex chromosome aneuploidies (including Turner)**; **does not analyse the whole karyotype**, and cannot detect **structural anomalies, neural tube defects or most microdeletions** reliably.
- **Detection rate for T21 is > 99% with a very low false-positive rate** — far better than serum screening — but **positive predictive value depends on prior risk**, which is why low-risk women get more false positives.
- **Low fetal fraction** (obesity, early gestation, aneuploidy itself) causes test failure — a "no-call" result is itself associated with increased aneuploidy risk.
- Also determines **fetal RhD status and fetal sex** (the latter is **prohibited from being disclosed in India under the PCPNDT Act**).

**EXAM CONNECTION:** "False statement" → the one calling it **diagnostic**. Every other statement about NIPT in the options is true.

**REMEMBER:** *It reads the placenta, so it can only screen.*

`MC4557`

---

## 197. Tubal Sterilisation — The Eponyms

**PEARL:** **Fimbriectomy = Kroener's procedure.** **Pomeroy** is the commonest technique; **Uchida** is the most effective and best preserves reversibility; **Irving** is the most reliable but needs a laparotomy.

**UNDERSTAND:** The techniques differ in **how much tube they destroy and where the cut ends go** — which determines both **failure rate and reversibility**. Burying the proximal stump in myometrium (Irving) or broad ligament (Uchida) separates the ends permanently, driving failure toward zero; simply tying a loop (Pomeroy) is quick but leaves the ends adjacent, allowing recanalisation.

**MUST KNOW:**
- **Pomeroy:** a loop of the mid-tube is ligated with **plain catgut** (deliberately absorbable, so the ends separate) and excised. Commonest, done postpartum.
- **Modified Pomeroy (Parkland):** the two ends are ligated separately without forming a loop.
- **Uchida:** serosa stripped, muscular tube excised, **proximal stump buried in the broad ligament** — lowest failure rate, and preserves potential for tuboplasty.
- **Irving:** proximal end buried in the **myometrium**, distal end in the **broad ligament**.
- **Kroener (fimbriectomy):** ampulla ligated and the **infundibulum with fimbriae resected** — high failure rate and irreversible, since fimbrial pickup is destroyed.
- **Timing:** ideally **postpartum within 7 days** or **interval (day 5–10 of the cycle)**; **isthmic portion** is the preferred site. Failure ~**0.5%**, and a failure carries a **higher proportion of ectopic pregnancy**.
- **Vesicovaginal fistula repair aftercare:** continuous bladder drainage **14 days**, **sexual abstinence 3 months**, **avoid pregnancy for 1 year**, and **caesarean delivery** thereafter.

**EXAM CONNECTION:** Eponym matching. **Kroener = fimbriectomy** is the single most-asked pairing.

**REMEMBER:** *Kroener takes the fimbriae; Pomeroy takes a loop.*
